Not S-1.1's `execute`, which bounds captured output: a truncated diff hashes the same for two trees that differ past the limit, which is a stale hit rather than a visible failure. **The key is hashed as a structure, never as a joined string** — `("api.books", "list")` and `("api", "books.list")` join identically under any separator that can occur inside a field, and a dot occurs in every workload id the slug rule permits; the hit that results is another workload's measurement. `ExperimentKey.of` derives `workload_id` and `fixture_hash` from one `Workload` together, so the two fields describing the subject cannot describe different subjects. **The machine partitions the store rather than joining the key** (ADR 054): a result is mostly durations, and as a fifth key field the environment would be a value somebody could later compare loosely — *close enough, same OS* — with no defensible threshold for how similar two machines must be before one's timings may stand in for the other's; as a directory the question never arises and a foreign recording simply misses. **`run` returns a `Recall`, not the result**, because a bare `T` makes a recording indistinguishable from a fresh run at every call site downstream, and *no finding without a measurement* is only meaningful if *when* survives with it. Recordings are JSON and not pickles, since S-5.2's whole method is opening one that produced a surprising answer. An unreadable entry is a **miss** — recomputing is always correct — but counted apart from one, or a cache that has silently stopped working forever would look exactly like a cold one; asking for the wrong result type **raises**, since as a miss two callers sharing a key would each recompute and overwrite the other's recording and both would appear to work. AC 4 is asserted by removing the mechanisms rather than counting calls: the recorded screen of all six planted workloads replays with `socket.socket`, `socket.create_connection`, `subprocess.run` and `subprocess.Popen` all poisoned, and a second test checks it from the subject's side — every bound workload comes back uninvoked, because a replay that quietly re-seeded would still be the ninety minutes this story removes. AC 3 is measured against a sixty-metric, six-point recording rather than a toy, since the cost of a hit is parsing and validating the value. **Sabotage-verified on eighteen properties**, all caught; two were rewritten first. One tested nothing (it removed an untracked file's *name* from the digest while its contents were still hashed) and one was dropped as **unbreakable**: naming the staged write like an entry changes nothing, because lookup is by exact digest filename, so no file other than `<digest>.json` can ever be read whatever the stage is called — the atomic-rename property is already carried by the sabotage that writes in place. A nineteenth survives and is over-determined in the same way: reading `self._environment` instead of the recording's cannot differ, since the partition guarantees every entry a cache can read was written by a cache with its environment. **The date test needed a bracket on both sides** — a lower bound alone passes for a `recall` that stamps the moment of the replay, which is the wrong date and the flattering one.

### S-5.2 — Replay mode
Depends: S-5.1
AC:
- A recorded investigation can be replayed to debug downstream agents
- Replay is byte-identical to the original for deterministic experiments
- Non-deterministic experiments are marked and re-run rather than replayed

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/replay/cache.py`, ADR 055, 25 tests in `tests/replay/test_mode.py` (65 across the package). All three AC met. **AC 3's obvious reading deletes S-5.1.** Every measurement carries `seconds`, `cpu_seconds` and `blocked_seconds`, and no duration repeats — so if *deterministic* meant the artifact reproduces byte for byte, nothing in this system would be deterministic and the cache built in the previous story could never legitimately hit. `Determinism` is therefore a claim about the **answer**: a screening sweep concludes from counts, which reproduce to the integer and which ADR 052 already requires to be what raises a flag; a timing comparison, a load curve or a fuzzing campaign concludes from a distribution and answers differently on a second run. **It is declared and nothing here can check it**, so it sits at the call site nearest the primitive, exactly where `experiment_spec` sits. **The default is `SAMPLED`** — an experiment nobody classified is one nobody has thought about, and the other default spends the first unconsidered call site on a silently stale answer — and the default has teeth: adopting it broke four of S-5.1's own tests. **A replay while recording needs the claim from both sides and the stricter wins**: a caller who has decided their experiment is deterministic must not thereby promote somebody else's sample, and one who has said they need a fresh sample must not be overruled by a recording taken as deterministic. **`RECORD` and `REPLAY` treat a sample oppositely, and that is not an inconsistency**: a fresh investigation must never take one afternoon's sample as a standing fact, while a debugging session must never let the session being debugged stop being the session that was recorded — so recording re-runs it and replay plays it back, and the `Recall` says which, with the *a fresh run would answer differently* sentence present on a replayed sample and deliberately **absent** on a deterministic one (Epic 4: a caveat attached to everything is one nobody reads). **Three modes on one object because the alternative is a branch in every caller** — `run` takes the same shape in all three, which is what makes replay a way of debugging the agent that calls it rather than a second route through it. `OFF` earns its place from S-15.1, whose AC is *N runs with the cache disabled*: agreement between ten replays of one recording is 100%, and without a mode that study writes `if use_cache:` around every experiment, which is Epic 4's branch-in-no-module again. Two refusals are structural rather than conventional: **`replay()` has no `compute` parameter** (a caller able to pass one still had to build the checkout, container and database a debugging session does not have), and **only a recording cache writes** — `record` refuses in the other two, because a replay that wrote back what it played back would restamp the session it was reading and destroy the record of when the run happened. **A missing recording is refused and the refusal lists the store**, since the four things that could be wrong — different tree, different fixture, spec spelled differently, experiment never run — look identical from the call site. **No artifact here describes an investigation**: that is S-8.4's append-only log, and inventing a second would guess at a schema it already specifies (S-1.7's recorded argument for leaving `Certification` unlogged); `recordings()` answers the question replay actually raises, which is *what is on this disk*. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties, all caught** — but only after the sixteenth was added: *replay mode records over what it played back* survived the first pass, because the property held merely as a consequence of one early return, and it is now a refusal on `record`. **S-5.1's AC-4 test was passing for the wrong reason and this story found it**: the planted fixture needs neither a socket nor a subprocess, so poisoning both left a *live* second screen able to pass it — poisoning the world proves nothing reached for it, and only the hit count proves nothing needed to. That assertion is now there.

### S-5.3 — Token accounting
Depends: S-0.2
AC:
- Every model call records: phase, agent, step type, model, input tokens, output tokens, cached tokens, cost
- Cost is queryable per phase, per finding, and per run
- A run report includes euros per confirmed finding

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/accounting.py`, ADR 056, 36 tests in `tests/cost/test_accounting.py`. All three AC met. **AC 1's field list, read literally, cannot produce a cost.** The API reports two cache figures, not one, and they bill in **opposite directions**: a cache *write* costs 1.25× the input rate at the five-minute TTL and 2× at the hour — **more than not caching at all** — while a *read* costs 0.1×. A million write tokens and a million read tokens are both "a million cached tokens" and differ in price by 12.5×, so no arithmetic recovers a bill from the collapsed figure, and **the error is signed the flattering way** because caching is understood as a discount: one `cached_tokens` field priced at the read rate under-bills every cold prefix, which is the first call of every investigation. There is therefore **no `cached_tokens` field anywhere in the module**. The same trap sits one field earlier — **`input_tokens` is the uncached remainder, not the prompt** (the prompt is the sum of all three), so a reader who adds it to a cached-tokens figure double-counts and one who does not under-reports; `prompt_tokens` spells out the sum. The TTL is recorded **per call** rather than assumed, since 1.25× against 2× is a 60% difference on every cached prefix with nothing in the totals looking wrong. **`ModelCall` has no cost field to set** — `CLAUDE.md` forbids an agent reporting a measurement and a cost is one, so this is S-4.1's `work_verified` construction applied to the number with the strongest incentive to be wrong. **An unknown model is refused rather than defaulted**: fast mode on `claude-opus-5` bills at $10/$50 against $5/$25 — the same model id at twice the rate — so the book is keyed on the *billing* identity, and the first unlisted model this system meets will be a newer, dearer one, so a default under-bills. The book carries the date it was read; **ADR-002's indicative table was checked against current published rates and is correct as written** — no correction needed. Money is `Decimal`, because §12's figures are quoted to the cent and a project is ~1,000 runs of ~250 calls. **Euros carry their rate and its date** (a hardcoded rate is correct the day it is written and silently wrong after), and **`eur_per_confirmed_finding` is `None` when nothing was confirmed** — not zero, not the total, not infinity: S-4.5 ships *nothing found* as a successful answer, so the cost is real and the ratio undefined, and the rendered report says so in words because a missing figure must not read as a free run. **Not every call belongs to a finding**: §11 makes grounding once-per-repository, so `finding_id` is optional, the remainder is reported separately, and `reconciles` asserts the per-finding table plus the remainder equals the run total — trivially true today and the only thing that will notice the first time a call is attributed twice. `Phase` has **no screening member**, since Epic 4 is zero model calls and a slot for it could only ever be filled by a bug. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-three properties, all caught**, including pricing a cache write as a discount — the exact defect a single `cached_tokens` field would have shipped.

### S-5.4 — Budget enforcement
Depends: S-5.3
AC:
- Per-phase step caps: ground 60, investigate 40 experiments, repair 3 attempts, audit 2 rounds
- Global euro ceiling
- Exhaustion halts, checkpoints, and reports — it does not warn and continue
- A progress check escalates when N steps produce no new information

Notes: caps must be in code, not configuration. The worst case without them is unbounded.

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/budget.py`, ADR 057, 32 tests in `tests/cost/test_enforcement.py`. All four AC met. **AC 3 describes the global ceiling, not every cap, and read as universal it makes three of the four wrong.** `02-architecture.md` §7.2 gives each phase its own disposition and only one is a halt: ground *aborts with a diagnostic*, investigate **emits the partial chain with its exclusions**, repair and audit *escalate*, and only the euro ceiling *halts, checkpoints and reports*. The investigate row is the one that matters — forty experiments that established something and ran out is **an answer**, the same answer S-4.5 ships when a screen finds nothing, and halting would discard the most expensive thing in the run. So exhaustion carries a `Disposition` and `dispositions()` makes the four enumerable, because a handler written against a single outcome is silently wrong for three of six phases in the direction of throwing away a real result. AC 3's other clauses hold everywhere: exhaustion **raises** rather than returning a flag, carrying the counters, the disposition and the spend — S-1.7's `NoiseFloorTooHighError` argument, and also AC 3's checkpoint, deliberately **not** a checkpoint schema (S-6.1's artifact, not guessed at). **Four caps, four units, and calls are none of them**: §12.1 budgets 120 model calls per finding against a cap of 40 *experiments*, so an experiment is ~3 calls and a cap counted in calls would stop investigation at a third of its budget — S-4.4's finding recurring. **Two scopes, wrong in both directions and plausibly**: a run-wide investigate counter gives five findings eight experiments each, and a per-finding ground counter re-grants 60 steps per finding — a cap that *rises* with the number of findings. Each audit gets its **own** two rounds; a shared pool would let a patch audit spend rounds a finding audit had not reached. **Caps are compiled in and may only be lowered** — both the constructor and `tighten()` refuse a raise, so the backlog note holds for a running process and not just the source file; lowering stays legal because spending less is not the failure mode and forbidding it would make the caps unusable on a smoke test. **A ceiling checked after the spend is a report, not a ceiling**: `authorize` refuses on a worst case that prices every prompt token as a one-hour cache write (2×, ADR 056's dearest input) and assumes all of `max_tokens` returns. **Progress is measured from the conclusion, not the agent's opinion** — *did that step teach me something* is F6's self-judged criterion, so steps carry a harness-computed digest of the conclusion, and it is over the conclusion rather than the measurement for S-5.2's reason (no duration repeats, so a digest over raw numbers never detects a stall). A stall and an exhaustion are **different exception types**, since exhausted means stop and stalled means change approach while budget remains. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-four properties, all caught — after one test was strengthened**: re-scoping the patch audit to the run survived, because the test compared two phases on one finding and nothing had been recorded against the phase under test; the property needs two findings competing for one counter. Fourth time a passing sabotage meant a weak test rather than weak code. **The test-file name collision was hit a third time** — `tests/cost/test_budget.py` collides with `tests/screening/test_budget.py` under mypy; renamed `test_enforcement.py`.

### S-5.5 — Model routing
Depends: S-5.3
Why: ~30 calls per run genuinely need the frontier model; ~220 do not.
AC:
- Each call site declares `creative` or `mechanical`
- Routing maps step class to model tier
- Tier assignment is configurable without code changes
- A test asserts mechanical steps never hit the frontier tier by default

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/routing.py`, ADR 058, 36 tests in `tests/cost/test_routing.py`. All four AC met. **`creative` is a property of the step type, not a declaration.** `04-cost.md` §3 is a table of eight step types and the mechanical check that would catch a wrong cheap answer against each; six have one, hypothesis generation and attack design record *none exists*. So the class is derived, and `STEP_KINDS` keeps the **name** of each check rather than a boolean — a boolean nobody can audit is a routing decision made on a fiction, and S-5.6 needs to know *what* validates a step before it may retry one. AC 1's declaration is still taken and then **checked**: believed, a mislabelled step is the whole non-negotiable defeated through the front door, since relabelling hypothesis generation as mechanical sends it to the cheap model with every other guard satisfied. F6's finding again. **AC 3 sits directly against S-5.4's *caps in code, not configuration*, and both are right** — the two stories are asymmetric in mirror image because the harm runs opposite ways: caps permit lowering and refuse raising (unbounded spend is the harm); routing permits **dearer** and refuses creative below the frontier (an unvalidatable wrong answer is the harm, and one bad hypothesis wastes an investigation branch worth far more than the upgrade it saved). That is why AC 4 says mechanical avoids the frontier *by default* rather than always — the dearer override is permitted and tested. The creative rule is enforced on the per-phase route as well as the class-level one, because a rule enforced only on the general key gets walked around on the specific one. **A tier is what it costs, not what it is called** — the hole every other rule leaves open, since creative work always routes to the tier *named* frontier and the models behind tiers are configurable, so putting the cheapest model there satisfies every check while defeating all of them; tiers are resolved against S-5.3's price book and must be **ordered by price** (inversion refused, equality allowed — two tiers on one model is a legitimate deployment), and an unpriceable model is refused outright because it would produce a run whose cost is unknown. **The route is keyed on phase as well as class**: §12.3 runs grounding's mechanical calls cheap with a mature playbook and the investigate loop's mid-tier — two mechanical steps, two tiers — and a router that could not express that could not implement the €2,150 figure. `model_for` has **no default step class**, so a call site cannot decline to declare. ADR-002 is not contradicted: it states the *unrouted* default and explicitly releases cheaper tiers to E5's routing wherever a validator exists, and a patch has one. `frontier_share` puts the story's *why* in the run report — ~30 of ~250 calls — because a share drifting upward is the routing quietly ceasing to work. **This is the third Epic 5 story whose AC summarize a table elsewhere and lose what it specifies** (S-5.3's two cache figures, S-5.4's four dispositions, S-5.5's per-phase routing and validator column): in this epic the backlog is the summary and `04-cost.md` is the specification. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-one properties, all caught.**

### S-5.6 — Cascade with escalation logging
Depends: S-5.5
AC:
- Cheap model attempted first wherever a deterministic validator exists
- Escalates after two failures
- Escalation rate logged per step type
- **No cascading on hypothesis generation or attack design** — no validator exists for those

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/cascade.py`, ADR 059, 34 tests in `tests/cost/test_cascade.py`. All four AC met. **AC 4 is not a separate rule, it is a consequence of AC 1.** Cascade is gated on `mechanical_check is not None`, so `04-cost.md` §3's two *none exists* rows — hypothesis generation and attack design — are refused by the same condition that admits the other six. No branch names them, and a new step type with no check would be refused without anybody editing this module. Written as its own rule it would have been one somebody could forget to apply, which for `CLAUDE.md`'s standing non-negotiable is the wrong shape. **A caller may not supply the missing validator**: `cascade` refuses a creative step type even when handed a `validate` callable, because §3's table *is* the statement that no deterministic check exists and a caller-supplied one is a judgement wearing a validator's clothes — accepting it would route the one step nothing can verify onto a cheap model with every other guard satisfied. **The cascade starts where S-5.5 routes and escalates one rung**, which reconciles two things that look like they conflict: §12.3 lists repair as *cascade mid→frontier* while S-5.5's AC 4 says mechanical never hits frontier by default. Both hold once **routing** and **escalation** are separated — repair is *routed* mid and only *reaches* frontier by failing its own check twice. The rule is uniform and reproduces §12.3 at both ends: repair mid→frontier, grounding cheap→mid. An invariant follows: since S-5.5 never routes mechanical work to the top tier, every cascadable step always has somewhere to escalate to. **A rejected result is never returned** — `NoDearerTierError` rather than handing back the last attempt, because a cascade that returned an answer its own validator refused would make the validator decorative and the caller cannot tell the two apart by looking. **§3 specifies a fifth thing the AC omit**: *if a step escalates more than ~30% of the time, promote it permanently* — above that, two cheap attempts plus a dear one cost more than starting dear. A rate below ten samples is **`None`, not a number** (one escalation out of one is 100%, and promoting on that is a coin flip — S-4.2's rule again), and the log is **two-sided**: a step type that has never escalated across enough attempts is either the result the technique exists for or a validator that cannot fail, and the log reports the number rather than choosing. **S-5.5's decision to keep the validator's *name* pays off here** — the refusals, `cascadable()` and the promotion report all quote the check, which is what lets a reader verify a routing decision against §3 rather than trust it. **Fourth Epic 5 story whose AC summarize a table and lose part of it** (S-5.3's second cache figure, S-5.4's four dispositions, S-5.5's per-phase routing, S-5.6's promotion threshold). **Sabotage-verified on twenty-one properties, all caught.**

### S-5.7 — Cache-friendly context assembly
Depends: S-5.3
Why: the single largest cost variable in the system.
AC:
- Prompt structure is: stable system prefix, stable playbook, stable source, **append-only log**, varying question
- The experiment log is never reordered or re-summarized mid-investigation
- A test asserts the prefix is byte-identical between consecutive calls in one investigation
- Cache hit rate is measured and reported

Notes: 120 calls at 60k uncached costs ~$39; the same calls at 12k with caching cost ~$1.68. Any reordering of the log invalidates the cache and silently multiplies cost.

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/context.py`, ADR 060, 39 tests in `tests/cost/test_context.py`. All four AC met. **Every way of losing the cache is silent** — the request succeeds, `cache_read_input_tokens` is zero, the bill goes up — so this story is about failures that read as success. **The four cacheable boundaries fit the four `cache_control` breakpoints exactly**, with the varying question after the last of them and never given one (caching it writes an entry no later call can read and spends one of only four doing it). **AC 2 is an absence, not a discipline**: there is no method that reorders the log, none that re-summarizes it, and none that changes a stable segment — the system prompt, playbook and source are captured at construction, so a `datetime.now()` in them is evaluated once, which is what makes the prefix byte-identical rather than merely intended to be. **The minimum cacheable prefix is not monotonic and the cheap tier is the worst**: opus-5 512, sonnet-5 1024, **haiku-4-5 4096** — and a prefix below the minimum caches *nothing*, with no error. S-5.5 routes grounding to haiku *because it is cheap*, and §12.3's engineered grounding is ten calls with a mature playbook — a short prompt — so **routing a step down a tier can raise its effective cost**, the exact opposite of the routing's purpose. `minimum_prefix` refuses an unrecorded model rather than defaulting, and the below-minimum message names the routing interaction, because a reader just told to use a cheaper model will not otherwise connect the two. **The log is one block because a breakpoint looks back at most 20**: rendered per experiment it crosses that at experiment 21, and S-5.4 caps investigation at 40 — the obvious implementation stops caching exactly halfway to its own budget. One block also makes append-only checkable (call N's log is a byte prefix of call N+1's). **Token counts are measured, never estimated** — `viability()` answers `UNKNOWN` without a `count_tokens` figure, since `tiktoken` is OpenAI's tokenizer and undercounts Claude 15-20% on prose and more on code, and here the estimate would be *of whether a cost control works* (S-4.5's rule). **The first call can never hit** and pays the 1.25× write premium, so `warm_hit_rate()` excludes it — a blended figure alone makes a working cache look worse the shorter the run — and the report names tokens *written* too, since a write bills above the uncached rate (ADR 056). **Sabotage found three real gaps out of twenty-three, the highest proportion in Epic 5**: the breakpoint and lookback guards sat inside `render`, which builds a fixed five-block tuple, so neither could ever fire (S-3.12's finding again — a guard no test reaches is a guard nobody has checked); they are now a `check_blocks` function a test exercises directly. And `stable_prefix()` had only been asserted against *itself*, so dropping the source segment changed no test — a prefix missing a segment would report *byte-identical* while the omitted part moved freely. A fourth survivor was redundant code and was removed rather than tested. All twenty-three caught now.

### S-5.8 — Context pruning with on-demand detail
Depends: S-5.7, S-5.1
AC:
- The log in context holds one-line summaries per experiment
- `read_experiment(n)` returns full output, stacks, and raw counters
- The prompt states explicitly that detail is retrievable
- No information is discarded — only deferred

**DONE (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/pruning.py`, ADR 061, 38 tests in `tests/cost/test_pruning.py`. All four AC met. **The detail is held, not referenced, and pruning is what `render` omits** — there is no `truncate`, `forget`, `evict` or `compact`, so AC 4 is an absence rather than a promise (S-5.7's construction again). Held here rather than pointed at, because *nothing discarded* must not depend on another store still having it. **Retrieved detail never re-enters the log**: S-5.7 made the log append-only so the cached prefix stays byte-identical, and writing experiment 3's stacks back into the middle of it at call fifty would invalidate every breakpoint after — **turning the 23× win into a loss on the same call that was trying to save tokens** — so `read_experiment` returns text and mutates nothing. **The summary is composed, not authored** (F6, fourth time this epic): an agent asked to summarize its own experiment can write *nothing of interest* and the detail is then never retrieved by anyone — information preserved and lost at once — so the header comes from the primitive and target and only the outcome is supplied. Indexes are assigned by the log, since `read_experiment(7)` must mean the seventh experiment and a caller-supplied index can collide, skip or restart; an out-of-range index is **refused by name rather than clamped**, because the caller is a model and a model that guesses must be told, not handed the nearest record. **§5's 60-80% is measured, not repeated** — `reduction()` against an unpruned log and `net_reduction()` after retrieval — and **retrieving every experiment exactly once cancels the saving precisely** (zero tokens saved, plus round trips the metric does not count), with any re-read taking it negative; not clamped, because a clamp would hide the case the metric exists to expose. **AC 1 says one line and §5 shows two**: neither binds — the token budget does, so each *part* is single-line and bounded, giving §5's exact shape at a predictable size across forty experiments. Fifth Epic 5 story where the AC and the cost document differ and the document is the specification. **Two defects were found by tests failing rather than by sabotage**: the emptiness check was written against total characters, but the retrieval notice always renders, so an empty log reported a confident 0% saving instead of *no experiments yet*; and *retrieving everything* cancels the saving exactly rather than going negative — a sharper statement than the one first written down. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-two properties, all caught**, one after rewriting a clamp inserted *after* the range check, which was dead code rather than a sabotage.

### S-5.9 — Vendor cost comparison on effective cost
Depends: S-5.3, S-15.3
Why: ADR-002 chose a vendor on SDK ergonomics, not on price, and the question will otherwise be re-argued from list prices and memory.
AC:
- Runs the same fixed scenario set against at least two vendors
- Reports **cost per confirmed finding**, not cost per token
- Reports effective input cost with caching in effect, alongside list price, and states the measured cache hit rate for each vendor
- Reports experiments-to-conclusion per vendor — a dearer model reaching the answer in fewer experiments may be cheaper overall
- Records each vendor's minimum cacheable prefix and cache TTL, since both change effective cost independently of list price
- Result is published in the cost report and supersedes ADR-002's provider choice if the numbers warrant it

Notes: **list price is the wrong number for this workload.** Cache reads bill at roughly 0.1x of input on the first-party API, and the append-only experiment log exists precisely so the cached prefix stays byte-identical — so effective cost is dominated by hit rate, not sticker rate. Caches are also model-scoped, so a vendor that is 30% cheaper per token but has a larger minimum cacheable prefix, a shorter TTL, or weaker prefix semantics can cost more here. S-0.8's harness is already the right shape for this: a fixed scenario set with programmatic scoring, so swapping the request layer is the only work. ADR-002 is a record of a decision, not a commitment — this story exists to make it falsifiable rather than defended.

**PARTIAL (2026-08-09)** — `src/coldfix/cost/vendors.py`, ADR 062, 28 tests in `tests/cost/test_vendors.py`. **AC 3 and AC 5 are met; AC 1, 2, 4 and 6 are blocked**, and were put to Allen before any code was written (`CLAUDE.md`: ask rather than improvise when a dependency is missing). **AC 1** needs a second vendor's account and real spend — confirmed with Allen that **there is no second vendor**, so this is blocked indefinitely rather than pending; ADR-002's deferred different-vendor requirement for the Adversary is blocked for the same reason. **AC 2** needs E9's finding audit to confirm anything, and S-0.8's scenarios produce no findings at all. **AC 4 is not merely unmeasured — S-0.8 measured it and found it unmeasurable**: in 60 runs the model chose *no finding, stop* **zero times**, reasoning correctly, withholding the verdict, and always proposing one more experiment; until something bounds that the figure has no value, and S-5.4's budget halt is what bounds the damage. **AC 6** needs the numbers. What is built is the half that makes ADR-002 falsifiable rather than defended: `effective_input_usd_per_mtok` shows an input token costs ~a quarter of list at §12.2's engineered shape (120 calls, 12k prompt, 85% cached), with the write premium **amortised across the calls that read it** so a one-call run pays it in full; and the AC 5 facts — minimum cacheable prefix (per model, from S-5.7), cache TTLs and their write premiums, the read multiplier, prefix-match semantics and cache scope — are recorded with a **date and a source**, because a profile with no provenance is memory with a dataclass around it. **One profile is recorded and the second is deliberately absent**: `recorded_profile` refuses an unknown vendor, and a test asserts `VENDORS` holds exactly one entry so adding a second is a deliberate act with a failing test behind it. The Notes' claim — *30% cheaper per token but a larger minimum cacheable prefix can cost more* — is demonstrated against a **hypothetical** profile that lives only in the test file and is named hypothetical, with a control asserting it wins once the prompt clears its minimum (without which the demonstration would pass for a module that simply always preferred Anthropic). **Absent measurements report their absence and name their blocker** — *needs E9's finding audit*, *not measurable: the model never concludes* — because a reader who does not know why a column is empty will fill it with a guess; `cheapest()` returns `None` below two vendors and the report says *this is a cost model rather than a comparison*. The Anthropic profile is **derived** from S-5.3's price book and S-5.7's prefix table rather than copied, since a second copy is a second thing to go stale. **ADR-002 stands, untested** — nothing here supersedes it, and the report says so. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-four properties, all caught**, one after a real finding: `price_book_agrees` took no argument, so it could only ever return `True` and a version that always did was indistinguishable — **fourth time this project has recorded S-3.12's finding** that a guard nothing can make fail is a guard nobody has checked.

**EPIC 5 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-10)** — `src/coldfix/cost/session.py`, ADR 063, 26 tests in `tests/cost/test_cost_composed.py`. The epic finished with nine stories, 294 passing tests and **no way to spend money through it**: performing its own sentence — *make development fast and production affordable* — took seven objects in the right order, and **no test file in the epic touched more than three of the nine modules**, always a module plus its direct dependency. **Four defects, and the first two are invisible because both wrong joins keep the cache working** — caching is a prefix match, so a log that is wrong in *content* but still append-only reports full hits and a rising bill, with no failing request to notice. (1) *Two append-only logs with no correct join.* S-5.7's `Investigation` appends a line; S-5.8's `PrunedLog` renders a block. Appending each summary drops the retrieval notice, so the agent is never told `read_experiment(n)` exists and never asks — **detail preserved and lost at once, the exact failure S-5.8 was written to prevent**; appending the rendered block after each experiment re-appends every earlier experiment with it, so at S-5.4's cap of 40 the log is carried **40 times** on the prompt whose whole purpose is to be small. A log now has one owner (`log_source`) and the other object refuses `append` **and** `entries` — `entries` refuses rather than returning an empty tuple, because an empty tuple lets `is_append_only` compare two empty lists and report the property holding over a log it never saw (S-3.12's finding, fifth time). The one test that joined the two modules appended `render()` **once, after all 40 experiments** — a static snapshot, never the incremental case a real investigation has. (2) *Caches are model-scoped and nothing respected it.* S-5.9 records the fact in `CachePolicy.scope`; `Investigation` binds one model at construction, the router picks one per step and the cascade escalates to a third mid-step, so the obvious composition sends one prompt to three models and calls the result a cache. `Session` holds **one prompt per model** and never blends hit rates — a blended figure averages over caches that share no entry and credits the second model with the first one's warm calls. (3) *A cascade spends three times what was authorized, and worse than three times*: `cascade` makes up to three calls against a ceiling built to price one, and the third runs a **tier dearer**, so even multiplying by the attempt count under-prices it — authorization moved inside the `attempt` callable S-5.6 already took and nothing had used. (4) *`frontier_share` cannot see an escalation*: it maps (phase, class) through the router, and escalation is not a routing decision, so the one path S-5.6 guarantees exists is the one path the metric is blind to — and escalation is exactly how frontier use drifts upward, which is what the figure exists to catch. **S-5.7's routing hazard is now priced rather than described**, giving S-5.9 its first caller: at §12.3's engineered grounding (2k prompt, 85% cached) `claude-haiku-4-5` caches nothing against its 4096 minimum and bills $1.0000/MTok while `claude-sonnet-5` clears its 1024 and bills $0.7316 — **the cheap tier is 37% dearer** — with a control at 5k where haiku caches and wins by 3x. **Sabotage-verified on fourteen properties, all caught** — and **the first sabotage run was itself invalid**: the runner restored with `git checkout -- <paths>`, and a pathspec naming an untracked file makes git error and restore *nothing*, so the new module kept every sabotage and later results were confounded by earlier ones. A sabotage harness is code, and code unchecked against its own failure mode is what these checks exist to find. **Two limits recorded rather than closed**: `ModelCall` carries the step *class* while `EscalationLog` is keyed on step *type*, so §3's promotion rule (*above ~30%, starting dear is cheaper*) is still decided on rate alone though the ledger now holds every number the comparison needs; and **nothing in Epic 5 has met a real API** — the arithmetic is right and the guards fire, which is not the same as working in the system.

---

# EPIC 6 — State and persistence

**Goal:** state that survives crashes, and knowledge that survives rewinds.

### S-6.1 — Checkpointed state schema
Depends: S-0.2
AC:
- TypedDict with reducers: `experiments`, `attempts`, `flags` use append semantics
- A test proves a node returning a single experiment appends rather than replaces
- Schema validated on every node transition

Notes: `Annotated[list, add]` is load-bearing. Without it the agent loses its own history, re-tests rejected hypotheses, and loops while appearing to work. This is the most common bug in building these systems.

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/state/checkpoint.py`, ADR 064, 34 tests in `tests/state/` (`test_checkpoint_state.py` and `test_state_in_langgraph.py`). All three AC met. **No ADR had ever chosen the orchestrator**: every document assumes LangGraph (`02` §8, `03` §1.1, S-12.2's *SQLite checkpointer*) but it was never a recorded decision and never a dependency, and the backlog's decisions table lists orchestration nowhere — ADR 064 is that record. **AC 2 is only meaningful against the real runtime**, since *a node returning a single experiment appends rather than replaces* is a claim about the framework's merge step; against a reducer we call ourselves it passes whether or not the schema is annotated, which is the one thing worth knowing. So a spike compiled real graphs before anything was designed, and **one of its findings contradicted the design premise**: a Pydantic state schema does **not** give per-transition validation — LangGraph validates the graph's *input* and nothing after it. Two more decided the module: **a node returning an unknown key is silently ignored** (no error, no write, and `extra="forbid"` does not catch it), and **a custom reducer runs on every write and may raise**. **The schema is a Pydantic model, superseding §1.1's literal `TypedDict`** — `CLAUDE.md` requires a Pydantic model for every artifact crossing a node boundary and this is the artifact that crosses *every* one; a `TypedDict` is also `dict[str, Any]` under strict mypy and cannot be validated at construction. The reducers survive the change untouched, because `Annotated[list[X], AppendOnly(...)]` is `typing` plus a callable. **`langgraph` is a dev dependency and nothing under `src/` imports it**, so the schema stays framework-independent while being read correctly by the framework; it becomes a project dependency at S-12.1. **The reducer is checked rather than `operator.add`**: `add` appends but accepts the mirror-image bug in silence — a node returning the whole channel instead of its delta **doubles** history rather than losing it, which S-5.7's cached prefix and S-5.8's `read_experiment(7)` both depend on not happening; a test asserts `add` accepts exactly what `AppendOnly` refuses. **AC 3 is a wrapper because the framework leaves the hole**, and the unknown-key case is the one that matters: `{"experiements": [...]}` writes nothing and reports nothing, leaving an investigation that ran to its cap having recorded no experiments — the note's *loops while appearing to work*, reached through a typo. **The trust ledger is deliberately absent** (F5): keeping it here *is* the defect, since a rewind would restore the trust level that preceded the failure that caused the rewind. Every field is JSON-representable because ADR 003 stores checkpoints in SQLite or Postgres, and the artifacts these channels carry belong to epics that do not exist yet — the guess S-5.4 declined to make when it left this schema to this story. **Two LangGraph typing traps are recorded for S-12.1**: `add_node` infers `NodeInputT` from `input_schema=`, and without it that variable solves to `Never` so every *correctly annotated* node fails to type-check while an unannotated lambda passes; and `_Node` is a `Protocol` whose `__call__(self, state: ...)` takes a **named** parameter, so a plain `Callable[[State], ...]` can never satisfy it — `node` is therefore signature-preserving. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught**, including dropping the annotation from each channel independently, and the baseline was re-run green *after* the pass — the check the Epic 5 composition run skipped.

### S-6.2 — Persistent store
Depends: S-6.1
Why: rewind must not discard the knowledge that motivated it.
AC:
- Separate database holding: failure memory, playbooks, trust ledger, replay cache
- Append-only; no checkpoint restore touches it
- A test rewinds to an earlier checkpoint and asserts failure memory from the later state is still present

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/state/persistent.py`, ADR 065, 19 tests in `tests/state/test_persistent_store.py` (12 of them `postgres`-marked, run against a real container). All three AC met. **Append-only is enforced by a database trigger, not by this module's method list**: a Python class with no `update` method is append-only until somebody opens a connection, and the point of this store is that it survives operations performed *on* the system rather than through it — so the tests attempt `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` through `psycopg` rather than through the module (`CLAUDE.md`'s rule for a safety property). **`TRUNCATE` is guarded separately and deliberately**: it is the exact verb ADR 003 names when it says dropping checkpoints must not reach the playbook, and Postgres will not accept it on a `FOR EACH ROW` trigger — so the obvious single trigger leaves it open **while looking complete**, and it gets its own `FOR EACH STATEMENT` trigger and its own test. **One journal, not four schemas**: the four things F5 lists are *what must survive*, not four shapes this story knows, so entries are `(collection, key, jsonb)` and Epic 13 decides what one means — the third time this project has declined the same guess (S-5.4 left the checkpoint schema to S-6.1, S-6.1 left the log entry to S-8.4, S-6.2 leaves the playbook to S-13.1). **AC 1 lists the replay cache and S-5.1 already built it on a filesystem**; moving it into Postgres would trade away both of ADR 054's properties — a recording openable by hand when it produced a surprising answer (S-5.2's whole debugging method) and a foreign machine's recording missing rather than matching — for tidiness. What F5 requires is that a checkpoint restore cannot reach it, which a directory outside the checkpoint database satisfies at least as completely as a row would; **`MEMBERS` records all four with where each lives**, and a test asserts the replay cache is filesystem-backed and is *not* a journal collection, because a list quietly holding three is the kind of partial truth this project keeps finding in its own summaries. **The store takes a `VerifiedDatabase`** — S-2.5's construction reused, and its default name patterns already include `coldfix_*`, which is evidence the guard was built expecting to cover our own store. **`refuse_shared_store` enforces ADR 003's separation**, which had been recorded and never enforced: a `Path` is the development SQLite checkpointer and cannot collide, and a URL naming the same database on the same server is refused. **AC 3 is proved against a real rewind rather than a simulated one** — S-6.1 made `langgraph` available, so the test compiles a graph with a checkpointer, learns something in a late node, rewinds via `get_state_history`, and asserts the checkpointed state went back while the failure memory did not; a second test takes the stronger form and **resumes** from the rewound checkpoint, asserting the resumed run reads what was learned *after* the checkpoint it resumed from — which is the property that actually stops it repeating the rejected attempt. **Sabotage-verified on twelve properties, all caught**, including removing the `TRUNCATE` trigger independently of the row-level one; baseline re-run green after the pass.

### S-6.3 — Store experiment results by reference
Depends: S-6.1, S-5.1
AC:
- Checkpointed state holds hashes and summaries, not full results
- Full results live in the replay cache
- Checkpoint size stays bounded as the experiment log grows
- A 40-experiment investigation produces checkpoints under a stated size limit

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/state/reference.py`, ADR 066, 23 tests in `tests/state/test_results_by_reference.py`. All four AC met. **The stated limit rests on arithmetic rather than a sample**: every `ExperimentRef` is size-checked at construction against `MAX_REFERENCE_BYTES` (1 KiB), so S-5.4's cap of 40 experiments gives a log that **cannot** exceed 40 KiB whatever the measurements were, and the 64 KiB state limit has that behind it with 24 KiB left for artifacts that do not grow with the investigation. Without the per-entry check the bound is a hope — a spec carrying a large parameter block grows the checkpoint with nothing noticing, and the checkpoint is written after *every node*. **The reference carries the key, not only the digest**: F13 says *keyed by hash* and the digest is the filename, but S-5.1's lookup takes a key and derives the digest, so a digest alone finds the file only by scanning the store; carrying the key makes a fetch one read and makes the reference self-describing — and a key is identity, not a result, so the state still holds no measurements (asserted). **The outcome bound is S-5.8's, shared rather than copied** — F13's closing note about `04-cost.md` §5 is a design instruction, and importing `MAX_SUMMARY_CHARS` is what stops the prompt log and the checkpoint log drifting apart; the summary is composed from primitive and target with only the outcome supplied (S-5.8's construction, F6's reason). **Size is measured as JSON, and the reasoning behind that was wrong until a test corrected it**: the assumption was that ADR 003's Postgres checkpointer stores JSON so the JSON encoding *is* what gets written — LangGraph's serializer is **msgpack and smaller**, 13,404 bytes against 15,761 for a full forty-experiment log (~85%). That is the safe direction, since a state fitting this limit fits what is actually written; a proxy that under-estimated would pass here and be breached on disk. The ratio is pinned by a test rather than reasoned about. **The sharpest property is that checkpoint size does not depend on measurement size** — two investigations identical but for what they measured, 100 bytes of output per experiment against 100 kilobytes, produce checkpoints of *exactly* the same size. **F13's claim is measured rather than repeated**: the same forty stored by value come to **1.21 MB**, about 18× the limit at a conservative 30 KB of output each — the control without which 64 KiB would pass equally for a design that stored everything and was tested with small results. `ResultNotStoredError` names the state and the store disagreeing, which is what a checkpoint carried to another machine produces, since S-5.1 partitions recordings by environment. **One incidental fix**: the base exception was `ReferenceError`, which shadows a Python builtin (`ruff` A001) — now `ExperimentReferenceError`. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught**, including raising the per-entry budget until the arithmetic stops holding and raising the stated limit until the by-value control passes; baseline re-run green after the pass.

### S-6.4 — Post-patch staleness policy
Depends: S-6.1
AC:
- After a patch ships, screening results for workloads touching modified files are invalidated
- Untouched workloads keep their measurements
- Pending findings whose context files were modified are invalidated and re-investigated rather than repaired from a stale chain

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/state/staleness.py`, ADR 067, 19 tests in `tests/state/test_post_patch_staleness.py`. All three AC met. **F14 and §6's interacting-findings flaw are the same question about two artifacts**, so there is one mechanism and two consequences. The obstacle: **nothing records which files a workload touches** — S-4.1's `Workload` has an entry point, a fixture and a reset method and no notion of the source it executes, and the honest source is a measurement (S-3.9 captures stacks, and a frame names a file), so this module takes coverage from its caller rather than inventing it. **Unrecorded is a third state and it invalidates**: a workload whose files nobody recorded cannot be *shown* unaffected, and flattening that to *fresh* keeps a measurement the patch may have invalidated — the exact route to repairing from a stale chain that §6 exists to close. `UNCOVERED` stays **distinct from `STALE` in the report while identical in the action**, because the fixes differ — a stale workload needs re-screening, an uncovered one means nobody is recording what the workloads run, and one number hides a missing instrument behind a routine invalidation (S-3.1's four-way split, same reason). The distinction is in the type too: `files` is `frozenset[str] | None` with a named `Coverage.unrecorded` constructor, since an empty set is a *claim* that the subject runs no modified file and `None` is the absence of one. **There is no `FindingAction` meaning repair-from-a-stale-chain** — two members, only `FRESH` yields `REPAIR`, and the test enumerates `Freshness` so a state added later must decide what it means rather than defaulting to repairable. **Paths are normalized to one form and an unnormalizable one is refused**: git reports repo-relative forward-slash paths and a stack frame reports an absolute one, and intersecting the two forms yields the empty set — every workload reads unaffected, every stale measurement is kept, and **nothing raises**. **A test found that same bug in the guard itself**: `PurePath.is_absolute()` is platform-dependent, so on Windows `/home/allen/subject/src/api.py` has no drive, reports `False`, and would have normalized to a repo-relative-looking path matching nothing git reports — correctly refused on Linux, silently accepted here. `_absolute_anywhere` now checks both flavours. **The policy is only as good as the coverage it is given, and nothing produces it yet**: every workload reports `UNCOVERED` until something records what they run, so everything is re-screened after every ship — correct for the current system and **visible in the report** rather than a policy that looks like it is saving work while keeping stale measurements. **Sabotage-verified on fifteen properties**: fourteen caught on the first pass, and the fifteenth survived because the *test* was weak — it asserted the stale file's name appeared in `report.describe()`, which also prints the patch's own modified files, so it passed via the header line whatever the per-workload line said. Now asserted on the assessment's own line. **Fifth time a passing sabotage has meant a weak test rather than weak code.**

**EPIC 6 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/state/investigation.py`, ADR 068, 17 tests in `tests/state/test_state_composed.py`. The epic finished with four stories, 95 passing tests and **no way to run one investigation through them**: performing its own sentence — *state that survives crashes, and knowledge that survives rewinds* — took four modules in the right order plus three joins that lived in none of them, and **no test file in the epic touched more than two of the four**. **Three defects.** (1) *The staleness policy's answer had nowhere to go.* S-6.1 held `screening` as a flat `Sequence[JsonValue]` and S-6.4 invalidates **per workload** — nothing in a flat sequence of opaque entries says which workload an entry belongs to, so a correct answer **could not be applied** and the caller had to rebuild the channel from a shape that does not carry the identity the rebuild needs. Neither story could see it: one owns the shape and knows nothing of the rule, the other owns the rule and takes coverage as an argument. `screening` is now keyed by workload id and `apply_ship` is the rebuild, once. (2) *`experiments` accepted a full measurement.* S-6.3 bounds a checkpoint by storing references and S-6.1's channel is `list[JsonValue]`, so a node appending the measurement itself satisfied the schema, the reducer **and** `check_update` — every check either story makes — and F13's guarantee held only as long as every caller remembered; reproduced against the shipped modules before the fix. `record_experiment` is the one way in and `check_state` refuses a log holding anything else. It also enforces the size limit, which had the same shape of gap: S-6.3 proved the bound for 40 references and S-5.4 caps investigation at 40, but the two live in different modules and nothing joined them. (3) *Nothing produced coverage*, so S-6.4's correct *unrecorded is not untouched* left every workload invalidated forever — but a reference carries the experiment key and the key names the workload, so `coverage_from_state` reads back what the log already holds. **It is workload-granular, not file-granular, and says so** (file coverage is S-3.9's stacks). **Sabotage-verified on twelve properties, all caught — after four survived the first pass.** Three were **weak tests rather than weak code**: the size-limit test built its oversized state from raw measurements so it failed the *reference* check first and removing the size check broke nothing; the shipped-finding test left the patched finding out of the pending list so removing the filter changed nothing; and there was no test for an out-of-range index at all. **Sixth time a passing sabotage has meant a weak test.** The fourth survivor was **an artefact of the harness**: the runner passed `-m "not slow"`, deselecting the Postgres tests, and the assertion that `learn` writes to failure memory lives in one — re-run without the filter it was caught. A sabotage harness that excludes tests silently reports properties as unverified; the Epic 5 run was invalidated by a different fault in the same script, and both were visible only because the baseline is re-checked *after* the pass.

---

# EPIC 7 — Explorer agent

**Goal:** turn an unknown repository into a runnable, scalable, resettable workload.

**This is the riskiest component. Build it alone and prove it before anything downstream.**

### S-7.1 — Framework fingerprinting
Depends: S-0.1
AC:
- Detects framework, version, ORM, database, test runner from manifest files and imports
- Returns a structured fingerprint used to key playbooks
- Unknown frameworks produce an honest "unsupported" result

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/fingerprint.py`, ADR 070, 23 tests in `tests/explorer/test_fingerprint.py`. All three AC met, and **nothing here calls a model** — reading `pyproject.toml` and looking for `manage.py` is a function. **The version is reported as *declared*, because that is all a manifest holds**: `django>=5.0` is a **constraint**, not a version — what is importable could be 5.0 or 5.2, and on a project with a lockfile it is whatever the lock pinned rather than either — so recording it as *the version* would put a number in the fingerprint that nothing measured, which is the shape of claim the first non-negotiable exists to prevent. The field is `declared_version`, `describe()` says so in words, and the installed version is left to S-7.2, the story that stands the environment up and can ask it. **Identified-and-unsupported is a different answer from unknown**: *this is Flask, which is not supported yet* sends somebody to the roadmap and *nothing here looks like a web application* sends them to check the directory — so `Unsupported` carries what it **did** identify, and `Framework` names frameworks this system cannot ground (naming is not supporting). The two outcomes are **exclusive by construction** — `Fingerprint | Unsupported`, and a `Fingerprint` is only built for a supported framework (S-4.5's rule: a healthy-looking result with an empty field reads as success at every call site). **Every facet carries the file that established it**, which also lets the fingerprint keep strengths of evidence apart rather than flattening them: `psycopg` in the dependencies says Postgres is *possible*, `ENGINE: django.db.backends.postgresql` says it is what runs — the settings file wins, and where only the driver is found the evidence reads **"a driver, not a configured engine"**; `manage.py` beats a dependency list for the same reason, since a project can depend on Django for one management command but only an application has `manage.py`. **A facet nothing establishes is `None`**, enumerated by `undetermined` so the gaps are reportable and S-7.2 knows what to find out. **The playbook key is framework and *major* version** — a playbook learned against Django 5.0 applies to 5.0.3, and keying on the full version would make every patch release a cold start, so S-13.5's learning curve would never bend; a constraint with no determinable floor keys as `unversioned` rather than under a version nobody established. A test asserts **fingerprinting ColdFix's own root refuses it honestly**, since `realtime.py` had the mirror of that trap. **Sabotage-verified on fourteen properties, all caught** — two needed the *sabotage* rewritten rather than the code or the test: defaulting `Detected.evidence` changed no call site because every construction passes one, and the poetry pattern never matched the file. **A sabotage that changes nothing is not evidence of anything**, which is the same failure the harness itself had in the last two composition checks.

### S-7.2 — Environment standup
Depends: S-2.1, S-7.1
AC:
- Starts the database, installs dependencies, runs migrations
- Distinguishes "database not started" from "database started but rejecting connections"
- `logs(service)` and `ps()` tools available to the agent for diagnosing failures

Notes: the two failure states above look identical without log access. The agent needs those tools or it guesses.

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/standup.py`, ADR 071, 18 tests in `tests/explorer/test_standup.py` (2 `postgres`-marked, run against a real container). All three AC met. **The two states are told apart by two probes, not by parsing one error message**: `psycopg` raises `OperationalError` for both, and the text differs by driver version, locale and server release, so matching on it is a guess dressed as a check. The socket is probed on its own *before* the protocol — refused → `NOT_LISTENING` (wait, or check the port); accepts but the protocol fails → `REFUSING` (read the server's message; **waiting will not fix credentials**); both succeed → `READY`. That is a measurement rather than an interpretation, and it is the fork the note says an agent cannot see unaided. **Four states, because there are four repairs**: `NO_CONTAINER` is not a flavour of `NOT_LISTENING` — an exited container and a server still initialising look identical at the socket — so Docker is asked first; and `UNKNOWN` is not a failure, because reporting a verdict when Docker itself is unreachable would send the agent to restart a service it cannot see (S-3.1's rule). A test asserts every state prescribes a **different** action, so the split cannot decay into decoration. **Migrations are not attempted against a database that is not accepting** — the common way standup goes wrong, and it produces exactly the error the note is about — so the database is diagnosed *between* starting it and migrating. The commands are supplied rather than derived: S-7.1 says what the project is, and what stands *this* project up is a fact about its tooling that E14's adapter owns. **Two measurements worth keeping.** The probe timeout is **per resolved address, not per probe**: a host resolving to both `::1` and `127.0.0.1` with a server on only one pays it once before reaching the other — four seconds a diagnosis against `localhost` on Windows, which is most of why this test file dropped from 55s to 19s. And **the production guard reached across an epic boundary and was right to**: pointing a test container's database at `postgres` was refused by S-2.5 because that name matches no test-name pattern, so the container is created with a `coldfix_*` database — a guard that only fired inside its own epic would not be a guard. **Sabotage-verified on twelve properties, all caught — after two survived, both for want of a test**: nothing forced the `UNKNOWN` branch (Docker runs on this machine, and the branch is about what `diagnose` does with *cannot say*), and the stop-at-first-failure test asserted the *verdict* — continuing past a failure leaves both `stopped_at` and `succeeded` unchanged, so it passed either way; it now asserts the later steps were never attempted. **Eighth time a passing sabotage has meant a weak test.**

### S-7.3 — Entry point enumeration
Depends: S-7.2
AC:
- Enumerates HTTP routes, CLI entry points, management commands, background job handlers, integration tests
- Returns candidates ranked by likely usefulness
- Handles frameworks where routes are dynamically registered

**DONE (2026-08-11)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/entrypoints.py`, ADR 072, 43 tests in `tests/explorer/test_entry_points.py`. All three AC met, and **nothing here calls a model** — reading `urls.py` is parsing and asking Django for its route table is a subprocess. **AC 3 decides the design, and it is S-7.1's finding one level down**: a manifest's `django>=5.0` is a constraint and the installed version is a different fact; a `path()` call is a *declaration* and the route table is a different fact. A URLconf is a module, not configuration — a DRF router registers six routes per viewset when it imports, `include()` splices another application's table in under a prefix, a comprehension over a list of models produces a route per model — so a parser reading `path(...)` calls does not miss those by being weak, it misses them by being a parser. There are therefore **two enumerators kept apart by how each found the thing**: `PARSED` establishes that a call appears in a file and nothing about whether it registers or what prefix it lands under; `RESOLVED` runs a program in the **subject's** interpreter that calls `django.setup()` and walks `get_resolver().url_patterns`. **`django` is now a dev dependency for that reason and only that reason** — nothing under `src/` imports it, the program is source text, and the alternative was testing AC 3 against a fake resolver that would assert only what the fake was written to believe (the `langgraph` precedent, and S-0.7b's rule). **The route table is claimed complete only when the framework answered**, since a parse cannot prove completeness of something built by running code and ADR 009's *endpoint* predicate would otherwise be satisfied by a repository whose routes are all registered by a router. **The second half of that was found by a test and corrected the design**: `include("shop.api")` imports eagerly, so one broken URLconf takes the whole table with it and the resolver *answers* — zero routes, one problem — which counted as available-therefore-complete reports a repository as having no endpoints when what happened is that nobody could read them. **What a parse cannot expand is recorded rather than dropped** (`Unexpanded`, with the construct and the reason), which is the honest form of AC 3 before an environment exists; an `include(...)` mount is recorded the same way and **is not emitted as a candidate**, because requesting a prefix returns 404 and scored as a parameterless route it would sit at the very top of the list the Explorer works down. **AC 2's ranking is anchored on S-7.8 and says in words that it is a prior**: there is exactly one downstream gate, so a candidate is useful to the degree it looks able to pass it, and every term is a reason to expect it will or will not — kind 1–4, no path parameter +4 (*it addresses a set, and a set is what grows*), each parameter −2, each segment of depth −1, framework or plumbing −10. Infrastructure routes are **ranked last, never dropped**: AC 1 is enumeration and AC 2 is order. **Three unfamiliar repositories were enumerated and two ranking rules came out of that rather than out of review** — netbox ranked **39** routes level at the top with no depth term (18 with it), and it produced **430 unexpanded registration sites against 86 parsed routes**, so AC 3 is most of that repository rather than a DRF hypothetical. **One incident recorded rather than quietly fixed**: the enumerator was run against S-0.6's holdout during development and `tests/test_holdout_discipline.py` caught it; the depth rule was re-derived from netbox alone, the any-segment infrastructure rule stands on its a priori reason, nothing was added to `ALLOWED`, and the committed tests name only the development targets. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-five properties, all caught — after four survived**: three were **weak tests that passed for a reason other than the one they claimed** (the parameter test compared routes of different depth, so the depth term separated them; the management-command test compared `rebuild_reports` with `migrate`, so the collection-name bonus separated them; and no test reached the branch where the interpreter cannot be *started*, since a settings module that will not import is a command that runs and exits non-zero), and the fourth was a harness pattern that never matched. **Ninth time a passing sabotage has meant a weak test.** Baseline re-run green after the pass.

### S-7.4 — Auth resolution
Depends: S-7.3
AC:
- Detects the auth scheme from settings and failed-request responses
- Creates credentials and attaches them to subsequent requests
- Handles custom user models with non-standard username fields
- Playbook consulted before exploration

**DONE (2026-08-13)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/auth.py`, ADR 073, 44 tests in `tests/explorer/test_auth.py` (2 `postgres`-marked, all `slow`). All four AC met, and **nothing here calls a model** — reading `settings.REST_FRAMEWORK` is a subprocess, base64 is a function, and deciding what a `401` means is a table. **AC 1 names two sources in one line and they are not two ways of learning one fact**, which is S-7.1's finding and S-7.3's a third time: `DEFAULT_AUTHENTICATION_CLASSES` says what DRF will *accept* and `AUTHENTICATION_BACKENDS` says what can verify a password, but a view can be `AllowAny` inside a project whose defaults demand a token, and a project with no auth settings at all can wrap one view in `login_required`. So `Established` records which source settled a requirement, **the observation wins**, and a disagreement is *reported* rather than corrected — the same shape (settings naming `TOKEN`, the route answering `403`) is also exactly what a session-authenticated DRF endpoint looks like from outside. **A followed redirect is never a route that answered.** `login_required` does not challenge, it redirects, and a client with redirects enabled turns that `302` into a `200` holding the login page — nothing in the status, headers or body separates it from the endpoint answering, so the Explorer grounds on a login form and S-7.8 then *correctly* reports that its bytes do not grow. `Reply` carries `answered_path`, which every HTTP client already has (`response.url`, `redirect_chain`), and a `200` from another path is never read as *no authentication required*. **Credentials are minted, not negotiated**: driving the login form needs a CSRF token out of a page, the name of a field this stage has just established is not always `username`, and it stops dead at a second factor — while Django's own test client writes a session row and hands back a cookie, which is what this does in the subject's interpreter. **A login flow is a flow; a session is a row.** **A credential is a fixture, not a setup step** — S-2.6 resets between S-7.8's two scales and takes the user row with it, so a captured credential works for the first half of every sweep and returns `401` for the second, reading as *the endpoint failed at scale*; `Credential` carries the `Recipe` that made it and minting is idempotent by construction. **AC 3 is answered by `get_user_model()`, never by a parser**: `create_user(username=…)` against a model whose `USERNAME_FIELD` is `email` writes an account nothing can log in as, and a model whose manager cannot make users is **refused** rather than worked around, since writing the row directly stores a hash that fails later as a `401` rather than here as an error. **`JWT` is detected and deliberately not minted** — every package signs its own way and none is installed to verify against, and a branch that ships unverified is worth less than a refusal naming what would make it work. `djangorestframework` is now a **dev** dependency on ADR 072's exact terms. **AC 4 is a seam, not a schema**: `playbook_from_store` reads `Collection.PLAYBOOKS` out of S-6.2's journal under S-7.1's key, `resolve_auth` consults it **before the first request**, and entries are carried **unread** — S-13.1 owns what one means and S-13.2 owns the gate that makes trusting one safe. **Every status code in the tests was produced by the framework**, through `django.test.Client` in the subject's own interpreter: real `login_required` redirects, DRF's `401`-with-`WWW-Authenticate` for tokens and its `403`-without for sessions. **That found a defect nothing else could**: the token branch guarded the *import* of `rest_framework.authtoken.models`, but a model whose app is absent from `INSTALLED_APPS` still imports — Django leaves it without a manager and the `AttributeError` lands on the next line — so an ordinary project (DRF present, its optional token app not) produced a traceback instead of the sentence naming the app to add. **A guard on the wrong verb is not a guard.** **Sabotage-verified on thirty-six properties across two passes, all caught — after one survived.** The survivor was neither weak code nor a weak test but **a redundant condition**: `needs_credential and can_be_minted` reads as two guards, and `NONE` is not a mintable scheme precisely because there is nothing to mint for it, so the first term could never decide anything. It is now one condition saying so, with the intent it expressed verified from the other side — `can_be_minted` gaining `NONE` is a sabotage the tests catch. **Tenth time a passing sabotage has meant something other than working code**, and the first time it has meant *the code says a thing twice*. One gap the pass exposed by omission: a `404` fell through to `UNKNOWN`, whose sentence is *something is enforcing authentication* — false about a route that is not there, and not hypothetical, since S-7.3 emits routes with path parameters and requesting one literally returns `404`. `speaks_to_auth` separates them, with a control asserting the four real answers are not reported as inconclusive.

### S-7.5 — Fixture discovery
Depends: S-7.2
AC:
- Locates existing factories or fixtures (factory_boy, pytest fixtures, management commands)
- Uses them in preference to synthesis
- Records the fixture recipe in the workload artifact

**DONE (2026-08-13)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/fixtures.py`, ADR 074, 32 tests in `tests/explorer/test_fixtures.py` (all `slow`). All three AC met, and **nothing here calls a model** — finding a class whose `Meta.model` is `Book` is parsing and counting rows either side of a run is arithmetic. **AC 3 decides the design, and by arithmetic rather than by taste**: S-4.1 already defines the artifact, and `FixtureRecipe` requires an `entity`, a `per_parent` and a `distribution` — *how many rows of what, spread how* — and **a file establishes none of the three**. `BookFactory` says it makes books; it does not say that `create_batch(10)` also wrote ten authors through a `SubFactory`, and `per_parent` is exactly that ratio. So **a located factory is a declared capability and a recipe is what a run wrote** — the epic's running distinction a fourth time, here *forced* by an existing schema rather than chosen, since minting from the parse would put three unmeasured numbers into the artifact that keys S-5.1's replay cache and carries S-3.3's blindness qualification. **AC 2's preference is anchored on S-7.8, and the anchor is whether the size can vary**: kind 1–4, takes a count +4, fixed set of rows −6, names its model +1, contents countable +1. **A `loaddata` fixture file is worth least, and that is the finding rather than a slight** — fixed rows with fixed primary keys, so loading it twice is a collision rather than twice the data, and **a repository whose only fixture is a `.json` file needs S-7.6 despite having a fixture**; `NeedsSynthesis` carries what *was* located, because *has a fixture that cannot be scaled* and *has nothing* send a reader to two different places (S-7.1's rule). A pytest fixture is located because AC 1 names it and **never chosen**: driving one means running the subject's own suite, and S-2.4 refuses to edit a test to change what it seeds. **The distribution is measured by a real `GROUP BY` and refused when it cannot be named** — forty children over ten parents divides evenly and says nothing about whether one parent holds thirty-one, which is the assumption S-3.3's whole argument is *about*; **parents holding nothing are counted too**, since they are absent from a grouping over the child table and leaving them out makes *nine parents with one child and one with none* indistinguishable from *nine with one*. A non-uniform spread **does not become `POWER_LAW` by elimination**: `Distribution` has three values and none means *not uniform*, so `recipe_from` refuses with the measured spread and names S-7.7, the story that makes distribution a parameter of `scale()` rather than a label on found data. A `per_parent` measured as zero is refused rather than clamped to one. **The entity is the model that grew by what was asked for**, not the largest grower — seeding five authors with three books each grows the book model by fifteen, and `scale(n)` means n *of the entity*. `factory-boy` is now a **dev** dependency on ADR 072's terms. **Writing a real factory found a real defect**: the rule for skipping abstract bases was *no `Meta.model`*, and a factory that subclasses another to add a `RelatedFactoryList` declares no `Meta` at all and inherits its parent's — that is the ordinary shape of the factory building the most interesting data, **the only shape where `per_parent` is not 1**, and the rule dropped exactly it. Abstractness is what `Meta.abstract` says. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-nine properties across two passes, all caught — after two survived, and both survivors were the same shape of weak test: a comparison whose subjects differ in more than the one thing under test.** The ranking test put a factory against a fixture file — three terms apart — so zeroing the scalability penalty left the order unchanged; it is now two mechanisms differing only in scalability, with the end-to-end ordering kept as a separate assertion. And **every exercising test started from an empty database, where a total and a difference are the same number**, so nothing established that `grew` subtracts — a repository shipping seeded data is ordinary, and there the total would name the wrong entity and inflate every count; there is now a test that seeds before it measures. **Eleventh and twelfth times a passing sabotage has meant a weak test.** One test was also renamed for claiming more than it exercised: it triggers an unimportable module, not a factory raising.

### S-7.6 — Fixture synthesis
Depends: S-7.5
AC:
- Reads the schema and walks foreign key chains to construct valid rows
- Handles required fields, enums, and unique constraints
- Handles multi-level FK chains discovered on IntegrityError
- Falls back gracefully and reports when synthesis fails

**DONE (2026-08-13)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/synthesis.py`, ADR 075, 33 tests in `tests/explorer/test_synthesis.py` (12 `slow`, 21 in the fast subset). All four AC met, and **nothing here calls a model** — walking foreign keys is a topological sort and picking the first of a field's `choices` is indexing. **AC 3 names an instrument, and it does so because a schema lies about itself in three ordinary ways**: `blank=True` is a *form* concept, so a `blank=True, null=False` column is required by every database and optional in every admin form (the commonest way a plan built from the models alone fails on its first row); a `UniqueConstraint` is **invisible on the fields it covers**; and an unapplied migration leaves the database stricter than the models describing it. So **the ORM declares and the database decides** — the epic's distinction a fifth time, with the roles unusually sharp — and the loop is plan, attempt, read the refusal, revise. **The planner is a pure function over a schema mapping and only the executor runs in the subject's interpreter**, which puts ordering, counts, value choice and revision where they are typed and unit-tested and leaves a thin program that creates rows and reports what was refused; 21 of the 33 tests need no subprocess. **An error message is not a diagnosis** (S-7.2's rule, second occurrence): integrity error text varies by driver, server version and locale, so `psycopg`'s structured `column_name`/`constraint_name` are read *first* and patterns over the message are the SQLite fallback — and **`Learned` records which of the two settled it**, because a revision built on a regex over someone's translated error is worth less than one built on the server's own field. A refusal naming nothing — a check constraint, a trigger — is **reported, never revised**, since the same plan submitted again is not made satisfiable by being submitted again. **The whole plan is one transaction, and that was found by a real run rather than by review**: with one transaction per step, the first attempt created its publishers and authors, failed on a unique book code, and the revision then failed on **the author e-mail it had itself inserted a second earlier** — a retry loop that writes either rolls back what it wrote or poisons its own next attempt, and rows left behind would inflate S-7.5's `grew` and S-7.8's scaling either way.
