The exception is caught *outside* the atomic block, because querying inside a broken one raises `TransactionManagementError` and buries the integrity error underneath it. **Refusals come before writes where a refusal is possible**: an unfillable column type is reported with the model, the field and the type (writing `None` fails at the database with a worse message; writing a zero produces rows that are valid and meaningless), and a required FK cycle is reported the same way. A field with `choices` takes one of them, because Django does not enforce choices at the database and a row holding `coldfix-0` in a status column inserts cleanly and then breaks the application that reads it. **Synthesized data is uniform by construction and says so** — `07-use-cases.md` §5 is blunt that *if every generated customer has three orders, an N+1 that only hurts customers with three thousand stays invisible* — so the recipe says `UNIFORM` because that is what was **built**, and `Synthesis.blindness` states the consequence in words so it travels with the fixture. **That is the mirror of S-7.5 and the difference is the point**: there the distribution is *measured* and a non-uniform spread is refused; here it is *chosen*, and until S-7.7 the only choice is the blindest one. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-nine properties across two passes, all caught — after one survived**, and the survivor was **an unreachable branch** (the third known cause, not a weak test): nothing made the subject's program fail to answer, so replacing that refusal with an empty payload changed no outcome. It now has a test that breaks the settings module, because *this project has no models* and *this project would not load* are two answers and flattening them turns a broken subject into what reads as a bad argument.

### S-7.7 — Skewed fixture generation
Depends: S-7.6, S-3.3
AC:
- Can generate power-law and long-tail distributions across relationships
- Distribution is a parameter of `scale()`
- Recorded in every measurement taken with those fixtures

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/synthesis.py` (wiring) and `src/coldfix/screening/workload.py` (the widened artifact), ADR 076, 19 tests in `tests/explorer/test_skewed_fixtures.py` plus one added to S-7.5's file. **S-3.3 already generates the three shapes and nothing here re-implements it** — this story is the wiring and the bookkeeping, and **the bookkeeping is where the decision was**. `FixtureRecipe.per_parent` is one `int`, a skewed fixture has no single children-per-parent, and that field feeds `digest()`, which keys S-5.1's replay cache. **`per_parent` is now the heaviest parent and `parents` is a new optional field** — agreed with the user before implementation. The two alternatives were worse: **the mean is the one reading that is never the interesting one** (the whole reason to build a long tail is the request that takes minutes while every other request stays fast, and that request is made by the heaviest parent, so recording the mean would name the shape and then describe it with the number the shape exists to avoid); and the nominal divisor keeps the field constant while making it mean two different things by distribution, which stops S-4.1's own *the number `Σk²` is about* from being true for two of the three shapes. With `entity`, `per_parent` and `distribution`, `parents` makes the fixture **reproducible**, since `allocate` is deterministic — and `None` means *not recorded*, **never one parent**. **The shape applies to the target's own parents, not to the whole chain**: applied at every level of `Publisher → Author → Book` it compounds into one nobody asked for, and the cost S-3.3 is about is paid where the request walks rather than three joins up. **The per-row parent assignment is computed in the planner**, so the subject indexes a list and decides nothing — the arithmetic stays S-3.3's, and the shape lands in the plan and therefore in the replay key rather than in a decision made at write time. **A shape that will not fit is refused, never flattened**: `allocate` guarantees every parent at least one child, so twenty parents and twenty children leave nothing to skew with, and building it anyway would write `LONG_TAIL` into the one field that exists to stop a fixture being described as a shape it does not have; the refusal names which knob to turn. **The blindness note inverts under a skew** — a flat result under a long tail is a *much stronger* exclusion than a flat result under uniform data, because the cost was given somewhere to hide and did not hide there — and keeping one sentence for both would make it boilerplate. **`07-use-cases.md` §5's admission stops being true of this tool.** **Sabotage-verified on fourteen properties, all caught, no survivors** — but **two patterns did not apply on the first run**, which is ADR 072's *a sabotage that changes nothing is not evidence of anything*, and rewriting them surfaced a genuine gap: the parent count recorded by S-7.5's discovered recipe had **no test at all**, because the pass ran only the two files this story touched most. **A sabotage pass scoped to the files a story edits will miss what the story changed elsewhere.** One unrelated flake was seen and confirmed as one: `test_perturbation.py::test_in_serial_code_the_sensitivity_is_just_the_share_of_runtime` failed once in a full fast-subset run, passed alone, passed with this story's changes stashed, and passed on a second full run — S-3.14 measures timing sensitivity and CPU contention distorts it, the same class as the recorded `test_isolation.py` flake.

### S-7.8 — Objective work verification — **SAFETY**
Depends: S-7.6
Why: the agent is incentivized to claim success because success completes its task.
AC:
- `work_verified` is computed by the harness, not the agent
- Requires all three: query count, response bytes, and wall time each rise between N=10 and N=100 by stated thresholds
- The agent cannot override or supply this value
- A workload failing verification is rejected regardless of what the agent claims

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/work.py`, ADR 077, 31 tests in `tests/explorer/test_work_verification.py`. All four AC met. **Half of this already existed and is not re-implemented**: S-4.1 put `work_verified` on the artifact as a property with no field behind it, and what was missing was the half that produces the numbers it reads. **The signature is the enforcement.** `verify_work` takes a path, an interpreter, a seeding plan and a credential — **there is no argument through which a query count, a byte count or a duration could arrive** — and `accept` takes exactly one parameter, the harness's own measurements. A gate that accepted a claim and ignored it would be one refactor away from honouring it, so there is no `claimed`, no `override` and no `force`; **a test asserts the signature by inspection and fails the moment somebody adds one to make a demo pass.** Fifth instance of the recurring construction: make the unsafe state have no object to exist in. **The three metrics are measured in the subject's interpreter** — wall time and bytes could be read from outside, but **the query count cannot**, since nothing outside the process knows how many statements a request issued — so one program drives the request under Django's own `CaptureQueriesContext` and reports all three for the same invocations. **A warm-up precedes the measured requests** (the first request through a Django stack pays imports, template compilation and connection setup, and charging those to the small scale point is how a flat workload comes to look like a growing one) and **the median of five is taken, not one reading**, because the wall-time condition is a ratio that a single scheduling slice flips. **The sweep resets between scale points**: measuring N=100 on top of N=10 makes it a measurement of a hundred and ten, and the growth is then arithmetic rather than a property of the workload. **An error response is refused rather than measured** — an error page is cheap, constant and identical at every scale, exactly the profile the check exists to reject. **F6's first condition is ADR 051's corrected one** (*queries did not fall*); this story's AC still carries the audit's original *rise*, which rejects every correctly batched endpoint, and the correction predates the criterion. **The guarantee is the schema, not the type checker**: `Workload(..., work_verified=True)` produces **no mypy error at all**, because pydantic's plugin does not model `extra="forbid"` as a signature — it type-checks and fails only at runtime, which is exactly why `CLAUDE.md` puts enforcement in code rather than convention. **The aggregate false negative is kept and named**, with a `/count/` endpoint in the fixture so the refusal is exercised rather than assumed. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-eight properties across three passes, all caught — after two survived and three patterns never applied.** **All six attacks on the safety properties were caught on the first pass** (removing the gate's check, adding `force`, reading the verdict off the wrapper, un-forbidding unknown fields, un-freezing the artifact, making `work_verified` unconditional). The two genuine survivors were **unreachable or unobserved rather than wrong**: the empty-samples guard could not be reached because `repeats` was **silently clamped to one** (clamping answers a question nobody asked — it is now a refusal, and the malformed-answer branch is forced with `monkeypatch` at the subprocess boundary), and **removing the warm-up changed only noise**, so the warm-up is now *timed and reported* — a field that disappears when it does. One weak assertion of S-7.3's recorded shape: `min <= seconds <= max` is satisfied by **every** sample, so a sabotage returning `samples[0]` walked through the test meant to establish the median; three disagreeing samples are now fed in at the subprocess boundary, where what is under test is the arithmetic rather than the answer. **A residual risk is stated rather than solved**: the agent chooses *when* to call this, and wall time is noisy near its threshold, so repeated attempts on a borderline workload will eventually pass one — the median narrows the window and S-7.10's cap bounds the attempts, but nothing here makes a borderline case deterministic.

### S-7.9 — Workload artifact emission
Depends: S-7.8, S-4.1
AC:
- Emits a validated workload object
- `evidence_of_work` is mandatory and harness-computed
- Reset method verified by S-2.7 before emission

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/emission.py`, ADR 078, 19 tests in `tests/explorer/test_emission.py` (18 in the fast subset; the envelope is an artifact and most rules about it need no subprocess). All three AC met. **`03-agents.md` §2.4 gives the reason** — *`evidence_of_work` is mandatory and exists to make "it ran but did nothing" structurally unreportable as success* — and this is where *structurally* has to become true of a **document** rather than of an object in memory, which is a different problem. **S-4.1's solution does not survive serialization**: `work_verified` is a property with no field behind it, which is exactly what stops an agent writing it into a `Workload` — and a property is **not serialized**, so a document that must carry the evidence has to carry a **copy**, and a copy is the one thing an agent could edit. **So the copy is checked against the recomputation.** `EmittedWorkload` holds the verdict and the evidence as ordinary required fields, and validating one **recomputes both from the observations it carries** and refuses any disagreement — tampering does not produce a more convincing workload, it produces a document that will not load. **Mandatory and powerless is the pair AC 2 asks for**, and neither half alone gives it: mandatory without the check is a field an agent fills in, and the check without the field leaves *unverified* indistinguishable from *not yet asked*. Three things are compared, and the third is not obvious — the verdict, the evidence string, and the envelope's `reset_strategy` against the workload's `reset_method`; **the evidence string names the scales and the ratio**, which is what makes the subtler edit (leave the verdict alone, improve an observation) fail too. **The reset method is proved by a type rather than asserted by a name**: `emit` requires S-2.7's `VerifiedReset`, whose constructor refuses a failing report, so there is no way to emit holding only the name and **no unverified proof exists to offer**. A proof of a *different* strategy is refused — verification is a property of a strategy **on a project**, not of a strategy (S-0.5 had rollback alone pass its own check and fail 10/10 on sequences) — and the cycle count travels in the document so a reader knows whether the guarantee behind it was established over three cycles or ten. Emission goes through S-7.8's `accept`, so an unverified workload is refused at the boundary rather than emitted with `work_verified: false` for somebody downstream to notice. **One consequence worth stating: the evidence string is now part of a validation contract**, so changing the wording of `work_evidence` invalidates every previously emitted document — the correct direction, but it makes the string an interface rather than a message, and S-17.2 will have to version it to publish old emissions. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties across two passes, all caught — after one survived**, and the survivor was **S-7.4's shape again**: `emit` records `reset.strategy` where the guard immediately above has just established it equals `workload.reset_method`, so swapping them changes no outcome and no test can tell them apart. The pair is separable only by attacking **both at once**, and that combined sabotage *is* caught — removing the guard and recording the claimed strategy accepts a wrong proof. **Second time a passing sabotage has meant the code states a thing twice**, and the generalisation is that **a guard which forces two expressions equal makes every later choice between them untestable**: find the pair, and attack the guard and the choice together.

### S-7.10 — Caps and honest failure
Depends: S-5.4, S-7.11
AC:
- 60-step cap enforced
- Progress check escalates after 15 steps with no new information
- Per-stage attempt budget enforced on top of the global cap (S-7.11 supplies the stages)
- On failure, reports **which stage never completed** and what was attempted there
- **Never reports success when no workload does real work**

Notes: "reports what was attempted" is a transcript; "stage 4 never completed, here is its predicate and the last error" is something a user can act on and S-17.2 can publish. The per-stage budget is the tighter instrument — S-0.3's grounding runs took 5–19 minutes, and detecting at stage 2 that a repo will not ground saves the other seven stages.

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/run.py`, ADR 080, 22 tests in `tests/explorer/test_run.py`. All five AC met, and **most of the machinery is S-5.4's, deliberately**: it already compiles `GROUND` at sixty steps with a disposition of `ABORT`, already authorizes *before* the work rather than after, and already has a stall check. Reimplementing any of it would be a second set of caps to keep in step with the first. **What S-5.4 could not supply is the two things this story is about.** **S-7.11 gives "no new information" something to mean**: S-5.4 compares a digest of what a step *concluded*, and left to grounding that digest would have to be the agent's own account of its progress — the self-judged criterion F6 exists to remove — so the digest is **the nine-verdict stage report**, and fifteen steps that move nothing have taught the run nothing whoever believes otherwise. **The digest is the verdicts and not the details**, because a detail string carries row counts and error text that drift between otherwise identical steps (S-5.2's argument about durations, one layer up). **Grounding stalls at fifteen, not S-5.4's three**, and a budget built with the wrong value is **refused rather than corrected** — silently substituting the right one would hide that the caller asked for something else, and escalating after three unchanged reports would abandon a repository mid-install. **The per-stage budget is eight, chosen against S-0.3** (the worst single stage took six attempts) — and **with the defaults it dominates the stall check**: a run hammering one stage is stopped at eight, five steps before fifteen, which is exactly the note's point and is now asserted by a test rather than left as an inference. What the global stall catches is the *other* shape: an agent moving **between** stages, spending steps, changing nothing anywhere. **A failure leads with the incomplete stage**, states what done would have meant in ADR 009's words, gives the last measurement, and lists **only the attempts made at that stage** — the other fifty are noise to somebody fixing this one. `give_up` **returns** rather than raises, because an agent choosing to stop is not an error and the null-result rule makes an honest *this will not ground* a legitimate output. **AC 5 is structural: `finish` is the only method that returns an `EmittedWorkload`**, it calls S-7.9's `emit` → S-7.8's `accept`, and a test asserts by inspection that no other method returns one. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties across two passes, all caught — after one survived**, and the survivor was **the recurring shape a fourth time**: every test attempted the stage that was *also* the incomplete one, so *last attempted* and *first incomplete* coincided and a report built on either read identically. **The generalisation, now stated in four stories: before asserting a value comes from rule A rather than rule B, check the fixture makes A and B disagree.**

### S-7.11 — Stage predicates — **SAFETY**
Depends: S-7.1
Why: without a definition of done per stage, an agent stuck at stage 4 and an agent progressing normally are indistinguishable until the global cap fires.
AC:
- Grounding is modelled as the nine stages in ADR 009, each with a predicate
- **Every predicate is computed by the harness; the agent cannot supply or override one** — the S-7.8 rule, extended to all stages
- Predicates are framework-scoped and resolved through the S-7.1 fingerprint
- A stage whose predicate is already true is complete without action (repos shipping a seeded database skip seeding)
- A test proves an agent claiming a stage complete cannot advance while that stage's predicate is false

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/stages.py`, ADR 079, 41 tests in `tests/explorer/test_stages.py`. All five AC met. **ADR 009 had already made the decision**; this story is the code plus three choices the ADR did not have to make. **An agent may claim, and a claim settles nothing.** `claim(stage, …)` exists and is *meant* to be called — an agent reporting *I think I have configured it* is the agent doing its job, and refusing to hear it only pushes the claim into prose nobody checks — but what comes back is **that stage's predicate measured now**, and a claim about a stage that does not hold is refused with what was measured and what done would have meant. S-7.8's construction generalised: there the gate had no parameter a claim could occupy; here the claim is welcome and powerless. **Three verdicts, because three next moves**: a predicate that could not be evaluated is not one that failed, and *migrations are unapplied* vs *nothing could be asked because the database is not answering* send a reader to two different places (S-7.2's flattened-ignorance mistake in a new costume). **`UNKNOWN` does not complete a stage** — treating ignorance as progress is how a run reaches the final gate having skipped everything — so `first_incomplete` reports the first stage that does not *hold* rather than the first that *fails*. **The seeding predicate excludes the framework's own tables, and that is the story's real finding**: ADR 009's *row counts exceed a threshold in at least two tables*, implemented literally, **is satisfied by a freshly migrated and entirely empty repository**, because `migrate` populates `django_content_type` and `auth_permission` by itself — and the Explorer would go on to measure nothing. Only the application's own tables count; the framework's rows are **reported separately** (they are real rows a reader should see, and what they are not is data). The threshold is ten, S-7.8's small scale point — a database is seeded when it holds enough to be *measured* — and two tables rather than one because the costs this system looks for live in relationships. **The predicates delegate rather than re-deriving**: `configure` runs the framework's own `check` (Django knows what a misconfigured Django looks like and this module does not), and `auth`/`work` read S-7.4's and S-7.8's results rather than recomputing them. The three questions only the framework can answer are asked in **one** subprocess, so a nine-stage report costs one `django.setup()`. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-six properties across two passes, all caught — after three survived.** **All five attacks on the safety properties fell on the first pass.** **All three survivors were the same gap: no test reached the negative case.** `connect`, `endpoint` and `auth` were each only exercised where they hold — SQLite opens anything it can create, the fixture always had a route, and the auth resolution was only ever `None` — so each now has a subject that fails it, plus the control that an *open* route satisfies the stage rather than failing it. **The generalisation: a predicate tested only where it holds is a predicate tested as a constant**, and the fixture that makes the positive case easy is usually why the negative case is missing. One test also failed on first run for a reason worth recording: `assert "0 row(s) exist…" not in detail` **passes on `"30 row(s)"`**, because a negative assertion over formatted text is a substring check and numbers make substrings of each other; it now matches the number with a regex.

Notes: S-0.3 produced sixteen distinct obstacles across three repositories, no two identical, and **every one fell into one of nine stages with no obstacle needing a tenth**. Specifics never repeated; the taxonomy stayed closed. That asymmetry is what makes this tractable — an unfamiliar obstacle inside a known stage is a bounded search against a stated success condition, which is the shape an agent handles. This is the strongest lever found in E0 for the unknown-unknowns problem, and it is what lets S-13.1 key playbook entries to a stage rather than to a whole run.

### S-7.12 — Date-anchored environments
Depends: S-7.2
Why: a repository last touched in 2019 does not break because it is complex; it breaks because we hand it a 2026 toolchain.
AC:
- An anchor date is derived from the repository's most recent commit
- Dependency resolution is constrained to packages published on or before the anchor (`uv --exclude-newer`)
- The interpreter version is read from the repo's own declarations — `python_requires`, classifiers, `tox.ini`, CI matrices — and fetched to match
- The anchor, the resolved dependency set, and any override are all recorded in the workload artifact
- A test grounds a repository whose unpinned dependencies resolve incorrectly at HEAD and correctly at its anchor

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/anchor.py` plus `EnvironmentAnchor` on S-4.1's `Workload`, ADR 081, 27 tests in `tests/explorer/test_anchor.py` (4 marked `index`). All five AC met, and **the premise is measured rather than asserted**: `django>=2.0` resolves to **6.1** today and to **2.1.4** as of a 2019 anchor. **ADR 010 had decided the design**; three questions it did not have to answer came up in the code. **An enumeration is a claim and a floor is a minimum**, and ADR 010 lists the four sources as though they were one kind of evidence. A classifier, a `tox` envlist and a CI matrix each *enumerate* versions the project says it supports; `requires-python = ">=3.8"` says 3.8 works and **nothing whatever about 3.11**. Taking the floor as *the* version hands 3.8 to a project whose CI tests 3.11, so `Basis` records which was found and an enumeration beats a floor. **Versions are compared numerically** — `"3.9" > "3.10"` is true for strings, so a lexical maximum hands a project testing 3.9 through 3.12 the oldest of them. **The committer date, not the author date**: a patch written in 2015 and applied in 2019 was *resolved against* 2019's index by whoever applied it. **An override carries no commit and no separate flag** (two fields that could disagree about whether an override happened eventually would), and **an override with no reason is refused** — ADR 010 requires the override because a contemporary dependency may carry a since-fixed incompatibility or a known vulnerability, and *why this run differs* is the whole value of recording it. **A checkout with no history is refused, not dated today.** **AC 4's "workload artifact" is `Workload`, not Epic 7's envelope**, and reproducibility decides it: ADR 010's *anchoring makes the dependency set a recorded input rather than a function of when the tool happened to run* means the inputs must travel with the thing a later process reasons about; S-0.4's byte-identical guard counters are void if a rerun silently resolves a different Django. `EnvironmentAnchor` is optional and **`None` means not recorded, never resolved-against-today**. **AC 5 needs a real package index**, so those tests carry a new `index` marker — distinct from `slow` and `docker` for the same reason those are distinct from each other. **The bound is carried in the code**: `Anchor.residue` says in words that this covers the Python layer only, and a test asserts the sentence travels, because *the environment is era-matched* is exactly the claim somebody would quote without it. **Sabotage-verified on nineteen properties across two passes, all caught — after two survived and one pattern never applied**, and **both survivors were fixtures that could not discriminate**: the committer-vs-author test set **both** git dates to the same value, and the CI decoys were `v4.2` and `timeout-minutes: 4.5` — **which the version filter discards for an entirely different reason**, so removing the key check changed nothing and the test passed against exactly the code it was written to reject. **The sharper generalisation: a negative test needs a decoy the code would otherwise accept; a decoy rejected by some other rule proves only that the other rule works.**

Notes: see ADR 010. S-0.3's sample was drawn entirely from repositories committed to within three days of selection, which is why the *Python version mismatch* and *dependency resolution failure* rows of its recurrence matrix came back empty — the spike could not have detected this class. **Anchoring covers the Python layer only**; an old `psycopg2` needing a `libpq` current Debian no longer ships is an OS-level problem with no `--exclude-newer` equivalent, and that residue belongs in S-17.2 rather than being described as solved. The anchor must be overridable, since a contemporary dependency version may carry a since-fixed incompatibility or a known vulnerability.

---

**EPIC 7 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-14)** — `tests/explorer/test_explorer_composed.py`, ADR 082, 9 tests. The epic finished with twelve stories, **359 passing tests**, a sabotage pass on every one — and **no way to run one repository through them**: performing its own sentence takes nine modules in the right order and **no test file in the epic touched more than three**. **Six defects, and every one was a join** — not one was a module wrong about its own subject. (1) *A ranked route could not be requested as it came out*: the resolver reports `books/`, every downstream story takes a *path*, so the composed run requested `books/` and got a 404 — (2) *which `resolve_auth` then read as an authentication requirement*, stopping the run with **this route needs a credential nobody made**. The diagnosis was perfect and the input was wrong: **two correct modules producing a confident, wrong answer between them**, which is the shape composition checks exist for. `Candidate.request_path` now exists and is `None` for a *parsed* route (a parse cannot establish what prefix an `include()` mounted a fragment under), and `Enumeration.drivable` filters the ranked list, because it mixes both and leaving every caller to filter is how one of them does not. (3) *The environment could not reach the artifact*: S-7.12 computes an anchor, S-4.1 gained a field to hold one, and **nothing could get from the first to the second** — `verify_work` is the only code that builds a `Workload` and took no environment, so S-7.12's AC 4 was satisfied by a schema and unreachable in practice. (4) *Seeding always synthesized*, so S-7.5's *use existing fixtures in preference to synthesis* was honoured inside its own module and **nowhere else**, and a repository shipping a perfectly good `BookFactory` was measured against invented rows; `verify_work` now takes a `Seeder` defaulting to synthesis. (5) *`target` was required even when a seeder made it meaningless.* (6) **The sharpest: `prefer()` chose the wrong factory.** The subject ships `AuthorFactory` and `BookFactory`; S-7.5 ranks by how well a mechanism seeds *two scales* — a property of the mechanism, not of the workload — so they tied and **the alphabetical tie-break chose `Author`**. The run seeded a hundred authors, drove `/books/` and measured an empty list: **one query, thirteen bytes.** Every S-7.5 test passed throughout, because **none of them had a second factory to choose between**. `prefer(discovery, entity=…)` now prefers a mechanism that builds the entity the workload needs, and nothing infers which entity a route serves — that is the Explorer's to know. **Three of the six are one shape: a value one story produces and another consumes, where nothing in either story's tests holds both ends** — a module's own tests always supply their own inputs, so they cannot see this class at all. **Two of the six were AC satisfied in isolation and unreachable in practice**, which is worth remembering when reading a `DONE` note: *the criterion is met* and *the criterion is reachable from the rest of the system* are different claims. **Sabotage-verified on ten properties, all caught — after two survived**, both fixtures that could not discriminate (the fifth consecutive story to record that shape): removing the resolved-only condition changed nothing because every route in the subject was root-mounted, so a parsed fragment was a valid address **by luck** — the subject now mounts one under `include()`, where the fragment is `orders/` and the address is `/api/orders/`. **Finding a defect and being able to keep it fixed are two different pieces of work.**

---

# EPIC 8 — Diagnostician agent

**Goal:** determine the cause by experiment, and switch instruments when a hypothesis fails.

### S-8.1 — Hypothesis generation
Depends: S-3.1, S-5.5
AC:
- Separate call at temperature 0.8
- Receives the experiment log, exclusions, source under suspicion, and applicable instruments
- Returns a structured hypothesis plus the primitive that would test it
- Routed to the frontier tier; no cascading

**DONE (2026-08-15)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/hypothesis.py`, ADR 084, 17 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_hypothesis.py`, plus two added to S-0.7b's suite. **The first call to a model in this system** — everything before it is deterministic, which is the shape `00-BRIEF.md` §1 argues for. All four AC met, and **two of them were already enforced elsewhere**: `CLAUDE.md`'s *never cascade on hypothesis generation* is S-5.5 (which derives *creative* from §3's table rather than from a caller's declaration) plus S-5.6 (which cascades only a step whose caller supplies a validator), so AC 4 needed **no new mechanism** — it needed this module to use `StepType.HYPOTHESIS_GENERATION` and to have **nowhere to pass a validator**. `generate` has no `validate` parameter and a test asserts it by inspection; third instance of the construction after S-7.8's missing `force` and S-7.10's single exit. **AC 1 required a change to S-0.7b: temperature joins the request digest.** The client had no temperature at all, and adding the parameter is obvious while **adding it to the digest is the decision** — S-0.7b's own argument for including the model applies one axis over, because `03-agents.md` §2.4 sends the Diagnostician's two calls at 0.8 and 0.0 and **those are frequently the same question about the same log**, so without it the recording made for the call that *must not vary* would answer the call that is supposed to, and nothing would fail. Required rather than defaulted, on S-5.4's `max_output_tokens` argument. **The instrument the model names is checked against the instruments it was offered** — a hypothesis proposing a primitive S-3.1 withheld is a step that fails in S-8.2 *with the reason already lost* — and the control is asserted too, since a validator refusing everything would pass the negative test and leave the agent unable to propose anything. **Nothing repairs a malformed answer**: *the model answered something else* and *the model was wrong* need different fixes, and a parser that guesses turns the first into the second. A refusal is checked before the text is read (S-0.7b: a decline is a successful response with an **empty content list**), and a truncated reply is refused because half a JSON object parses as nothing. **Sabotage-verified on fourteen properties, all caught, no survivors** — including all three non-negotiables: the call made at the interpretation temperature, the step declared mechanical so it may cascade, and the temperature removed from the request's identity.

### S-8.2 — Experiment design
Depends: S-8.1
AC:
- Translates a hypothesis into a concrete experiment specification
- Specification is validated against the chosen primitive's schema
- Mechanical step — routed to the mid tier with cascade

**DONE (2026-08-15)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/design.py` and `schema.py`, ADR 085, 49 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_design.py`. All three AC met, and **the mirror of S-8.1 in every respect that matters**: that story is creative, frontier-only and structurally unable to request a cascade because nothing can check it; this one is mechanical, mid-tier and cascaded because AC 2 hands it the check. **AC 3 required a ninth row in `04-cost.md` §3, and there was no way to avoid it**: `Step.step_class` is *derived* from that table (S-5.5, so a call site cannot misdeclare) and `cascade()` refuses any step type whose check is `None` (S-5.6), so a step with no row has no derivable class and cannot be routed or cascaded at all. Borrowing the closest existing row would let a row about *one* primitive decide the tier for a step serving all thirteen. The check is what AC 2 states, and it is the same **kind** of check as the evidence chain's already-present row — a schema over an artifact, no judgement in it — which is the argument for the cascade-safe side rather than a convenience. §3 and `STEP_KINDS` were updated together, and **the non-negotiable is re-asserted from the story that touched the table**, since editing §3's rows is precisely how the two *none exists* entries would stop being *none exists* without anybody deciding to. **The story's substance is what a primitive's schema turns out to be.** `scale_volume` takes nine parameters and a model can answer three — `scales`, `distribution`, `counters`; the other six are the grounded workload — so **a specification is not a call, it is the answerable half of one**, and the partition falls out of annotations that were already written: a parameter whose type a JSON document can express is the model's, everything else is the harness's. **Nothing had to be added to any primitive**, which is the test of whether a partition is real, and it holds across all thirteen instruments by sweep rather than by assertion on one. Read from the callable, which is S-3.1's `Primitive.signature` argument one layer on and worse here because the reader is not a human. **A mapping of numbers is never the design's to set**: the registry's one `Mapping[str, float]` is `bounds.headroom(metrics=…)`, the shape of a *measurement*, and admitting it would defeat *agents do not report measurements* through the front door. **The schema checks shape, not sense, and the bound is stated in code** — `scales=[-4]` is a well-typed `Sequence[int]` and a meaningless sweep, refused by `scale_volume` on its own rules — so *validated against the schema* means the primitive will accept the call and **never** that the experiment will produce a measurement. **The second finding: a retry has to be able to differ from the attempt it retries.** §3's *2 cheap attempts, then strong* silently assumes the second attempt is a second answer, and at temperature 0 it is the same call — same model, same prompt, same sampling — which against S-0.7b's client is literally the same digest, replaying the recording already rejected. So the rejection is fed back into the question and the temperature stays 0.0: **a retry told what was wrong is a correction, a retry at a higher temperature is a dice roll.** The rejections go **last**, because §4 puts the varying part last and this is the one step designed to make three calls. That line also decides what is retryable: a malformed reply or a schema failure is a *wrong answer* and cascades; a refusal or a truncation is an *absent* one and raises. `UndesignableError` carries every attempt's rejection, because `NoDearerTierError` reports the step type and the model and **not what was wrong**, which here is the whole diagnosis. **There is no `validate` parameter, which is S-8.1's absence inverted** — that story's danger was a caller requesting a forbidden cascade, this one's is a caller supplying a check that returns `True` and makes the cascade decorative. Fourth instance of the construction. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-seven properties, all caught — after two survived, and one was a defect in this story's own claim.** The mapping exclusion survived because the decoy could not discriminate: adding `Mapping` to the specifiable origins changed nothing, since a mapping has two type arguments and the *element* check rejects it for that instead — sixth consecutive story to record a fixture that could not discriminate, and its sharpest form yet, **a rule that can only be broken by two simultaneous edits cannot be tested by one**. And **`strict=True` was guarding nothing while the docstring said it was**: turning it off changed no behaviour, because `JSONValue` covers every type JSON has and there is nothing for a coercion to reach for — what keeps a boolean a boolean is `bool` being *in* the union, and that is the sabotage that fails. The claim was corrected rather than the setting removed, because a guarantee resting on something that is not providing it is worse than no claim: it is the one nobody re-checks.

### S-8.3 — Result interpretation
Depends: S-8.2
AC:
- Separate call at temperature 0.0
- Returns verdict: confirmed / narrowed / rejected, with the measurement attached
- A test asserts identical inputs produce identical verdicts across repeated calls

Notes: the split temperature is a real design decision. Hypothesis generation benefits from diversity; interpretation must not vary.

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/interpretation.py` plus `replies.py`, ADR 086, 39 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_interpretation.py` and 9 in `test_replies.py`. All three AC met. **This needed a tenth row in `04-cost.md` §3, and unlike S-8.2's it is a documented omission rather than an argument from the loop's shape**: §2 — the section whose whole thesis is *route by step, not by agent* — lists **"Interpret a growth table | Diagnostician | ~40"** in its *mechanical* table, which makes it the most frequent step the Diagnostician takes and the largest single mechanical workload in the system, and §3 gives it no row. Since the class is derived from §3, the one step §2's argument is most about was the one step §3 could not express, and it would have run creative on the frontier — the exact drift §2 exists to prevent. **Two rows added by one epic is worth stating plainly: §3 is not an enumeration of the loop's steps**; it was written against the agents that existed then, and Epic 8 is the first code to walk the whole investigate loop. A test asserts from *both* stories that the two *none exists* rows are untouched. **The check that makes it cascade-safe is `CLAUDE.md`'s first two non-negotiables made mechanical**: the verdict carries the figures it rests on, every one is checked against the mapping the harness took, and a name that was not measured, a value that does not match, or a verdict citing nothing are all refused — *confirmed, queries grew 40x* against a flat table fails. **Exact, with no tolerance**, because a tolerance would be this module deciding how far a quoted figure may sit from the measured one, which is a judgement and the point is that the check contains none. **The attached measurement is the harness's structurally** — filled from the mapping the function was handed, with a `measurement` key in the reply never read — and **the citation check runs twice deliberately**: in `parse` to give the cascade a correctable sentence, and in a model validator so no other code path can build one without, since *enforced by schema, not by prompt* would be false if it lived only in the one function that calls it today. **The bound is stated: it catches fabrication, not misjudgement**, and S-5.6's `never_escalated()` is what tells those apart in practice. **AC 3's obvious test is worthless and the story's real work is upstream of it.** Against S-0.7b's replaying client, *identical inputs give identical verdicts* is trivially true — one digest, one recording, one verdict — and would pass against code that rendered the measurement in a different order every call. It tests the cache. **Temperature 0 makes an answer stable for a *fixed prompt* and says nothing about a prompt that moves**, so the property is that identical inputs produce an identical **request**: the measurement is rendered in sorted key order (a `Mapping` iterates in insertion order, so the same measurement built two ways would be two prompts) with values through `json.dumps`, and the tests compare a byte-identical question and a **request digest agreeing across a process boundary** (S-8.4's construction — hash-order randomization only has room to move between processes). The end-to-end test asserts the two calls reached the *same recording*, and says in its docstring that the digest is the assertion and the verdict is not. **`replies.py` was extracted as the second case** — the reason to share is the *rejection text*, since those sentences are fed back to the model as corrections and two hand-written versions would drift; **S-8.1 is deliberately not a third caller**, because it raises where the mechanical steps return and ADR 085's line between a wrong answer and an absent one is what that difference is. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-two properties, all caught — after three survived, of three different kinds.** (1) *A weak assertion*: the all-problems-at-once test checked for `"db.query"` and passed against a first-problem-only implementation, because the *unmeasured metric* message lists everything that **was** measured and `db.query` is in that list — an assertion matching a different part of the same output is not an assertion about the part it names. (2) *Two guards no test reached*, both in the newly extracted module, both previously sabotaged only through their callers, and neither reachable by any reply either caller's suite sends — a truncated reply has no closing brace, so the malformed-JSON path had never executed. (3) **One of those guards could not fire at all**: the *that was not an object* rejection shipped in S-8.2 is unreachable, since the pattern takes text from the first `{` to the last `}` and JSON beginning with `{` that parses is an object. That makes it S-7.4's redundant condition — unverifiable by construction, reading as protection while protecting nothing — and S-7.4's recorded remedy is the one applied: collapse it, and verify the intent from the other side with a parametrised sweep, keeping an object nested in an array as the control.

### S-8.4 — Append-only experiment log
Depends: S-6.1, S-5.7
AC:
- Every experiment appended with hypothesis, primitive, design, measurement, verdict
- Never reordered or re-summarized
- Serialization is stable and cache-friendly

**DONE (2026-08-14)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/log.py`, ADR 083, 30 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_experiment_log.py`. **Taken before S-8.1 with Allen's agreement**, because S-8.1's AC says it *receives the experiment log* and this story defines what one is — the project's own precedent of not guessing another story's schema (S-5.4 left the checkpoint to S-6.1, S-6.1 left this log to S-8.4, S-6.2 left playbook columns to S-13.1). All three AC met, and **most of the work was not duplicating what already exists**: S-5.7's `Investigation` has an `append` and no `reorder`, and **S-5.8's `PrunedLog` already holds records, renders summaries, defers detail and guarantees the byte-prefix property**. **Epic 5's composition found *two append-only logs* as a defect**, and recorded why it was invisible — caching is a prefix match, so a log wrong in *content* but still append-only reports full cache hits and a rising bill with nothing failing — so `ExperimentLog` **wraps** `PrunedLog`: one log, one rendering, one way in. What S-8.4 adds is the **record**, since S-5.8 holds the cost view `(primitive, target, outcome, detail)` and AC 1 needs hypothesis, design and measurement too — S-8.6 assembles an evidence chain from these, and a chain built from records missing their measurements is exactly the *conclusion drawn from reading code* the first non-negotiable prevents. **A defect was found by running it: the artifact must be validated BEFORE the summary is taken.** The first version appended to the pruned log, took its index, then built the `Experiment` — and **an append-only log cannot retract an entry**, so a record passing S-5.8's rules and failing AC 1's left a summary in the *rendered prompt* with no experiment behind it (`artifacts: 0, pruned records: 1`), with nothing raising at the point the two collections stopped agreeing. Order is now build-then-append, and **every way of being refused is parametrised**, because the two collections are validated by different rules and only some fired first. **Append-only is an absence** — no `reorder`, `summarize`, `replace`, `forget` or `truncate`, asserted by inspection so it fails the moment somebody adds one for a demo — and `experiments` returns a tuple so a caller sorting what it was given is not sorting the log. **"Cache-friendly" is tested as the property the cache needs**: the render at N is a **byte prefix** of the render at N+1. The **digest test runs in a fresh interpreter**, because the guarantee a digest has is that a second process agrees — S-4.1 recorded a sabotage walking through a digest test that built the model twice in one process. `Verdict` is defined here because the log stores it; **`NARROWED` is kept separate** since collapsing it into `REJECTED` throws away the half of the search space the experiment bought. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught, no survivors** — including the ordering defect, which is now a sabotage in its own right.

### S-8.5 — Conditional exclusions
Depends: S-8.4
Why: an exclusion recorded as fact permanently blocks the correct hypothesis.
AC:
- Every exclusion records its preconditions: fixture shape, platform, concurrency, scales tested
- When a later experiment changes a condition, affected exclusions are surfaced as stale
- The agent may re-test a stale exclusion
- A test proves a uniform-fixture exclusion is reopened when skewed fixtures are introduced

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/exclusions.py`, ADR 087, 36 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_exclusions.py`. All four AC met. **`invalidated_if` is derived, not stored, and that is the one departure from F3's sketch**: the sketch carries `conditions` and `invalidated_if` as two fields and they are two statements of one fact — an exclusion is invalid exactly when a condition it was established under no longer holds — so staleness is **computed on every read** (S-7.12's argument for refusing an override flag beside an override value; a stored flag is also wrong for exactly as long as nobody recomputes it). **The four conditions are two kinds, and that is the story's substance.** *Categorical* (fixture shape, platform) covers by membership, and multi-valued is ordinary rather than hypothetical since S-3.3's `compare_shapes` sweeps all three distributions in one experiment. *Numeric* (concurrency, scale) covers by **envelope**, min to max — an exclusion that saw 10, 100 and 1000 covers 500 and does not cover 10 000 — and **the asymmetry is the model rather than a convenience**: a defect invisible at 10/100/1000 but present at 500 would have to be non-monotonic, while one appearing past 1000 is an ordinary threshold (a cache that stops fitting, a page that splits, an index the planner abandons). Treating scales as a set would reopen everything on every intermediate point, which is Epic 4's *a caveat attached to everything is one nobody reads* with the opposite sign. The kind is a property of the **dimension**, declared once, so one exclusion cannot treat scale as categorical while another treats it as a range. **All four are required, which is AC 1 read literally** — and it also removes a tri-state, because with an optional dimension a later experiment reporting a condition the exclusion never recorded is neither a change nor a non-change (S-3.1's lesson). **An exclusion can only be made from a rejected `Experiment`**, so *no finding without a measurement* comes free and there is no constructor taking a sentence; a confirmed experiment is a finding and a narrowed one is a hypothesis that survived, and an exclusion built from either would tell the agent a live branch was closed. **`reopen` refuses a LIVE exclusion, which AC 3 does not ask for**: F3 names the danger of an exclusion treated as permanent fact, and fixing it creates the opposite one — an agent setting aside an inconvenient result by calling it stale — so a reopening must be something the recorded conditions justify, and the refusal says what would have to change. **Two bounds are stated in code rather than implied**: nothing here decides whether a *new* hypothesis is the same as an excluded one (a semantic judgement with no deterministic check, so a live exclusion blocks by being rendered as settled and not by any refusal), and `RESIDUE` says in words that four dimensions are modelled — an experiment varying a fifth produces an exclusion that **looks fully conditioned and is not**. **`render()` returns sentences, closing S-8.1's open note** that it took `exclusions: Sequence[str]` because *S-8.5 owns what an exclusion is*; the structure now exists and still hands over sentences, asserted by feeding S-8.1's `render_question`. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-two properties, all caught — after two survived and one sabotage was a no-op I wrote badly.** The pair that matters was caught from the start and is why every staleness test here has a control beside it: *nothing is ever stale* restores F3 exactly, and *everything is always stale* passes AC 2 and AC 4 while making every exclusion worthless. **The survivor worth recording is the second instance in two stories of one shape**: the AC 4 test asserted `"fixture shape" in describe(skewed)` and the **settled half of that same string already says *under fixture shape uniform***, so a rendering naming no drifted condition passed — re-anchored on *established at … went to …*. S-8.3's survivor was identical one module earlier, which makes it a habit rather than an incident: **when asserting that a rendering mentions X, check X does not already appear elsewhere in it.** The no-op sabotage (*fixture shape stops being a condition*, written as a comment change) exposed a real gap when rewritten properly — no test had covered all four dimensions individually, and one is now parametrised so none can quietly stop counting.

### S-8.6 — Evidence chain assembly
Depends: S-8.5
AC:
- Pydantic model requiring: symptom, exclusions, localization, mechanism, complexity, site, context, confidence
- **Every localization link requires an attached measurement** — schema rejects otherwise
- Context files each carry the reason they were implicated
- Golden-file test for serialization

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/chain.py`, ADR 088, 44 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_chain.py` plus the golden file at `tests/diagnosis/golden/evidence_chain.json`. All four AC met. **Two of §2.4's lines turned out to be the story.** (1) **`exclusions` holds S-8.5's `Exclusion`, not a bare experiment.** §2.4 sketches an exclusion as `{hypothesis, primitive, measurement, verdict}` — with **no conditions** — and F3 is the finding that an exclusion recorded as fact permanently blocks the correct hypothesis. S-8.5 fixed that *inside the investigation*; flattening it back here would reintroduce F3 at the boundary where it does most damage, because §2.4 calls this artifact *the body of the eventual pull request*. **"Not the database, queries flat at 7, 7, 7" printed in a PR with no mention of the uniform fixtures is F3's false fact with a reviewer's signature under it**, so `render()` prints the conditions beside the claim and a test asserts it. (2) **`confidence` is derived, required and recomputed — S-7.9's construction.** §2.4 says *derived from number of independent confirmations* and `03-agents.md` §4.4 writes a bare `float`; the authority map gives artifact schemas to §2.4. A confidence an agent writes is a number nobody measured, but a bare property would not survive serialization — and this artifact is serialized on purpose, travels to two agents and becomes a PR — so the copy is **mandatory and powerless**: validating recomputes it and refuses disagreement, and `assemble` has no `confidence` parameter at all. **Independence is counted in distinct primitives** (two runs of one instrument are one kind of evidence; scaling and ablation agreeing is S-8.7's thesis behaviour showing up in the number), and **exclusions deliberately do not raise it**, since counting them would let an agent lift its own figure by excluding things nobody suspected. **It is not a probability and two properties keep that honest**: `1 - 2**-k` is a *model* (each independent instrument halves the remaining doubt), it can never reach 1 so no chain claims certainty, and the report says in words that it is a count on a 0–1 scale — `00-BRIEF.md` §6's diagnostic agreement across ten runs stays the project's actual reliability number. **AC 2 is structural rather than a validator**: a link holds an `Experiment` and S-8.4 already refuses one with an empty measurement, so there is **no `measurement` field to leave blank** (asserted by inspection, with the active attempt made one layer down). `share_of_cost` is bounded to a fraction and requires a stated `basis` — `Bound.basis`'s construction — and the schema says it cannot check the arithmetic. **`RESIDUE` travels into the rendered report**: this proves every link carries a measurement and that the confidence matches, and **does not** check that the mechanism follows, which `08-audit.md` names as the separate flaw the finding audit exists for. **A defect in S-8.5 was found by serializing one**: numeric conditions were stored as Python `int` under a field annotated `str | float`, and pydantic's union serializer said so — a stored type disagreeing with its annotation is a golden file waiting to move; `_canonical` now converts. **Sabotage-verified on twenty-six properties, all caught — after five survived in two distinct groups.** (a) **Two redundant conditions, S-7.4's shape for the third and fourth time this epic**: `min_length=1` on `localization` cannot change an outcome because *at least one confirming link* strictly implies *at least one link*, and `min_length=1` on `Implicated.reason` cannot because *not whitespace* implies *not empty*. Both collapsed, leaving the stronger check — and the surviving refusal is the better sentence. **Habit worth stating: when two checks sit beside each other, ask whether one implies the other before writing a test for both.** (b) **Three genuinely untested guards, found only because the redundant pair forced a second pass** — `mechanism`, `scope` and `metric` could all be emptied with every test passing while their neighbours `basis` and `reason` were covered. **That asymmetry is the tell: a field is not tested because it is important, it is tested because somebody happened to think of it**; they are now swept in one parametrised test over every string a reader depends on.

### S-8.7 — Instrument switching — **the thesis behaviour**
Depends: S-8.1, S-3.1
AC:
- On a rejected hypothesis, the next hypothesis must select a different primitive where the evidence supports it
- Demonstrated end to end: a repo where query count is flat, the agent concludes "not the database," switches to ablation, and localizes the real cause
- The switch and its rationale appear in the experiment log

Notes: this is the demo that justifies the entire architecture. Record it as a video when it first works.

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/loop.py`, `rationale` added to S-8.4's `Experiment`, `switches()`/`describe_switches()` on `ExperimentLog`, the ablatable subject at `tests/fixtures/planted/rendering.py`, ADR 089, 15 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_switching.py`. All three AC met. **AC 1 is a code-enforced property, not an expectation of the model**: *the next hypothesis must select a different primitive* is a rule about the system, and `CLAUDE.md`'s hard-enforcement table says such a rule lives in code — so a hypothesis re-proposing an instrument already rejected under unchanged conditions is refused and re-asked, and after three repeats the loop raises `NoNewInstrumentError`, **written as a result rather than a fault** (the honest reading is *this subject's applicable experiments are exhausted*, which §9 ships as an answer). **Re-asking is not cascading and the distinction is the non-negotiable**: `generate` is called again at the same temperature on the same tier with a longer exclusion list, no validator is supplied, and what makes it legitimate is that the thing corrected is not the hypothesis's *quality* — which nothing can judge — but a fact the agent can be told, that this instrument has already answered. A test asserts `validate` appears nowhere in `propose`. **"Where the evidence supports it" is S-8.5's rule, not a new one**: the loop asks the register which instruments are settled *under the conditions in force*, so a reseed reopens one without this module knowing reseeding exists. **AC 3 required a field**: `Experiment` stored the primitive and not why it was chosen, and the primitive alone shows *that* the instrument changed while **the thesis claim is about the choosing** — so `rationale` (which S-8.1 already produced and was discarding) is now required, not defaulted. `switches()` is a **view, not a record**, the shape S-8.5 refused for `invalidated_if` and S-8.6 for `confidence`; `describe_switches()` names the provoking verdict, because switching after a rejection and after a confirmation are different behaviours and only the first is claimed. **What the demo proves is bounded in writing, because this is the story where over-claiming would be easiest.** The measurements are **real** — `scaling.volume` and `ablation.stub` execute against a planted defect, two queries at every scale and stubbing the renderer removes essentially all the wall time — and the subject is not a new defect but `render_with_expensive_downstream` expressed as a collaborator so an instrument can interpose (the shape S-3.4's docstring says it needs and no real subject had provided). **The model calls are replayed**, so the test proves the rejection propagates, the harness refuses a repeat and the log records the switch — and it does **not** prove a model would choose to switch unprompted. `run_investigation` takes the client as a parameter for exactly that reason: **the video runs this same function against `AnthropicClient`**, since a demo of a second implementation would be a demo of the second implementation. **The measurement reaching the model is counts and a rounded share**, because a prompt carrying `8.2447281639…` differs on every run and an end-to-end replay over raw timings is impossible in principle; counts reproduce to the integer (ADR 052) and the timings travel in `detail`. **The loop holds no budget** (`max_steps` is a loop guard against a `while True` around a paid API; S-8.9 owns caps) **and assembles no chain** (S-8.6 needs a symptom, mechanism, site and implicated files that the loop never measured). **Sabotage: the FIRST PASS WAS WORTHLESS AND REPORTED A CLEAN SWEEP OF NINETEEN** — the runner passed `--timeout=120` to a pytest without `pytest-timeout`, so every run exited non-zero on an argument error and every sabotage read as caught. The tell was present throughout: every "caught" line had an **empty `by:`**. Third instance of this class after Epic 5's `git checkout` and Epic 6's marker filter; the runner now **fails loudly on a non-zero exit with no parsed failure** and checks the baseline at both ends. **The real pass: nineteen properties, all caught after four survived, and all four were untested properties rather than weak code** — the rationale dropped on its way into the log (AC 3 was asserted everywhere except through the loop that has to satisfy it), the loop treating a narrowed verdict as confirmed and the loop unbounded (neither path reachable with a defect that confirms on step two, so both needed a synthetic run), and **`switches()` reporting every consecutive pair** — the fixture had two experiments with two different primitives, so *every pair* and *every changed pair* were the same list, the sixth instance of a fixture that could not discriminate.

### S-8.8 — Reseed tool
Depends: S-7.7
AC:
- The Diagnostician can request new fixtures with a specified shape
- Reseeding invalidates affected exclusions per S-8.5
- Cost of reseeding counted against the experiment budget

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/reseed.py` plus `Investigation.reseed`, ADR 090, 17 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_reseed.py`. All three AC met, and **almost everything needed already existed**: S-7.7 builds a fixture at a chosen shape, S-8.5 decides what a changed condition reopens, S-5.4 has both halves of a step cap. What was missing was the doorway — and the guards that stop it being a way around the other two. **This is the other half of S-8.5**: that story made an exclusion conditional so it *could* be reopened, and until now nothing moved a condition on purpose, so **`Conditions` changed only when somebody rebuilt the world by hand and *may be reopened* was a property nothing exercised**. The composition test asserts it through `Investigation` rather than through the function alone, because AC 1 is *the Diagnostician can request new fixtures* and a capability reachable only from a helper would satisfy the criterion without closing the gap (Epic 7's finding restated: *the criterion is met* and *the criterion is reachable* are different claims). **Two guards the AC do not ask for.** (1) **A reseed that would move no condition is refused** — it reopens nothing, establishes nothing, and costs one of the forty experiments; this is S-8.5's `reopen` guard pointing the other way, and the control matters as much, since reseeding to the same shape at a **wider scale** does move a condition and refusing it would make the guard a prohibition on reseeding at all. (2) **The conditions change only after the seeding succeeded**, which is the whole correctness argument: adopting them first and failing second would reopen every exclusion mentioning the old shape **against a fixture that was never built**, and the agent would re-run an experiment believing the world had changed, get the same answer and record it as new evidence — **worse than the gap this story closes, because it manufactures a reason to disbelieve a correct exclusion** (S-8.4's build-then-append finding one module over). **AC 3 needed no new machinery**: `authorize(phase, finding_id)` with a zero worst-case is already a pure step-cap check and S-5.4's argument for calling it first applies unchanged; a failed reseed is **not** charged, following `Session.run`'s precedent, which is safe only because the refusal propagates and nothing retries a broken seeder in a loop. **The recorded conclusion carries the recipe digest**, which a sabotage proved load-bearing — S-5.4 escalates a phase whose last three steps concluded the same thing, so a reseed always reporting `"reseed"` would stall the investigation on its third genuinely different fixture. **Who decides to reseed is not settled here** (E12's, the same line S-8.7 drew), and this module **seeds nothing itself** — S-7.6 and S-7.7 own that. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties, all caught, after two survived and one run hit a harness fault.** **The harness-fault detector added in S-8.7 paid for itself one story later**: a sabotage produced an unparseable file, so pytest exited non-zero with no failure line — which under the old runner would have read as a clean catch — and the `!!! HARNESS FAULT` branch reported it instead. The survivors: **dropping the carried-forward concurrency to the literal `1` changed nothing because every fixture in the file ran at concurrency 1** (seventh instance of a fixture that could not discriminate; a reseed during a load experiment must not quietly reset the recorded load to serial), and **the stall test performed two reseeds where `DEFAULT_STALL_AFTER` is three**, so it could not distinguish a per-fixture conclusion from a constant one — it now runs three different fixtures and asserts no stall, **with a control running the same recipe three times and asserting one**, because a conclusion that never repeats makes the stall check unreachable, which is the opposite defect and just as silent.

### S-8.9 — Budget and progress
Depends: S-5.4
AC:
- 40-experiment cap
- Escalates after 8 experiments with no narrowing
- On exhaustion, emits a partial chain containing the exclusions — a proven negative is a result

**DONE (2026-08-16)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/progress.py`, `Investigation.partial_chain`, ADR 091, 21 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_progress.py` plus 3 in `test_switching.py`. All three AC met. **AC 1's cap existed since S-5.4 — and running a loop against it found it was a THIRD of its size.** `Session.run` called `record_step` once per **model call**, and the loop makes three calls per experiment, so the forty-experiment cap was a **thirteen-experiment cap**. S-5.4's own docstring predicts it word for word: *§12.1 budgets 120 model calls per finding against a cap of 40 experiments — so an experiment is about three calls, and a cap counted in calls would halt investigation at a third of its intended budget.* It survived because nothing ran a whole loop against it: **Epic 5's composition test drove one `session.run` per iteration, which made a call and an experiment look like the same thing — the test that should have caught it encoded it instead.** `Session.run` now records a step only where the phase's cap is counted in steps; a phase counted in experiments, attempts or rounds has its unit counted by whoever owns it. Two Epic 5 composition tests were updated with the reason recorded in them. **AC 2 needed a number the default gets wrong**: §4.5 puts the investigate check at 8 and `DEFAULT_STALL_AFTER` is 3, so S-7.10's rule applies — **a budget with the wrong value is refused, not corrected**. S-7.10 also wrote in a comment that three *is* right for an investigation; **that comment is now corrected**, since every phase that has looked has needed its own value. `Session` gained a `stall_after` pass-through, because it is the only thing that constructs a `Budget` and a phase had no way to ask. **"No narrowing" is decided by the harness** (S-5.4's F6 rule) and **a rejection is not narrowing** — §2.2: *reject → new hypothesis informed by the exclusion; narrow → one level deeper*. **AC 3 is the story: a partial chain is a separate artifact with the OPPOSITE requirement.** `EvidenceChain` requires ≥1 confirming link; relaxing that so it could represent an investigation confirming nothing would destroy the guarantee S-8.6 exists for — a chain would stop meaning *a cause was established*. So `PartialChain` **refuses to hold a confirmation**, the two partition, and neither can impersonate the other; one carrying a confirmation would report an established finding as an absent one, which **loses a result** rather than mis-typing one. **It has no `mechanism`, `site` or `confidence`, and their absence is the artifact's meaning** — those are claims a stopped investigation cannot make, and a field for one is somewhere a reader could put a guess. Empty exclusions are legitimate (forty narrowings exclude nothing while learning something), so the rendering says which case it is. **Stopping is not failing**: `Stopped` has three members because a reader's next action differs for each, each carries §7.2's disposition, `run_investigation` returns rather than raises, and **`max_steps` is gone** — there is now exactly one number that stops the loop, the forty `04-cost.md` costed. **A euro-ceiling breach still propagates**, since S-5.4 says *the halt is the global ceiling's alone* and reporting one as *the experiment cap was reached* would be false about why the run ended. **Sabotage-verified on seventeen properties, all caught — after five survived, EVERY ONE in the loop wiring rather than in the module.** The unit tests exercised `progress.py` thoroughly and the loop's use of it not at all — the same asymmetry S-8.7 hit, *a criterion asserted where it is defined and not where it has to hold*. **Two of the five were not test gaps but code that could not matter**: the loop's `authorize` before each step (removing it changed nothing, because `Session.run` authorizes inside its first attempt against the same cap — S-7.4's redundant condition, **the fifth in Epic 8**), and `Investigation.confirmed`, a property with **no caller at all** duplicating what `PartialChain`'s validator refuses. Both deleted.

---

**EPIC 8 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-16)** — `tests/diagnosis/test_diagnosis_composed.py`, `src/coldfix/diagnosis/emit.py`, ADR 092, 13 tests. The epic finished with nine stories, **295 passing tests**, a sabotage pass on every one — and **no way to take a screened workload, investigate it, and emit an evidence chain**. Epic 7's finding, repeated almost exactly: **three defects, and every one was a join**; not one was a module wrong about its own subject. (1) **Two append-only logs, again — this time across an epic boundary.** `Session` constructs a `PrunedLog` and renders it into the block §4 caches; `ExperimentLog` wraps its own; nothing joined them, so **the session's log block rendered empty forever** while the real log rode in the uncached question. Measured before the fix: two experiments in the loop's log, **zero records in the session's**, a 208-character block containing only S-5.8's retrieval notice. Epic 5 found this *inside its own epic* and recorded why it is silent — caching is a prefix match, so a log wrong in content is still append-only and still reports hits — and what it costs is the entire growing part of the prompt at full price against a cost model assuming 85% cached, plus S-5.8's pruning report (where §5's 60–80% claim is measured) measuring an empty log. **S-8.4 exposed `.pruned` for exactly this join and nothing used it.** (2) **The conditions and the symptom had no producer.** Every caller built them by hand **including every test, which is precisely why nothing noticed**; `Workload` already carries `fixture.distribution` (the shape actually seeded) and `observations` (the scales actually driven). A hand-built `Conditions` can say `uniform` while the recipe says `long_tail`, and **an exclusion recorded under a shape that was never used is permanently and wrongly live** — F3 reintroduced at the join S-8.5 exists to close. (3) **S-8.6 was unreachable**: a confirmed investigation had no path to the artifact the epic exists to produce, so `EvidenceChain` could be built by hand in a test and by nothing in the system — Epic 7's *AC satisfied in isolation and unreachable in practice*, and **the more dangerous half, because the criterion reads as met**. `chain_from` is that path and deliberately invents no mechanism, site or implicated files, and refuses a confirming experiment with no measured share. **ONE DEFECT FOUND AND NOT FIXED, recorded so nobody rediscovers it or quotes §12.3's cost as achieved**: `Session.run` assembles S-5.7's blocks and **the request never carries them** — each diagnosis module builds its own `messages=[{"role": "user", "content": question}]`, so the `cache_control` breakpoints are never sent and **Epic 5's entire prompt-caching design is inert**. Not fixed here because it touches S-0.7b's client, `Session.run` and all three call sites, and changes how every request in the system is shaped; it belongs in its own story with its own sabotage pass. **The harness moved to `tests/fixtures/thesis.py`**, because two suites need the planted subject and a test module importing another test module is a source file mypy sees under two names. **The `query_counter` fixture registers under a name different from its function's**, so importing it does not read as a redefinition of the parameter using it — the obvious route, a `tests/diagnosis/conftest.py`, collides with `tests/sandbox/conftest.py` under mypy. **This project had already recorded that hazard and this session walked into it anyway**, which is the lesson worth keeping: a recorded hazard only helps if it is read *before* the obvious thing is tried. **Sabotage-verified on ten properties, all caught after one survived** — hardcoding `"uniform"` in `conditions_for` changed nothing because the subject's fixture **is** uniform, **the eighth instance of a fixture where the right answer and the wrong answer coincide**; the test is now parametrised over every `Distribution`. Worth carrying into Epic 9: **restoring defect 1 is caught by two tests and neither is about logging** — they are about what the prompt contains and what the run reports, and *a defect whose only symptom is a cost figure needs a test that reads the cost figure*.

---

# EPIC 9 — Finding audit

**Goal:** audit the diagnosis before any repair spend. **Build before the Surgeon.**

**Why this epic exists:** the patch audit checks equivalence, cheats, trades and scope. If the diagnosis is wrong, all of those pass — a correct fix to a non-problem is equivalent, is not a cheat, trades nothing, and breaks no callers.

### S-9.1 — Finding-audit invocation
Depends: S-8.6
AC:
- Adversary role invoked with the **raw experiment log**, not the assembled evidence chain
- Separate message list constructed fresh — no Diagnostician reasoning included
- Different model vendor where configured

Notes: giving it the raw log rather than the chain reduces framing inheritance. Isolation is partial, not clean — document it as such.

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/invocation.py`, ADR 093, 21 tests in `tests/audit/test_invocation.py`. All three AC met. **AC 1 and AC 2 contradict each other, and S-8.7 is why**: AC 1 says hand over the raw log, AC 2 says include no Diagnostician reasoning, and **S-8.7 added `rationale` to every log record** — *why this instrument was worth its cost*, written by the Diagnostician, sitting in the raw log. Handing it over verbatim satisfies AC 1 by breaking AC 2. Neither criterion is wrong; they were written before the field existed, and this is the most direct instance yet of composing two stories exposing a conflict neither could see alone. **`08-audit.md` decides it and supplies the number**: isolation *removes the explicit rationalization, which is the documented risk — **72% of reward-hacking episodes carry explicit justifying reasoning***. So `render_evidence` withholds **`rationale`** (prose whose purpose is to justify a choice) and **`outcome`** (the agent's gloss on its own measurement — *"queries flat at 7, 7, 7 — this is clearly not the database"*), and keeps what was tested, what ran, how it was configured and what was measured. **`verdict` stays and that is a decision**: it is a three-valued classification S-8.3 ties to cited measurements rather than prose, and an auditor asked whether an exclusion was adequate must know something *was* excluded — the opposite failure, *isolation by sending nothing*, is a sabotage in its own right because it would satisfy AC 2 perfectly while making the audit useless. **The evidence is rendered from the `Experiment` artifacts, not from S-5.8's summaries**, because those are composed from `outcome` — and removing a field from rendered prose would be editing text to enforce a boundary, which is exactly what `CLAUDE.md` says must not be how this holds. **The audit gets its own `Session`, because a shared one leaks silently**: `Session` caches one assembled prompt per model carrying its owner's system text, playbook and source as a cached prefix, so an audit billed through the Diagnostician's session inherits all of it — **isolation undone by the object it was billed through while every message list stayed clean**. `refuse_shared_session` refuses anything else, checked **before** any spend. Its playbook is deliberately empty, since an auditor reasoning from the investigator's habits inherits exactly the frame this cannot remove and should not add to. **The enforcement is an absence three times over**: `invoke` has no `chain` parameter (AC 1), no `messages`/`history` parameter (AC 2), and no `validate`; `audit_messages` returns a **new list every call**, tested by mutating it. **Routing reuses §3's existing `ATTACK_DESIGN` row** — an audit is an attack, on the diagnosis rather than a patch — so no new row after Epic 8 added two. AC 3 needs no code: the vendor is `Router` configuration and ADR 062 records the second-vendor blocker as indefinite. **`RESIDUE` carries `08-audit.md`'s honest position** — the isolation removes explicit rationalization and **does not remove framing bias**, since the log still records which experiments the Diagnostician thought worth running — asserted word for word including *not describe this as clean separation*. **Sabotage-verified on fifteen properties, all caught, no survivors.** **Deliberately scoping-independent**: Epic 9's shape is under review against S-0.8 (the audit was designed to catch fabrication, which happened **0 times in 60 real-model runs**, while the measured failure was non-termination), and S-9.2–S-9.8 may change — but an isolated auditor working from measurements is needed under any scoping, and its input is already the log rather than the chain, so pointing it at an unfinished investigation is a change to S-9.8's routing rather than to this.

### S-9.2 — Exclusion validity attack
Depends: S-9.1
AC:
- Checks whether ruled-out hypotheses were ruled out under adequate conditions
- Flags exclusions whose preconditions were too narrow
