**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/exclusions.py`, ADR 096, 20 tests in `tests/audit/test_exclusion_validity.py`. Both AC met. **S-8.5's machinery turned on itself**: that story made every exclusion carry the conditions it holds under so it *could* be reopened; this reads them and asks whether they were ever wide enough to establish anything. **The second attack that turns out to be arithmetic** — which axes were varied is computable from `Conditions`, so two of Epic 9's seven attacks need no model, worth noting because the epic's framing (*attacks*, *the Adversary*) reads as adversary calls throughout. **What it cannot do is named rather than glossed over**: it sees which axes were unvaried and cannot judge whether an unvaried axis was *relevant to this hypothesis* — that is S-9.5's — so every objection is phrased as **what was not varied** and the report says in words that the relevance judgement is not being made. **Uniform-only is a sharper objection than single-shape, and the asymmetry is PROVED**: S-3.3 exists because `Σ k²` is minimized exactly when every parent is equal, so for **any** per-parent cost the uniform fixture is the *provably blindest* one — F3's worked example, attacked. Long-tail-only is also single-shape but is the deliberate worst case, so it gets the weaker objection; collapsing the two would tell a reader who used the hardest fixture that they used the blindest. **Actionable and inherent narrowness are reported separately**: uniform-only is reopened by S-8.8's reseed *and the remedy text says so*, closing the loop S-8.5 opened; a **single platform** is none of those — demanding a second architecture is not a remedy anybody can apply — so it is recorded as a bound and does **not** make an exclusion inadequate, which is `00-BRIEF.md`'s *exclusions carry their preconditions* read as a reporting rule. **The scale axis delegates to S-9.4** rather than asking the same question twice, and **a missing fit is not an objection** — not every rejection came from a sweep, and inventing a fit to judge would be auditing a curve nobody drew (`scales is None` means *not audited*, which S-3.1 distinguishes from *passed*). **The control is the load-bearing half**: an auditor objecting to every exclusion satisfies both AC while making §9's *null results are valid output* unreachable, so *a multi-shape sweep is flagged anyway* and *every run is called serial* are sabotages in their own right. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties, all caught after one survived** — the report's remedy could be deleted without failing anything, because the tests asserted each objection **has** a remedy and never that the rendering **prints** it. **Testing the data and not the rendering is the same gap the Epic 8 composition found in a different place: the reader gets the report, not the enum.**

### S-9.3 — Fixture adequacy attack
Depends: S-9.1
AC:
- Assesses whether fixture shape could have hidden the real cause
- Can request a re-run under different fixture shape

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/fixtures.py`, ADR 097, 13 tests in `tests/audit/test_fixture_adequacy.py`. Both AC met. **AC 2 is the one with teeth**: every audit story before this can only object, and this is the first that can *cause work* — which makes it where ADR 094's warning applies most directly, since an audit whose lever is *run more experiments* worsens the failure S-0.8 measured. **So the capability is split: this module can ASK and cannot DO.** It produces a `ReseedRequest` and has no seeder, no budget and no call into S-8.8; executing goes through `reseed`, which authorizes against the experiment cap **before** it seeds. An auditor that could seed directly would be doing the harness's job and spending budget nobody authorized — from the one component whose whole purpose is scepticism about the run it examines. Asserted by inspection. **A request is only made when it would change something**: S-8.8 refuses a reseed that moves no condition, and this refuses to *ask* for one — two guards on the same waste at different layers, and this is the cheaper one, since S-8.8's refusal arrives after a caller has already spent a round producing an instruction destined for rejection. **Which shape to ask for is derived**: `LONG_TAIL` first because S-3.3 calls it *the deliberate worst case for any per-parent cost*, `POWER_LAW` second as the smooth spectrum a long tail does not cover, `UNIFORM` last because `Σ k²` makes it blindest. **The request changes only the distribution** — same entity, size and source — because `allocate` spends the same total over the same parents, so shape is the only difference; a request that also changed the size would produce a number differing for two reasons. **How it differs from S-9.2**: that audits an *exclusion* (was this ruled out adequately), this audits the *investigation's fixture* against the cause claimed or missed, and produces something executable. They agree uniform is blind because both read the same `Σ k²` proof — two modules agreeing by consulting one argument is not duplication; each holding its own copy would be. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught, no survivors** — the pair that matters being *a request is made even when every shape was swept* against *no request is ever made*. **Two test defects worth recording, both mine**: a control asserted `"could have hidden" not in describe()` and the adequate rendering says *"Nothing ... **could have hidden** a per-parent cost"*, so it failed on the negation — **a negative assertion over formatted text is a substring check, recorded at S-7.11 and walked into again**; and the isolation test asserted `"budget" not in source.lower()` while the docstring discusses the budget at length — **a substring check over source text cannot tell an explanation from an action**, so it now asserts what the module imports and takes.

### S-9.4 — Scale adequacy attack
Depends: S-9.1
AC:
- Checks whether tested scales were large enough to separate linear from superlinear
- Flags fits with poor r² or too few points

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/scales.py`, ADR 095, 20 tests in `tests/audit/test_scales.py`. Both AC met. **Nothing here calls a model, and that is the story's first finding**: S-9.4 sits in an epic of *attacks*, which reads as adversary calls, and `CLAUDE.md` says *do not add a model call where a function would do — counting and curve fitting are code*. A test asserts the module imports no client and no session. **The threshold is DERIVED, not chosen.** The obvious implementation picks *ten times* and cannot say why; the number falls out of two figures already measured here — `SUPERLINEAR_ABOVE` is 1.15 so the gap to resolve is **0.15 wide**, and S-0.4 measured **12% drift**. A power fit is linear in log space, so relative error `e` becomes `e / ln(span)` in the exponent, and requiring that `sigma` times smaller than the gap gives `span >= exp(sigma*e/gap)` = **11.0x** — which is why this project's fixtures sweep 10x and 100x rather than 2x. **The consequence worth having runs the other way**: a harness with S-1.7's *certified* 2% floor needs only **1.5x**, so `required_span` takes the noise as a parameter rather than holding every caller to a constant derived from somebody else's machine. The number that makes it non-arbitrary: **at 12% noise a 2x sweep determines the exponent to +/-0.17 against a 0.15 gap** — it cannot tell the classes apart at all. **Two failures that look alike and are not**: a tight fit over a narrow span is *confidently wrong* (r-squared near 1.0 because few points define a line), a loose fit over a wide span is *honestly uncertain*, and the remedies are **opposite** — widen the sweep vs reduce the noise or add points — so they are separate objections and `describe` states the right remedy for each. **The audit's bar is one point above the instrument's**: S-3.2 needs three to *fit*, this needs four to *check*, because at three there is no point that can be dropped and re-fitted — **an audit whose bar equals the instrument's bar is not auditing anything**; distinct scales are counted, not measurements. **A narrow sweep can still support the weaker claim** — the constant/linear gap is 1.0, not 0.15 — so `resolves_growth` answers per claim rather than per sweep, since refusing outright would throw away the exclusions section 9 ships as answers. **Order matters reading the fit**: S-1.5 nulls `exponent`, `power_r_squared` and `growth` *together*, so checking r-squared first would report *the power law does not describe these measurements* about a power law nobody fitted. **Sabotage-verified on seventeen properties, all caught, no survivors** — the pairing that matters being *a narrow sweep is never flagged* against *every sweep is flagged as narrow*, because an auditor that objects to everything passes every negative test while making the epic a machine for rejecting sound findings.

### S-9.5 — Alternative explanation attack
Depends: S-9.1
AC:
- Proposes a different mechanism consistent with the same measurements
- If one exists and was not excluded, verdict is `unsound`

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/alternatives.py`, ADR 098, 20 tests in `tests/audit/test_alternatives.py`. Both AC met. **The first attack in this epic that genuinely needs a model** — S-9.2, S-9.3 and S-9.4 turned out to be arithmetic, and *is there another story these numbers would also tell* is not computable; `08-audit.md` names it as the flaw schema validation cannot reach (*"no finding without a measurement" prevents fabrication; it does not prevent a correct measurement supporting a wrong conclusion*). **"NO ALTERNATIVE" IS THE DESIGN, not a tolerated edge case.** AC 2 turns **any** alternative into `unsound` and the amended S-9.8 routes `unsound` back to investigate, so **an auditor that cannot say *I have nothing* guarantees an investigation never ends** — S-0.8's measured failure reached through the audit instead of through the agent. So the prompt offers the empty answer explicitly (*that is a result, not a failure*; *the right answer whenever you have to strain to find one*), because a model that must invent the escape hatch will not use it; the parser treats it as ordinary and case-insensitively; and the report says *this attack passing, not this attack failing to run*, because a reader who cannot tell those apart treats a clean finding as an incomplete audit. **The judgement is the model's; the citations are checked** — an alternative quoting a figure nobody measured is a story that happens to mention numbers. **Checked against EVERY experiment, not one, and this is why it does not reuse `check_citations`**: that compares one mapping to one measurement, while a log holds many and the same metric legitimately differs between them (`db.query` is 7 in the sweep that ruled the database out and 1004 in the ablation that found the cause), so reusing it would call one of two real numbers a fabrication. **A case where writing a second checker was correct rather than duplication**, recorded so nobody tidies it away. **"Not excluded" is ARGUED by the auditor, not decided by code**: deciding whether exclusion X covers alternative Y is the same semantic judgement this story exists because code cannot make, so the auditor is shown the rejections (S-9.1's evidence carries verdicts) and must say which fails to cover its proposal — *there might be another explanation* with no account of why the experiments missed it is not an objection anybody can act on. **A malformed answer raises rather than being re-asked**, because `ATTACK_DESIGN` cannot cascade. **Sabotage-verified on sixteen properties, all caught, no survivors** — the pair that matters being *the empty answer stops being recognised* against *every answer reads as no alternative*.

### S-9.6 — Reproducibility check
Depends: S-9.1, S-5.1
AC:
- Re-runs one key experiment, bypassing the replay cache
- Compares against the recorded result
- Material divergence produces `unsound`

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/reproducibility.py`, ADR 099, 13 tests in `tests/audit/test_reproducibility.py`. All three AC met. **The AC hand over one undefined word — *material* — and getting it wrong in either direction breaks something.** Both rules were already written down in `MetricKind`: *a count is exact and reproduces to the integer; a duration here is **one sample***. So a comparator with one rule is wrong one way or the other — **a count that moved at all is material** (ADR 052 makes counts what raises a flag *because* they reproduce exactly, so 7 queries becoming 8 is a different run, not noise), and **a duration that moved is expected**, with only a move beyond S-0.4's floor being evidence. The rule comes from the kind and **the kind is supplied by the primitive that produced the metric** — every Epic 3 result carries a `kinds` mapping — because `seconds_ablated` and `render.calls_baseline` are not distinguishable by spelling and a wrong guess picks the wrong rule silently. **The control is the most important test in this epic**: if a duration moving *within* the floor counted as divergence, every check would fail, every finding would be `unsound`, and the amended S-9.8 would route every investigation back for more experiments **for ever** — ADR 094's hazard reached through the most mechanical attack here. **A metric that vanished is material and is not a small divergence** (silence read as agreement is the S-3.1 failure), and a duration recorded as zero gets its own branch because dividing by it would be arithmetic nobody can check — `relative_change` returns `None` rather than `inf`, since a report carrying `inf` carries a number meaning *undefined*. **Nothing here measures anything**: the re-run arrives as a callable, there is no parameter for a number, and there is no clock in the module. The cache is bypassed with `ReplayMode.OFF`, which S-5.2 built for exactly this. **What it costs is stated rather than hidden** — auditing a `longitudinal.soak` doubles an hours-long experiment, the caller decides, and `CostClass` says in advance what the bill will be. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught, no survivors — but the first pass SKIPPED the most important one on a pattern that did not match.** That is a failure the runner's harness-fault detector does not cover: **a `SKIP` is not a `CAUGHT`, and a pass reporting twelve of thirteen is not a pass.** Re-run individually and caught.

### S-9.7 — Representativeness assessment
Depends: S-9.1
AC:
- Assesses whether the workload resembles something users exercise
- Verdict `unrepresentative` skips to the next finding without repair spend
- Limitation documented: the agent cannot know real traffic patterns

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/representativeness.py`, ADR 100, 12 tests in `tests/audit/test_representativeness.py`. All three AC met, and **AC 3 is not a footnote on this story — it is most of it**: `08-audit.md` states the gap and the limits of its own fix in the same breath (*this is the `unrepresentative` verdict in the finding audit. It is a **partial** fix — the agent still cannot know real traffic patterns*). **THE TWO ERRORS ARE NOT SYMMETRIC, SO THE SAFE ANSWER IS THE DEFAULT.** `unrepresentative` skips straight past repair, so a wrong one **throws away a real finding silently** — nobody sees what was not investigated — while a wrong `representative` wastes effort somebody notices and can undo. So a workload is representative unless there is a **stated reason** it is not, *absence of evidence is not a reason*, and the prompt says which way to lean and why, because a default nobody is told about is a default nobody uses. S-9.5's empty answer inverted. **The default must be reached deliberately**: a parser falling back to `representative` on a malformed answer would make it invisible, and the next reader could not tell a considered *yes* from a shrug — so an unanswerable reply is refused rather than defaulted. **One fact is computable and handed over rather than judged**: S-7.6 records synthesized data as uniform **by construction**, so a schema-seeded workload is known not to resemble a production distribution whatever the endpoint is — a fact about the fixture, not an opinion about the route, and the report keeps the two apart because a reader who cannot tell a measurement from a guess will trust both equally. **`RESIDUE` carries the limitation**: `unrepresentative` means *this does not look like production usage to a reader of the code*, **never** *this is not exercised*; there is no traffic data behind it; the check is a partial fix. The report also says a skipped finding **can be overturned**, because nobody sees a finding that was not investigated and the only person who can notice is the one reading that line. **Consequence recorded so it is not lost**: `08-audit.md` also asks for this limitation in `07-use-cases.md`, which is a customer-facing capability claim and belongs with the others. **Sabotage-verified on thirteen properties, all caught, ZERO SKIPPED** — and the skip count is now printed by the runner rather than left in the scrollback, after S-9.6's pass reported twelve of thirteen and quietly omitted the most important one.

### S-9.8 — Verdict and routing
Depends: S-9.2–S-9.7
AC:
- Verdict schema: `sound` / `unsound` + objection / `unrepresentative` + reason / `negative_sound` / `inconclusive` + what is missing
- `unsound` returns to investigate with the objection in context **only while the investigation has budget left**; with none it escalates
- Cost of the audit is under 15 calls

Notes (**amended 2026-08-17, ADR 094**): the last two verdicts and the budget condition were added after S-0.8. An audit whose only lever is *run more experiments* makes the one failure that spike actually measured — non-termination — worse; and `00-BRIEF.md` §9 makes a null result shippable output, which the original vocabulary had no way to express.

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/verdict.py`, ADR 101, 64 tests in `tests/audit/test_verdict.py`. All three AC met. **Nothing here calls a model either**: six attacks have already answered and combining them is counting, so it is now **five of Epic 9's seven attacks plus the routing decision** that turned out to be arithmetic — worth stating because the epic's own vocabulary (*attacks*, *the Adversary*) prices it as adversary calls from end to end. Asserted over what the module **imports and takes**, not over its source text, applying S-9.3's recorded lesson that a substring check cannot tell an explanation from an action. **`unsound` and `unrepresentative` are separated by whether an experiment could settle them, and the routing falls out of that rather than being asserted.** An unsound finding is a claim the evidence does not support and another experiment can settle it; an unrepresentative one is usually **correct** — the N+1 is real — about something nobody runs, and no experiment changes that. **So when both land, `unrepresentative` wins**: it is the only ordering under which the audit does not spend experiments it cannot use, ADR 094's hazard reached through the verdict rather than through the agent. It does not weaken S-9.7 — the verdict still needs a stated reason to be reached at all; precedence only decides what happens once it has one, and skipping a finding that is *also* unsound discards less than skipping a sound one. **`inconclusive` exists so an attack that did not run cannot read as one that passed** — S-3.1's *no* versus *not known*, which S-9.4 drew for a missing fit and S-9.6 for a vanished metric, and which a four-verdict vocabulary reintroduces at the top of the epic: an audit that ran two of six attacks and objected to neither would report `sound`. **An attack that does not apply is not an attack that is missing**, and folding them makes `inconclusive` the answer to almost everything, since a finding resting on an ablation has no sweep to audit — *an audit that escalates every finding is as useless as one that passes every finding, and less obviously so*. `inconclusive` sits **below** both objections, because an audit that landed a real objection has told the reader something actionable and *the audit was incomplete* would bury it; and it **escalates rather than asking for experiments**, since what is missing is an attack, not a measurement. **`negative_sound` is unreachable from a finding by schema, not convention**: a verdict carries the `Subject` it is about, `negative_sound` requires a `PartialChain`, and the three verdicts presupposing a claimed cause require a finding — S-8.9's partition reapplied, since a `PartialChain` confirms nothing by construction and there is nothing in one to repair, disprove, or call unrepresentative. `inconclusive` is legal about both, or S-9.9 would have no way to say the thing ADR 094 added it to say. **The two-round audit cap has been decorative since S-5.4 and this story owns it**: the cap counts *rounds*, S-8.9 correctly stopped `Session.run` recording a step for phases not counted in steps, and **nothing else ever counted a round** — the counter stayed at zero and `authorize` compared zero against two on every call. `authorize_round` is separate from `Session.run`'s authorization because that one only fires if a round makes a model call, and a round objecting on the four arithmetic attacks alone would slip past a cap enforced at the API boundary. **Routing reads the budget and spends none of it** — charging an experiment for the decision to run one is S-8.9's finding in a new place. **AC 3 is met by an order of magnitude and the figure is measured**: §4 costs the audit at *~10 calls* assuming six adversary invocations, and with four attacks arithmetic a **full audit makes two model calls**; the ceiling of 15 is checked anyway because a seventh attack needing a model would move the figure silently, and because the Epic 8 composition check's closing lesson was that *a defect whose only symptom is a cost figure needs a test that reads the cost figure*. **The six adapters are part of the story, not convenience** — the Epic 8 check found the opposite arrangement three times: *every caller built them by hand including every test, which is precisely why nothing noticed*. **Sabotage: 37 properties, all caught, zero skipped, zero harness faults, after one survived** — *a gap outranks an objection* was asserted for the five hard objections and not for the soft one, so a representativeness objection alongside an attack that did not run returned `inconclusive` and nothing failed. **The property was right and the coverage was half**, which is this project's recurring shape rather than a new one.

### S-9.9 — Sufficiency and the null result
Depends: S-9.1, S-8.9
Why: **added 2026-08-17 (ADR 094).** S-0.8 measured the agent declining to stop 60 times out of 60, and concluded the stopping decision "probably cannot be the agent's own". S-8.9's cap bounds the damage without deciding sufficiency — and an investigation stopped by it produces a `PartialChain` that **nothing else in Epic 9 can audit**, because every other story assumes a finding exists.
AC:
- Audits a `PartialChain`: were the exclusions established under adequate conditions?
- Distinguishes *nothing was found and that is a result* from *this run stopped too early*
- A run judged sufficient is not returned to investigate, whatever budget remains
- A test proves an investigation the agent would have continued can be stopped by the audit

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/audit/sufficiency.py`, ADR 102, 35 tests in `tests/audit/test_sufficiency.py`. All four AC met. **NO MODEL IS ASKED, and that is the story's central decision rather than an optimization.** S-0.8 measured a model — given clean evidence, curated scenarios and a correct diagnosis — declining to stop **60 times out of 60**; routing that same question through a second model and hoping a different frame saves it is the same question asked of the same kind of thing. ADR 094's *cannot be the agent's own* reads honestly as **cannot be a model's**, and F6 already supplies the rule: *what counts as enough is decided by the harness, because a self-judged criterion is one the judge is incentivised to claim*. Every input is a fact the run already recorded, so `CLAUDE.md` settles it — and **six of Epic 9's eight audit stories now need no model at all**. **Why the run stopped is the first-order signal and the three ways genuinely differ**: `INSTRUMENTS` ran out of *questions*, `STALL` is a sufficiency judgement S-5.4 already made (*more steps of the same kind will spend budget without changing the answer*), and **`CAP` ran out of *money* with something still being proposed — a negative from an interrupted search is not a negative, so it is never sufficient**. **Sufficiency and `Stopped.disposition` answer different questions and deliberately diverge**: §7.2 gives the cap `PARTIAL` and the other two `ESCALATE`, which is what the *run* does next, while whether the negative is *believable* is separate — the `CAP` stop is at once the one that ships a partial chain and the one worth least, and folding one into the other would answer one question with the other. **A result with no content is not a result**: `PartialChain` says the exclusions *are* the result and rightly allows an empty tuple (forty narrowings that never rejected still learned something), but *learned something* and *established a trustworthy negative* are different claims and §9 ships the second. **The rule over several exclusions is `any`, not `all`** — a negative is only as good as its weakest exclusion, since the hypothesis the narrow one failed to rule out is still live; **a one-exclusion fixture cannot tell the two apart**, which is the ninth instance of that shape here and the reason a two-exclusion case was added *before* the sabotage pass. **There is deliberately NO minimum experiment count** — any floor would be a guess and S-9.4's precedent is that a threshold is derived or it does not belong. **AC 1 needed no new machinery**: *were the exclusions established under adequate conditions* is S-9.2's question word for word over the same `Exclusion` type, and a second checker would be two copies of one argument. **AC 3 is a property of the type rather than a branch**: the verdict is about a `Subject.PARTIAL_CHAIN`, S-9.8 refuses the three verdicts that presuppose a claimed cause about one, so the only constructible answers route to `REPORT` or `ESCALATE` — **neither can reach investigate however much budget remains, and there is no exception anybody could add**. Escalation is right for a reason: a cap-stopped run has no experiments left and a stalled one has just been told more of the same will not help, so *run more experiments* is unavailable — ADR 094's forbidden lever. **AC 4 runs against the real loop**: `run_investigation` with one instrument, one rejection and three proposals of the instrument that already answered ends `Stopped.INSTRUMENTS` with **39 of 40 experiments available** — the agent had not decided to stop, it had asked for a fourth experiment three times — and the audit sends it to `REPORT`; only the conditions are widened for that assertion, and **the same run under the conditions it actually had is `inconclusive`**, which is the honest reading of a uniform-only serial-only fixture and still does not return to investigate. **An undeclared dependency recorded rather than silently satisfied**: the backlog lists S-9.1 and S-8.9, and this also depends on **S-9.8** (ADR 094 put `negative_sound` in its vocabulary) and **S-9.2** (AC 1) — both written before ADR 094 rearranged the epic. **Sabotage: 22 properties, all caught, zero skipped, zero harness faults, after one survived and one was skipped.** The survivor is the one worth keeping: hardcoding the stop reason in the artifact left **every verdict correct and every report wrong** — a cap-stopped run whose audit says *every applicable instrument had already answered* tells a human the search finished when it was cut off, and nothing was asserting that the audit's account of the run agreed with the run. The skip was a pattern that stopped matching after a reformat, **surfaced only because S-9.7 made the runner print the skip count**.

---

**EPIC 9 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-17)** — `tests/audit/test_audit_composed.py`, `src/coldfix/audit/compose.py`, ADR 103, 23 tests. The epic finished with nine stories, six attacks, a verdict vocabulary and a routing rule — and **nothing could take an investigation and audit it**. Every attack was reachable from inputs a test built by hand and not one from what the system actually produces: Epic 7's finding, Epic 8's, and now a third consecutive epic ending the same way. **Four defects, and every one is a join.** (1) **There was no path** — six attacks, six input shapes, nothing assembling them, counting the audit's round or checking its ceiling; and **two enforcement functions had no caller at all**: S-9.1's `refuse_shared_session` (the isolation the whole epic rests on, which only fires if somebody calls it) and S-9.8's `authorize_round`/`record_round`, which is how a cap compiled at S-5.4 stayed decorative through nine stories. (2) **The log cannot say which metrics are counts.** `Executor` is typed `Callable[[ExperimentSpec], Mapping[str, float]]`, so everything the primitive knew *about* its numbers is discarded at the loop boundary — every Epic 3 result carries `kinds`, an `Experiment` carries none, and S-9.6 requires one. **The obvious repair is worse than the gap**: `metric_kind` is a pure function of spelling defaulting to `COUNT`, the thesis ablation reports `seconds.share_removed` — a *share of a duration* — and it reads as a count; S-9.6 calls any moved count material, so every re-run would diverge, every finding would be `unsound`, and the amended S-9.8 would ask for more experiments **for ever**. That is S-9.6's own control failure reached through the join. So `kinds` is **supplied and its absence is `NOT_RUN`, never a guess** — S-9.2's missing-fit construction applied to the two other inputs that share its shape, and **the first real caller of the outcome S-9.8 added** so an attack that did not run could not read as one that passed. (3) **A growth fit does not survive into the log either** (`measurement` is `Mapping[str, float]`), so S-9.4 and S-9.2's scale axis had no reachable input. (4) **The conditions must be taken, not derived** — found while wiring the third: `emit.conditions_for(workload)` is the obvious call and is wrong here, because a `FixtureRecipe` holds **one** distribution (the one the run *started* with) while S-8.8 moves the conditions on the `Investigation`. An audit rebuilding them reports a single shape after a reseed swept two, objects to a narrowness already fixed, **and the remedy it names is the reseed that just happened** — F3 again, at a new join. **TWO HONEST RESULTS, ASSERTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND.** *The thesis diagnosis does not survive its own audit*: the finding is real, but *not the database* was established under a uniform fixture driven serially and S-9.2 proves uniform is the blindest shape there is — so the project's showcase run audits `unsound`, which is **the first evidence the audit objects to anything real**. *The thesis sweep is too narrow for a growth claim*: 10/20/40 is 4x across three points against S-9.4's derived 11x, so scale adequacy objects the moment a fit exists — and so does exclusion validity, because S-9.2 delegates the scale axis rather than asking twice, which is that design showing up through the composed path. Neither is a defect; the thesis run exists to demonstrate an instrument *switch*. **ONE LINE DELETED**: `audit_finding`'s `authorize_round` changed no outcome, because its first attack is a model call and `Session.run` authorizes against the same cap before any spend — S-7.4's redundant condition, which S-8.9 collapsed on the same evidence. **`audit_partial` keeps it**, and the asymmetry is the point: that path makes no model call, so nothing else would ever refuse it a third round. **TWO DEFECTS RECORDED AND NOT FIXED.** (a) **The loop discards `kinds` and `Fit`** — the composed path takes them from the caller; the real repair is that `Executor` should carry what the primitive measured *about* its numbers, which touches S-8.2, S-8.7, S-8.9, the `Experiment` schema and every caller. It needs its own story. (b) **Carried from Epic 8 and still true**: `Session.run` assembles S-5.7's blocks and the request never carries them, so `cache_control` breakpoints are never sent and **Epic 5's prompt-caching design is inert — do not quote §12.3's cost as achieved.** **Sabotage: 20 properties, all caught, zero skipped, zero harness faults, after FOUR survived and TWO were skipped on the first pass.** Three survivors were real test gaps: the mid-audit ceiling check could not be told from the final one because the test handed the client **both** recordings (it now gets only the first, so a check landing too late spends the call the ceiling exists to prevent); `key_experiment` preferring a confirmation could not be tested against the thesis log, where the last confirmation and the last settled experiment are **the same record** — **the tenth fixture here that could not tell the right answer from the wrong one**; and the partial-chain authorization had no test at all. Both skips were patterns matching two call sites, **surfaced by the skip count S-9.7 added to the runner**.

---

# EPIC 10 — Surgeon agent

**Goal:** fix the confirmed finding, test first.

### S-10.1 — Falsification test generation
Depends: S-9.8
AC:
- First output is a test, not a patch
- Test asserts both cost improvement and correctness preservation
- Test enumerates the cheat classes it is designed to catch

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/repair/falsification.py`, ADR 104, 32 tests in `tests/repair/test_falsification.py`. All three AC met. **AC 1 is enforced by ABSENCE, not by ordering**: *first* is a claim about sequence and a sequence enforced by convention is one an agent can reorder, so `FalsificationTest` has no `diff`, no `patch`, no `files` and no `approach`, and `generate` has no parameter through which one could arrive — S-8.1's construction for `validate` and S-9.1's for `chain`. **`03-agents.md` §5.4's `failed_on_unpatched: bool` IS NOT A FIELD, and that is a correction to the document**: it has the model returning the outcome of a run it did not perform, which `CLAUDE.md` forbids in as many words and which S-4.1 already closed once for `work_verified`. That fact is the single thing making the test worth anything and it is the harness's to observe — S-10.2 owns the run and the gate — so `describe()` says in words that the test has not been run, because a reader handed one could reasonably assume somebody had. **AC 2's cost half carries the guard-counter non-negotiable, and this is the FIRST artifact where it is checkable before anything runs**, because it is the first that states what *would* count as improvement: a test naming only the metric it wants to move is one a cheat passes by moving it. Two failure modes are refused in opposite directions — a guard pointed at the cost metric guards nothing, and a guard demanding an improvement fails the patch for succeeding where nobody claimed — and `at_most` above baseline is how a bounded, accepted regression gets stated in the open. **A cost threshold at or above its own baseline is refused** for §5.3's reason: any unchanged run satisfies it, which is *a test that passes before you change anything* written down. **The baselines are checked against the chain** (S-8.3's discipline in the repair phase), **and the chain's EXCLUSIONS count as measurements, not only its confirmations** — a baseline legitimately comes from the sweep that ruled the database out, and a checker reading only confirmations would call `db.query = 7` a fabrication; `measured_pairs` was widened from `ExperimentLog` to `Sequence[Experiment]` so S-9.5 and this ask one question of two artifacts rather than holding two copies. **AC 3's `catches` is an ENUM rather than §5.4's `list[str]`**: the five classes are transcribed identically by `02-architecture.md` §210, `03-agents.md` §412 and S-11.3, and *enumerates* means S-11.3 must be able to ask *could a cheat of class X pass this test* in the same vocabulary — so a name nobody defined is **refused rather than dropped**, since discarding it silently leaves a shorter list reading as a complete one. **Unlike S-8.1 this step MAY cascade** — §3 gives `FALSIFICATION_TEST` a real check (*fails on unpatched code*) — and the asymmetry is the table's; **this module cannot perform that check** (no runner, no worktree, no way to execute), so `validate` comes from S-10.2 or nowhere, and the cascade path is exercised here rather than deferred because a parameter with no tested path is the dead code this project deletes. **ONE REAL DEFECT, found by a test written for the criterion rather than for the field**: `Field(min_length=1)` is satisfied by a single space, so an `equivalence` of `"  "` passed every check while asserting nothing — and the hole was open on `claim` and `script` too, where a blank script would have reached S-10.2's runner. **Sabotage: 24 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived** — the step type was **interchangeable with `PATCH`** with nothing failing (both mechanical, same tier today; the only visible difference is which documented check a cascade validates against), and **a truncated reply was accepted**, which would hand S-10.2 a script whose failing assertions were cut off at the token limit. **The substring-over-source trap, walked into for the THIRD time**: an isolation test asserted `"worktree" not in source.lower()` against a docstring using the word to explain why there is no worktree — recorded at S-7.11, recorded again at S-9.3 with the same remedy, repeated here; it now asserts what the module imports and what its functions take. **OPEN AND NOT DECIDED HERE**: `00-BRIEF.md` §4 requires the metastability gate *before the Surgeon can emit its first patch* and this backlog places it at S-10.6, after S-10.4 generates one. Nothing in S-10.1 emits a patch so the question does not arise yet, **but it must be settled before S-10.4 and the resolution is not obviously the backlog's ordering.**

### S-10.2 — Must-fail gate
Depends: S-10.1
AC:
- Test runs against unpatched code before any patch is written
- If it passes, the story stops with a report — no patch is written
- A test proves this gate fires on a vacuous falsification test

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/repair/mustfail.py`, ADR 106, 26 tests in `tests/repair/test_mustfail.py`. All three AC met. **"Unpatched" is a PROPERTY OF THE TYPE, not a promise from the caller**: the gate takes a `DiagnosticSession` and nothing else, and S-2.3 built that class with `apply_patch` and `diff` deliberately absent — *every operation that could carry a change out of this session is absent rather than guarded* — so a patch has **no route into** the worktree this runs against. A gate accepting any session would be checking which revision the caller happened to check out, which reads as met and is satisfied by convention. **THREE OUTCOMES, and the third is the whole design.** The obvious implementation reads *non-zero exit* as *the test failed* — under which a syntax error, a bad import or a missing fixture **authorizes patch generation**, the gate inverted. So the script runs under a wrapper separating an `AssertionError` from everything else: **0 = vacuous (stop), 1 = falsified (proceed), 3 = the script is broken (repair it)**, plus a timeout which is none of the three because a killed run proves nothing in either direction. That is S-3.1's *no* against *not known* at the top of the repair phase, and each refusal carries a **different remedy** — a passing test is a branch to abandon, an errored one is a script to repair, and sending the second back as the first would have the Surgeon rewriting a correct test because of a typo. **The script is NEVER written into the subject's tree**: it travels on the command line, because S-2.4 refuses a patch touching a test and a falsification script materialised as a file would be a test the patch must not touch and which every later diff would show — §5.2's `write_test(script)` effect without the file. `repr` embeds it (quoting becomes the interpreter's problem) and `compile` names it `falsification_test` so a traceback points somewhere. **`Falsified` refuses to represent a passing run** (S-2.7's `VerifiedReset` construction) and carries the run's own output, because *the test failed* with nothing under it is an assertion by whoever wanted it to have failed; `Falsified | NotFalsified` are exclusive by construction so a caller cannot proceed without branching (S-7.1's shape). **A REAL DEFECT, and it is the exact failure this design exists to prevent**: `compile` was **outside** the guarded block, so a script with a **syntax error** raised before the `try` and the interpreter exited **1** — which is `FAILED_EXIT` — so a malformed script read as a falsification and would have authorized a patch. Two lines of indentation, and it survived my own review: the test docstring predicted it while the code did the opposite. The protocol is now verified against a **real interpreter** for all four cases rather than assumed. **Sabotage: 20 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived and three were skipped.** One survivor was **my own broken sabotage** (an `if False else` that changed nothing) — worth recording because a sabotage that does not sabotage reads exactly like a well-tested property. The real one: **dropping `wrap` entirely changed no outcome**, since the fake session returns a canned exit code whatever it is handed and the test only checked the script was *in* the command, which it is either way — and without the wrapper there is no protocol at all, because a bare script exits 1 for an assertion and 1 for a syntax error. The three skips were patterns that stopped matching after `ruff format` reflowed the source — **third occurrence of that class**, visible only because of the skip count S-9.7 added.

### S-10.3 — Test audit before patch
Depends: S-10.1, S-9.1
Why: the test is written by the agent that then writes the patch.
AC:
- Adversary reviews the falsification test **before** patch generation
- Question asked: could a cheat pass this test?
- If yes, the Adversary supplies a strengthened test and the Surgeon must satisfy it
- Costs under 5 calls

**DONE (2026-08-17)** — `src/coldfix/repair/testaudit.py`, ADR 107, 31 tests in `tests/repair/test_testaudit.py`. All four AC met. **THERE IS NOTHING TO WITHHOLD, and that is S-10.1 paying off**: S-9.1 had to strip `rationale` and `outcome` from the experiment log because the Diagnostician wrote justifying prose into it (*72% of reward-hacking episodes carry explicit justifying reasoning*), and `FalsificationTest` has **no rationale field** — S-10.1 refused one — so the artifact goes over whole. `claim` stays for the reason S-9.1 kept `verdict`: an auditor asked *could a cheat pass this test* must know what the test claims, and the opposite failure — isolation by sending nothing — satisfies every rule while making the audit useless. **A STRENGTHENED TEST IS RE-GATED, NOT TRUSTED**: `strengthened` is a `FalsificationTest`, never a `Falsified`, and only S-10.2 produces the second — so it must go back through the must-fail gate, and it must, because *a stronger test the unpatched code already passes is exactly as useless as a weak one*. `forward` is the single accessor for *the test the Surgeon must satisfy*, so a caller cannot carry the weak one forward by reading the wrong field — **the mistake this story exists to prevent, made one layer up**. **THE REPLACEMENT IS CHECKED FOR ACTUALLY BEING STRONGER**, three ways, each of which reads as an improvement to anybody skimming: the cost threshold may not rise (more changes would satisfy it), a guard may not disappear (a trade the original caught would now pass), and **it must claim to catch every class the auditor just named** — an auditor that names a hole and returns a test not claiming to close it has produced a round of work and no coverage, and the Surgeon would satisfy it while the hole stayed open. Everything else about the replacement is validated by **S-10.1's parser**, because a second implementation would be a second answer to the same question. **S-9.1's isolation is PARAMETERISED, not copied** — `audit_session(system=)` and `refuse_shared_session(expected=)` gained one defaulted argument each, `CLAUDE.md`'s *concrete until a second case exists* being satisfied by this being the second case; a test asserts the **finding auditor's** session is refused here just as the Surgeon's is. **A REAL DEFECT the sabotage found: an omitted `weaknesses` field defaulted to *no cheat found*** — `[]` is the auditor saying *I looked and found nothing*, a missing key is a reply that never addressed the question, and reading the second as the first **passes a weak test on silence**; S-9.7's rule in the place where it costs most. **A SECOND SPELLING WAS DELETED RATHER THAN TESTED**: `NO_CHEAT_PASSES = "none"` was S-9.5's construction transplanted and it does not fit — there the empty answer is a *string field*, here an empty *list* — so the branch had no test and no caller, a sabotage removed it and changed nothing, and widening what counts as *the auditor found nothing* is the last thing this module should do. **`Phase.TEST_AUDIT`'s two-round cap had NO CALLER since S-5.4**, the same shape `FINDING_AUDIT`'s had until S-9.8. **The second survivor was a test that could not discriminate**: the round-conclusion test recorded two rounds and asserted the count, but the default `stall_after` is 3 against a cap of 2 — the cap always fires first, so the conclusion is never compared and a constant string passed; at `stall_after=2` the two differ, with a control asserting a repeated conclusion stalls. **`__test__ = False` on `TestAudit`/`TestAuditError`** — their names begin with `Test` because the *subject* is a test and pytest collects on the prefix alone; a warning today, a silently-collected suite the day either gets a no-argument constructor. **Sabotage: 24 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived.**

### S-10.4 — Patch generation
Depends: S-10.3, S-2.4
AC:
- Scope determined by the evidence chain's context list, not agent guessing
- Multi-file patches supported
- Runs in candidate mode only
- Patch applier rejects protected paths

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/repair/patch.py`, ADR 109, 30 tests in `tests/repair/test_patch.py`. All four AC met. **THE MANDATORY ORDERING IS A SIGNATURE, NOT A LIST**: `generate` requires a `Falsified` with **no default**, only S-10.2's gate constructs one, and that type refuses to represent a passing run — so a caller who skipped the gate has nothing to pass. `03-agents.md` §5.3 writes the ordering as numbered steps for an agent to read; **a numbered list is a thing an agent can read quickly and a required parameter is not**, which is why S-10.2 produced an artifact rather than a boolean. **AC 1: scope is the CHAIN'S and the model is never asked what it may touch** — the site plus the `context` files S-8.6 requires a stated reason for; the chain is shown as **evidence** (where the cost was measured, why each file is implicated), which is a different thing from a permission list, and S-2.4's finding is that *a rule a model is told is a rule something can be argued out of*, so the check is server-side either way. An agent that decided a fourth file needs changing has decided something no experiment supports — the remedy is a new investigation, not a wider patch. **`files` IS DERIVED, NOT REPORTED** — §5.4 gives `Patch` a `files: list[str]`, which is the agent restating what the diff says, and a list disagreeing with its own diff would have the scope check passing against a **claim** rather than a change; **third correction of this shape in Epic 10** after `failed_on_unpatched` (S-10.1) and `invalidated_if` (S-8.5). **AC 3's pair is the point**: `apply` takes a `CandidateSession` (the only class with `apply_patch`) while S-10.2's gate takes the `DiagnosticSession` (which has neither) — *a patch must not be able to exist where the must-fail check runs, and has to exist where it is applied*, the same rule read from both ends. **AC 4 stays S-2.4's**: the protected-path filter lives inside `apply_patch` because that is the only route by which a diff becomes a file; this adds a **narrower** rule on top, and **a file can be both in scope and protected** — a chain whose context listed a test file puts it in scope and the applier still refuses it. **A DUPLICATE BLOCK WAS DELETED RATHER THAN TESTED**: `render_brief` carried its own *FILES THE EVIDENCE IMPLICATES* section while `EvidenceChain.render` already emits `SITE` and `IMPLICATED FILES` with the same reasons — two statements of one fact in a **prompt**, costing tokens on every call, and the duplication made the second copy **untestable** (a sabotage deleting its reasons changed no assertion because the chain's copy still carried them). **THE SUBSTRING-OVER-SOURCE TRAP, A FOURTH TIME** — a test asserted `"ProtectedPathError" not in inspect.getsource(...)` while `apply`'s `Raises:` names it correctly; recorded at S-7.11, S-9.3 and S-10.1, so **the rule is now simply that an isolation test never reads source text**. **`attempts_differ` is deliberately the crudest check** (byte equality) because S-10.5 owns the real one and a similarity threshold invented here would be the guess S-9.4 refuses; what it does establish is F12's point — two patches with entirely different `approach` strings and an identical diff are not different attempts. **Sabotage: 23 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after one survived.**

### S-10.5 — Retry discipline
Depends: S-10.4
AC:
- Three attempts maximum
- **Structural check**: attempt 2 is rejected before running gates if its diff touches the same lines with a similar edit shape as attempt 1
- Failure reasons carried in context
- Temperature raised on retries to force different approaches
- Escalates with full attempt history after three

Notes: "must differ in approach" cannot be self-judged — the agent writes its own approach label and can rename the same idea.

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/repair/retry.py`, `hunk_ranges` added to `sandbox/patching.py`, `Attempt` added to S-10.4's `patch.py`, ADR 110, 30 tests in `tests/repair/test_retry.py`. All five AC met. **THE CHECK IS AN EQUIVALENCE, NOT A SIMILARITY SCORE.** The obvious implementation scores two diffs and rejects above a threshold, and that threshold would be **a number nobody measured** — S-9.4's rule is *derived or it does not belong*, and there is no noise floor, class gap or measured quantity here to derive one from. So *similar edit shape* is defined as something that can be **decided**: added and removed lines only (context lines are the file, not the change), stripped of whitespace and trailing comments, compared as a **set** so reordering independent hunks is not a new idea. **What it catches**: an identical diff, one differing only in whitespace, one differing only in comments — which is what *renaming the same idea* looks like when the idea is a diff. **What it does NOT catch**: a renamed local variable, because deciding two token streams mean the same thing needs a parser and a judgement, and a judgement is what this check exists to avoid. **BOTH CONDITIONS, and requiring both is what keeps honest retries alive**: same lines alone would refuse the second genuine idea *at the site where the cost was measured*, and same edit alone would refuse the same change applied to a different target. **The overlap is measured on the ORIGINAL side** — two attempts that both rewrite lines 41-42 are working on the same code however far apart the results land, since an earlier hunk that changed size moves everything after it. **`Attempt` CORRECTED S-10.4**: that story showed retries only the previous `approach` strings — *precisely the self-judged label F12 says the agent can rename* — so the context meant to make the next attempt different consisted entirely of the thing that cannot be trusted to differ; §5.1 asks for *prior attempts **with failure reasons***, and the failure is also the stall conclusion S-5.4 reads, because three attempts failing the same way is a phase repeating itself while three attempts *named* differently is not evidence of anything. **`Phase.REPAIR`'s three-attempt cap had NO COUNTER** — third in Epic 10 after `FINDING_AUDIT` and `TEST_AUDIT`. **A GUARD WAS DELETED RATHER THAN TESTED**: `repeats` refused to compare two edits that both normalized to nothing, and its only reachable effect was calling two comment-only patches at the same lines *different* — they are the same no-op twice (S-3.12's rule). **TWO NON-DISCRIMINATING FIXTURES IN ONE STORY (12th and 13th)**: the hunk headers used the same number on both sides so reading the new side was invisible — **and shifting both by a constant did not fix it**, since both diffs shift together and still overlap; it takes two diffs sharing their *original* lines and none of their new ones. And the fixtures had no context lines, so dropping the filter that excludes them changed nothing. **THE SUBSTRING-OVER-SOURCE TRAP A FIFTH TIME, and the worst instance yet**: the test asserting *there is no similarity threshold* read the module source and **failed against its own docstring**, which uses the word *similarity* to explain why there is no similarity score. Five occurrences is a habit — **an absence or isolation test reads structure (imports, signatures, model fields), never prose.** **Sabotage: 26 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after three survived.**

### S-10.6 — Slack-reducing classifier — **SAFETY**
Depends: S-2.4, S-3.16
**Build order (amended 2026-08-17, ADR 105): this comes BEFORE S-10.4.** `00-BRIEF.md` §4 says *implement it before the Surgeon can emit its first patch*, and S-10.4 is where a patch is first generated. The old `Depends: S-10.4` was a statement about subject matter rather than about inputs: this classifies **a diff**, S-2.4 already parses diffs, and S-3.16 already supplies the retry-amplification check. Epic 10's order is **S-10.1 → S-10.2 → S-10.3 → S-10.6 → S-10.4 → S-10.5**.

Why: our own output is the class of change that pushes systems from stable into vulnerable.
AC:
- Pattern-matches the diff for: added cache or memoization, retry logic, connection reuse, pool size reduction, timeout reduction, added buffering
- Matched patches labelled `slack-reducing`
- **Auto-approval permanently blocked** for labelled patches, at any trust level
- Emits a specific staging warning naming what headroom was removed
- Where fault injection is available (S-3.16), runs the retry-amplification check and attaches the result
- **Does not claim the patch was tested for metastability**

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/repair/slack.py`, `hunk_lines` added to `sandbox/patching.py`, ADR 108, 45 tests in `tests/repair/test_slack.py`. All six AC met. **ADR 105'S OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS NEITHER**: `08-audit.md` F1 says the spike-and-recovery test *is not executable in our environment* — metastability needs a sustaining feedback loop that a single container with one synthetic driver does not have — so it is **not a precondition and is not run at all**. F1's corrected gate is four steps (classify statically, label and block auto-approval permanently, emit a specific staging warning, **do not claim we tested it**), §7's revised build order records the substitution at step 9, and primitive 3 is downgraded from *verification we perform* to **risk class we detect and hand off**. **A stale claim recorded rather than edited in place**: `01-primitives.md` §4 still states the original mandatory gate while §15 of the same file records the downgrade; where they disagree the audit wins. **A FALSE NEGATIVE IS THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION** — an unflagged patch reaches auto-approval, a wrongly flagged one costs a review — so this leans toward flagging, which is **the opposite of S-9.7's conclusion**: same reasoning, opposite outcome, because what a mistake costs is what decides. **TWO OF THE SIX PATTERNS ARE COMPARISONS, NOT KEYWORDS**: pool size and timeout are slack-reducing only when the value goes *down*, and an implementation that greps for `timeout` flags the patch that **raises** one — *a label that fires on the opposite of its subject is one every reader learns to ignore*. Settings are matched **by name across the two sides**, not paired by position, because a hunk that reorders makes position meaningless and `pool_size 20 -> 5` is a sentence a reviewer can check. **The diff parsing is S-2.4's**: a `+` starts content inside a hunk and a file header outside one, `touched_paths` already tracks the counts each `@@` declares, and `hunk_lines` is its sibling — with a test asserting the two agree on the adversarial case (a removed line whose content begins `-- a/x`). **AC 3 IS ENFORCED BY ABSENCE**: `may_auto_approve` has **no trust-level parameter**, because a function taking one is a function somebody can pass a high enough level to and Epic 14's ledger does not exist yet to be argued with; `label` is `None` when nothing matched, **never a second label meaning *checked and clean***. **TWO REAL DEFECTS, both found by running rather than by review, and both false negatives in a safety classifier**: the settings regex **matched nothing** (embedding the vocabulary in the name pattern let the leading `[A-Za-z_]` consume the `P` of `POOL_SIZE`, leaving `OOL_SIZE` to find `pool_size` in), and **`\bbackoff\b` missed the ordinary spelling** since `\b` does not match an underscore, so `adapter_with_backoff` was invisible. **A test fixture that was its own opposite**: the *non-amplifying* `Amplification` used `(1, 1, 2)` against S-3.16's threshold of exactly 2.0 — **eleventh instance here of a fixture that could not discriminate, and the first where it claimed the opposite of its own name**. **Sabotage: 27 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after one survived** — bypassing the settings vocabulary entirely changed no outcome, because nothing tested that an ordinary decreasing number (`page_size`, `column_width`) is *not* a setting; over-flagging is the safe direction for a missed cache and the fatal one for a label.

---

**EPIC 10 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-18)** — `tests/repair/test_repair_composed.py`, `src/coldfix/repair/compose.py`, `src/coldfix/repair/sessions.py`, ADR 111, 22 tests. Six stories, every one with its own sabotage pass — and the epic **could not perform its own sentence**, *fix the confirmed finding, test first*. **Fourth consecutive epic to end this way**, same shape every time: a value one story produces and another consumes, where nothing in either story's tests holds both ends. **FIVE DEFECTS.** (1) **THE TEST WAS NEVER RUN AGAINST THE PATCHED CODE.** S-10.2 proves it *fails* on unpatched code and takes a `DiagnosticSession` so a patch cannot be there; nothing ran it afterwards. The epic wrote a test, proved it failed, audited it, strengthened it, generated a patch confined to the evidence, classified it for slack — and **never asked whether the patch made the test pass**. Epic 11 does not cover it either: that epic *attacks* the patch, and §5.2 gives the Surgeon its own `run_test(script, on_ref)`. `verify` takes a `CandidateSession`, the exact inverse of `run_gate`'s, and between them **the pair states the whole rule: the test is proved to fail where a patch cannot exist, and proved to pass where it does.** (2) **THE SAME THREE EXIT CODES MEAN DIFFERENT THINGS ON THE TWO SIDES.** On unpatched code an error is a *broken script* (remedy: repair the script); on patched code the same script has already run cleanly once, so an error means **the patch removed something the test depended on** — a correctness failure of the patch, and reporting it as *fix your test* would have the Surgeon rewriting a test that was right. The protocol is **shared, not re-derived**: `verify` uses S-10.2's `wrap` and constants, because two encodings of *which exit code means an assertion failed* would be two answers in the two places that must agree. (3) **A STRENGTHENED TEST WAS NEVER RE-GATED**, though S-10.3's return type exists precisely to force that second trip — a stronger test the unpatched code already passes is as useless as a weak one. (4) **TWO SURGEON PROMPTS, ONE SESSION, NO COMPLAINT**: S-10.1 and S-10.4 have different `_SYSTEM` text, `Session` caches one prompt per model from *its* system string while each `generate` sends *its module's*, so a reused session bills and caches against a prefix never sent — **silently, because nothing about the reply looks wrong**. S-9.1 closed this for the audit; the fix lives in each `generate`, since a check only at the join is one something can be routed around. (5) **THE STALL FIRES BEFORE THE CAP CAN, AND ESCAPED AS AN EXCEPTION**: `Phase.REPAIR`'s cap is three attempts and `stall_after` defaults to **three**, so three attempts failing the same way raise `ProgressStalledError` on the third — before the cap could fire on the fourth — and a loop catching only exhaustion let it escape as an unhandled exception carrying no history. Both now escalate, caught separately because *tried three things, none worked* and *got the same answer each time* send a reader somewhere different. **Sabotage: 26 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after SIX survived — and every survivor was the same failure: THE FAKES THREW AWAY WHAT THE COMPOSED PATH PASSED THEM.** `FakeCandidate.run` re-wrapped a canned script instead of running what it was handed, so deleting `wrap` from `verify` changed nothing; it recorded no ordering, so verifying *before* applying — which runs the test against the unpatched worktree and burns all three attempts learning nothing — was invisible; and the stand-in for `patch.generate` discarded its keyword arguments, so the temperature raise and the prior-attempt context were **both untested at the one join that supplies them**. **A composition check whose doubles swallow the arguments is testing that the functions were called, not that they were called with the right things.**

---

# EPIC 11 — Patch audit

**Goal:** defeat the patch, not review it.

### S-11.1 — Isolated context
Depends: S-10.4
AC:
- Receives original code, patched code, evidence chain, falsification test
- Never receives Surgeon reasoning, rationale, or prior attempts
- Isolation enforced by fresh message-list construction, not by prompt instruction
- Different model vendor where configured

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/audit/patchaudit.py`, ADR 112, 24 tests in `tests/audit/test_patchaudit.py`. All four AC met. **S-9.1 HAD TO STRIP; THIS ONE HAS NOWHERE TO PUT IT.** The experiment log's type carries `rationale` and `outcome`, so the finding audit removes them while rendering; `Patch` carries `rationale` and `approach`, and **S-10.4 named the first for exactly this story**. Rather than render-and-strip, the artifact handed over is a **`Candidate` with no field for either** — a caller holding a `Patch` cannot pass its reasoning because there is nowhere for it to go, which is S-10.1's construction pointed at what this epic must not see; `candidate_from` is the single place the reasoning stops. **`approach` GOES AS WELL, and that is a decision**: §6.1 names *reasoning, rationale, or prior attempts* and `approach` is a label rather than reasoning — but it is the Surgeon's one-line account of what it believes it did (what `outcome` was to S-9.1's log), S-10.5 proved it renameable without anything else changing, and **72% of reward-hacking episodes carry explicit justifying reasoning**; a sentence reading *prefetch the authors once* over a diff that adds a cache is that reasoning in miniature. **The opposite failure is not committed** — S-9.1 recorded that *isolation by sending nothing* satisfies every rule while making the audit useless, so the whole diff, both revisions of every touched file, the chain and the falsification test all go; **a file whose source could not be supplied is NAMED rather than passed over** (S-3.9's best-effort reading, one epic on). **THREE AUDITS, ONE ISOLATION**: `patch_audit_session` is S-9.1's `audit_session` with this story's system text — the parameter S-10.3 added — and the message list is `audit_messages` reused rather than copied. `invoke` has **no `patch`, no `attempts` and no `messages` parameter**. AC 4 needs no code (ADR 062's second-vendor blocker is indefinite). **`Phase.PATCH_AUDIT`'s cap had NO CALLER — the fourth**, after `FINDING_AUDIT`, `TEST_AUDIT` and `REPAIR`; the round's *conclusion* stays the caller's because S-11.2–S-11.5 have not defined their verdicts. **A FIXTURE THAT LIED ABOUT ITS OWN HUNK**: the test diff declared `@@ -41,2 +41,2 @@` with one line each side, so the parser correctly ate the **next file's header** as hunk content and the second file vanished — the parser was right and the fixture was wrong. **Sabotage: 25 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived, and both were fixtures that could not discriminate** — the source blocks echoed the diff's own lines so **stopping the diff entirely changed no assertion** (14th instance), and the empty-conclusion test used `"   "` then `""`, different strings that could not stall under either implementation.

### S-11.2 — Equivalence attacks
Depends: S-11.1
AC:
- Constructs adversarial inputs: empty collections, nulls, duplicates, ties, unicode, boundary sizes, unordered results
- Runs both revisions and diffs outputs
- On difference, returns a reproducing input

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/audit/equivalence.py`, ADR 113, 50 tests in `tests/audit/test_equivalence.py`. All three AC met. **NOTHING HERE CALLS A MODEL, and that is the first decision.** §6.2 gives the Adversary `craft_input(spec)`, which reads like a generation problem and is not one: the seven classes are enumerated by AC 1 and transcribed identically by `02` §209 and `03` §411, so what varies between subjects is *which* of them a workload can be fed, never what they are — `CLAUDE.md` puts a model call only where a function will not do. The **driver** is the caller's, though: a `Probe` is source text that reaches one workload, and inventing one here would be this module guessing at a program it has never seen. **A FALSE `identical` IS THE WORST THING THIS MODULE CAN PRODUCE** — `bench/diffing.py`'s opening hazard, worse here because a difference this misses is one nothing downstream looks for again — and two constructions answer it. **Silence is never agreement**: the obvious implementation (collect payloads, diff, report no differences) calls a patch equivalent *precisely when the probe was broken enough to produce nothing*, because two dead runs agree, so a run with no parseable payload is `NOT_COMPARED` and **`survived` is false unless something was actually compared**; an elided stream gets its own reason apart from *not JSON*, since a subject that printed eight megabytes and a probe with a bug are different problems. **Nothing loosens the comparison**: `diff` makes order-insensitivity opt-in because whoever knows whether the query had an `ORDER BY` should choose and the Adversary is by construction the party who does not, so this module never opts in and there is no parameter through which a caller could — the unordered run only **labels** a difference order-only, never forgives one. **AN OBJECTION MUST REPRODUCE, AND THE ORIGINAL IS ITS OWN CONTROL**: on any difference both revisions run again and *the original is checked against itself first*, because a response carrying a uuid differs from its own second run and reported as a broken patch it sends the Surgeon to rewrite code that was right — a check re-running only the *pair* would confirm a difference the subject manufactures afresh every time. The artifact carries **the exact program**, since §222 returns `broken` *with a reproducing input* and one arriving without the means to re-run it is a claim taken on trust. **An unstable subject is not a clean bill either** — the inputs that matched matched once — and a patch audit resolving doubt in favour of shipping is the wrong way round. **THE WIRE IS ASCII AND THE UNICODE CLASS IS WHY**: `execute` decodes UTF-8 with replacement, so a character mangled in transit is a difference the patch did not cause or, mangled identically both sides, an agreement never tested. Session types are opposite for the third time (S-10.2, S-10.6); **new here is a common-base-commit check**, because a difference measured across two base revisions is somebody else's change reported as this patch's and looks exactly like a broken patch. `page_size` has **no default** — guessing one would let a report claim the page boundary was attacked when some other number was. **TWO TESTS COULD NOT HAVE DISCRIMINATED THE PROPERTIES THEY WERE NAMED FOR, both asserting on text instead of the protocol**: a `SyntaxError` prints a fine traceback whether `compile` is inside the guarded block or outside, and a missing `output` binding raises `KeyError: 'output'` — which satisfies an assertion looking for the word "output" as well as the harness's own message does. Both now assert the **exit code**. **THE UNICODE FIXTURES COULD NOT BE WRITTEN AS ESCAPES**: `"café"` written into these files comes back as the character, so the argument for legibility was defeated by the act of making it; they are built with `chr(0x00E9)` and `chr(0x0301)`, which nothing normalises. **Sabotage: 44 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after THREE survived** — `survived`'s unstable clause was **unreachable** under every existing test because a subject varying on *every* input leaves `compared` empty and the first condition answers first (it takes an input that matched *beside* one that would not settle); `Divergence`'s empty guard had no test because the composed path cannot produce one; and nothing asserted what makes **ß** worth sending — any letter round-trips, the property is that a case fold makes the string *longer*. **A surviving mutation is not always a defect**: swapping ß for the ligature ﬀ also survives and should, since ﬀ has the same property, and pinning the code point would test the fixture rather than the reason for it.

### S-11.3 — Cheat detection
Depends: S-11.1
AC:
- Checks for cached state across runs, deferred work, over-fetching, stubbed responses, shape-specific special-casing
- Verifies the improvement survives a fresh process

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/audit/cheating.py`, ADR 114, 35 tests in `tests/audit/test_cheating.py`. Both AC met. **THE FIVE CLASSES ARE S-10.1's ENUM, IMPORTED** — that story built `Cheat` as an enum rather than §5.4's `list[str]` *for this one*, in as many words, and a private copy would be two vocabularies at the one join that must agree: a test declaring what it catches and an audit declaring what it found. **Nothing here measures** (S-9.6's rule), and `_read` **checks the reading is of the revision and shape that were asked for** — a harness ignoring its arguments would return the same numbers for both revisions, every class would come back absent, and the patch would be cleared by a measurement that never distinguished it from the original. **`UNTESTED` IS A THIRD ANSWER AND IT IS THE WHOLE SHAPE OF THE MODULE**: four of the five classes need a metric only some adapters produce, and the obvious implementation skips those and reports the rest as passing — *five checks, nothing found* meaning *one check, nothing found*. So `CheatAudit` refuses to be built unless all five classes appear, and **`clean` is false while any is untested** (S-3.1's *no* against *not known*; S-11.2's `survived` one story on). **AC 2 IS THE COLD PASS**: `primitives/measurement.py` already names the hazard — *work the first run warmed is free for the second* — so a `Reading` is one fresh process driven more than once with the cold pass kept apart, one container run per revision, and the improvement measured warm must exist cold. **THE ORIGINAL IS THE CONTROL BECAUSE EVERY FRAMEWORK WARMS** — Django fills a pool, compiles templates and populates a registry on the first request through *any* codebase, so reading *the later passes were faster* as cached state would accuse every patch ever measured; what is reported is the patch's warm-up **in excess of** the original's. Thresholds are **S-9.6's rule imported, not re-derived**: a count is exact so any fall is real, a duration is one sample against S-0.4's ~12% floor. **S-11.2 answers the stubbed-response class better and goes first** — a size comparison is a proxy, and a proxy disagreeing with the real comparison would be the weaker number overruling the stronger. **One fixture shape cannot answer the shape-specific class** (S-9.3's argument, two epics on): with no alternative supplied it is `UNTESTED`, never clean. **ONE REAL HOLE, FOUND BY A TEST THAT EXPECTED THE WRONG THING**: it expected `detect` to raise on an undeclared metric kind and `detect` succeeded — with the check only in `kind_of`, a cost metric with no declared kind produced a **complete** audit, every class `UNTESTED` for want of *other* metrics so nothing ever asked what rule the cost metric moved under, and the error waited to surface from a property on an artifact a verdict could already read; it moved to `Metrics.__post_init__`. **THE SUBSTRING-OVER-SOURCE TRAP FOR THE FOURTH TIME** — `"class Cheat" not in source` matched `class CheatError` (S-7.11, S-9.3, S-10.1, here); the test now asserts the name bound in the module **is** `falsification.Cheat`, which no prefix can fake. **`Finding.answered` was dead and was deleted rather than tested** — a sabotage made it return `True` unconditionally and nothing failed, because nothing called it. **Sabotage: 41 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after FIVE survived — and three of them were one defect: `clean` HAS THREE CLAUSES AND EVERY EXISTING FAILURE WAS OVERDETERMINED.** Dropping any one clause left the whole suite green, because each test asserting `not clean` had two or three failing at once. Separating them needed a case per clause, the sharp one being a **warm-only gain too small to trip the caching check** — complete, detecting nothing, and still not an improvement anybody gets.

### S-11.4 — Trade attacks
Depends: S-3.8
AC:
- Checks the global resource envelope, not just declared guard pairs
- Reports what increased alongside what decreased

**DONE (2026-08-18)** — `src/coldfix/audit/trades.py`, ADR 115, 22 tests in `tests/audit/test_trades.py`. Both AC met. **THE ONE STORY IN EPIC 11 THAT BRANCHES** — every other hangs off S-11.1 because it asks a model something; this asks arithmetic of two measurements, which is why `Depends:` reads S-3.8. **`08-audit.md` F10 IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT AND IT IS ABOUT DENYLISTS**: `CostClaim` requiring a guard is the strongest thing a falsification test can do about a trade — it makes the Surgeon name what it thinks it might break — and **what it cannot do is make the Surgeon name what it did not think of**, from the party with the least interest in listing it. So the declared guards are evaluated *and* the envelope is checked, and the number this module exists to produce is the one in between: **`uncovered`, an envelope resource risen past tolerance that no declared guard was watching**. That is F10 as an observation rather than a warning — the guards held and something still got worse — and *usually all of them* is the finding, not an artifact, because guards are declared on domain metrics and the envelope is global. **The envelope check is S-3.8's, imported** (`compare_envelope`); it owns the tolerances, the absolute floors and the availability reporting, and a second copy of *a rise must clear a ratio and a floor* would be two answers to one question. **AC 2 IS THIS STORY'S OWN, AND IT CONTRADICTS S-3.8 CORRECTLY**: `compare` flags rises and never falls because a flag is a **verdict** and a two-sided one would flag every successful patch for the improvement it was written to make — but this is a **report**, and *a resource that rose with nothing falling beside it is a regression*, which is a different sentence to put in front of a human than *it bought its speed with memory*; printing only the rises makes the two indistinguishable. **An unevaluated guard is not a guard that held** (`held` is `None`, never `True`) — the denylist failing in its quietest form, the metric somebody *did* think of and still no answer — and `TradeAudit` refuses to be built with a declared guard missing from its report. **Unmeasured is not within tolerance**: three envelope metrics need `/proc` or `getrusage`, so `clean` requires `complete` and a five-of-eight run is not a clean envelope (S-11.2's `survived`, S-11.3's `complete`, third time). **The two metric sources merge in one place and never shadow** — one mapping would let a domain `wall_seconds` overwrite the envelope's, silently and in the direction of the patch looking better, since a domain timer measures the window and the envelope measures the process; a name in both is reported twice, the domain one prefixed. **A TEST HELPER THAT SILENTLY DID NOTHING, IN SEVEN TESTS AT ONCE**: `sample(THREAD_COUNT=64.0)` bound a key spelled `THREAD_COUNT` while the metric names are constants whose *values* are the keys, so `thread_count` stayed quiet and every test that thought it was raising a resource handed the code an unchanged envelope. It takes a mapping now. **Sabotage: 34 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived — and BOTH were fixtures that could not discriminate**, the same note S-11.1 and S-11.2 each ended on: every claim declared its guards on *domain* metrics, where *breaches not covered by a guard* and *all breaches* are the same list so skipping the coverage check changed no assertion; and every `Guard` had `at_most` equal to `baseline`, so comparing against either gives the same answer and a swap survived — **a tolerated regression** (baseline 1000, at_most 1200, measured 1100) is the only shape that separates them. **The substring-over-source check was not written this time** — the reuse test asserts on the module's bindings, since `"def compare" not in source` would have matched `compare_envelope` on its own name.

### S-11.5 — Scope attacks
Depends: S-11.1
AC:
- `find_callers` locates other call sites of every modified symbol
- Runs the full test suite
- Reports callers outside the tested workload

**DONE (2026-08-19)** — `src/coldfix/audit/scoping.py`, ADR 116, 31 tests in `tests/audit/test_scoping.py`. All three AC met. **THE WHOLE INVESTIGATION LOOKED AT ONE WORKLOAD** — the Diagnostician measured one endpoint, the falsification test drives that endpoint, S-10.4 confines the patch to that endpoint's evidence — and the symbol the patch rewrote is called from wherever it is called from, which nothing upstream has reason to know. This story asks the question none of the others can: *the change was verified against one caller; who are the others?* **`hunk_ranges` GREW A `side` RATHER THAN A SECOND PARSER**: S-10.5 reads original-side because *did this attempt change the same lines as the last one* is answered in the numbering two attempts have in common, and this needs the opposite — the symbols are looked up in the **patched** source, where the original numbering points at whatever the edit shifted, so a hunk inserting five lines above a method finds it five lines short and blames whatever used to be there.
