The parameter went beside the existing parser (S-11.3's `Cheat` call, S-11.4's `compare` call, a third time). **NAMES, NOT BINDINGS, AND WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AT ONCE**: Python resolves attributes at run time so no static pass can say which `to_representation` a call reaches — matching by name is over-inclusive (an unrelated class with the same method name) and **still under-inclusive** (`getattr(obj, name)()`, a dispatch table, a template, a signal handler). Over-inclusive is the direction to fail in, and both are in `RESIDUE`, because *a short list is not evidence that few things call this*. An **AST walk rather than a grep**, so a docstring or comment naming the symbol is not a caller — and **a name that is passed counts**, since `map(serializer.to_representation, rows)` breaks exactly as hard as a call and a pass collecting only `Call` nodes would report that file untouched. **AC 2 RUNS ON BOTH REVISIONS AND THE CONTROL IS THE WHOLE VALUE**: a repo whose tests already fail — stale snapshot, missing service, flake — makes every patch look like it broke something, so `ALREADY_BROKEN` is a fourth outcome that establishes nothing in either direction rather than a failure the Surgeon is sent to fix (S-11.2's control against nondeterminism, S-11.3's against warm-up, a third time). **AC 3's *tested workload* is `scope_of` reused** — a second notion of scope would let the patch be confined by one definition and audited against another. **Innermost definition wins**, and **a definition's range starts at its first decorator**, or a change to `@functools.cache` falls outside every definition and is filed as module-level with no callers to find. **`clean` is a much smaller claim than *safe***: given name-matching, an empty `outside` is the absence of evidence of other callers and not evidence of their absence. **Sabotage: 39 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after one survived — a fixture shape, for the THIRD story running**: every source fixture called through an attribute, so a sabotage that stopped reading `ast.Name` callees changed no assertion; a module-level function called by its bare name is what separates the branches. **A test's arithmetic was wrong and the code was right** — the new-side test asserted line 11 of a file grown by four blank lines was `author_name`; it is `to_representation`. The lines were counted by hand and the parser was not.

### S-11.6 — Test-quality attack
Depends: S-11.1
AC:
- Asks whether a cheat could pass the Surgeon's falsification test
- If yes, writes the test that would catch it
- Strengthened test becomes the permanent regression test

Notes: this is the deepest move in the design. The Adversary audits the verifier, not only the artifact.

**DONE (2026-08-19)** — `src/coldfix/audit/testquality.py`, ADR 117, 23 tests in `tests/audit/test_testquality.py`. All three AC met, **and AC 1 and AC 2 were already built**: `08-audit.md` §3.3 found this story's flaw (*the test is written by the agent that then writes the patch*) and its fix was to move the audit **earlier** — *the falsification test is submitted and audited before the patch is written* — which S-10.3 implemented, with no `patch` parameter so the ordering could not be got wrong. **This story's wording predates that correction and `CLAUDE.md` says the audit wins**, so `Weakness`, `render_test`, `parse` and `check_stronger` are imported and `invoke` returns S-10.3's `TestAudit` rather than a parallel type a composed path would branch on twice for the same branch. `check_stronger`'s three refusals therefore apply here unchanged. **WHAT THIS STORY OWNS IS THE EVIDENCE AND THE ARTIFACT.** *The evidence*: S-10.3 asks *could some change* slip through, this asks *did this one*, because by now the diff exists — and the **test is rendered first, the diff second**, because leading with the diff would invite an audit of the change, which is S-11.2–S-11.5's job. *The artifact*: **AC 3 exists nowhere else** — S-10.3's `forward` is the test the Surgeon must satisfy *in this repair*, consumed and forgotten, while a permanent regression test ships with the patch and runs against every later change, so the bar is higher. **`RegressionTest` NEEDS BOTH PROOFS AND THE SECOND IS THE TRAP**: it must have failed on unpatched code or it is not about this bug, and passed on patched code or it cannot ship green — and **the `Falsified` lying around at this point is a proof about the Surgeon's ORIGINAL test while the artifact being shipped is the Adversary's STRENGTHENED one**. Attaching the first to the second is S-10.3's *a strengthened test is re-gated, not trusted* failed at the last possible moment, and the result is a permanent regression test **nobody has ever watched fail**; the constructor compares them. `keep` reads `audit.forward` rather than either field, because this is the layer where shipping the weak test would be permanent. **A sound test is kept too** — `closes` may be empty, and what makes a regression test worth keeping is the pair of proofs rather than the audit having objected. Isolation is S-11.1's unchanged (the subject is a `Candidate`, which has nowhere to put `rationale` or `approach`), and `quality_session` is the **fourth** audit to share `audit_session`. **THE MODULE WARNED ABOUT THE PYTEST-COLLECTION HAZARD AND THEN WALKED INTO IT ON A FUNCTION NAME**: two classes carry `__test__ = False` because pytest collects a class on the `Test` prefix alone, and the session constructor was named `test_quality_session` — which pytest collects as a **test function** on the `test_` prefix alone, erroring on fixtures it does not have. **A second-round audit that finds nothing is the expected result and the residue says so**: the classes are the same five both times and the only new information is the diff, so a round that adds nothing cost a call — which is exactly what S-11.8 measures, and the honest place to record the doubt is here rather than in the ablation's conclusion. **Sabotage: 24 properties, all caught, zero skipped, NONE SURVIVED** — the first story this epic where the fixtures discriminated on the first pass, because most properties are about *reuse* (is this S-10.3's parser, is this its renderer) and about a constructor's refusals, both hard to write an indiscriminate fixture for.

### S-11.7 — Verdict
Depends: S-11.2–S-11.6
AC:
- Schema: `clean` / `broken` + reproducing input / `suspicious` + concern
- `broken` requires a reproducing input — schema-enforced
- Two rounds maximum, then escalate

**DONE (2026-08-19)** — `src/coldfix/audit/patchverdict.py`, ADR 118, 42 tests in `tests/audit/test_patchverdict.py`. All three AC met. **Nothing here calls a model** — five attacks have answered and combining them is *do not add a model call where a function would do*, which holds harder in this epic because three of the five are arithmetic over measurements and never asked a model anything. **THREE VERDICTS AND NO FOURTH, WHICH IS A DIFFERENCE FROM S-9.8**: that story needed `inconclusive` as a fifth because its `unsound` routes back to *investigate* and spends experiment budget, so it had to be able to say *do not spend that* — here `suspicious` already means escalate to a human (§4.4), so an audit that could not see enough **is** a suspicious one. The AC's vocabulary stays exactly as written while the rule this epic has now built five times holds: **an attack that did not run is a concern, never a pass**, and an attack *absent from the list* is checked against `Attack` so a caller that ran two and objected to neither cannot get `clean`. **THE TWO LANDING VERDICTS DIFFER BY EVIDENCE, NOT SEVERITY**: `broken` means *here is something you can run* and `suspicious` means *a person has to weigh this*, and the routing falls out rather than being asserted — a patch with a failing case is one the Surgeon can act on, a merely worrying one has nothing to fix and returning it would produce another guess. **`broken` therefore wins precedence**, spending a cheap repair attempt rather than a person, with the concerns travelling in `results`. That decides every adapter: a detected cheat, an envelope breach and a weak test are all `SUSPECT`, because *the improvement only exists warm* is a judgement about measurements and handing it back as a failing input would hand back an input that does not exist. **`Reproduction` IS A TYPE BECAUSE THERE ARE TWO SOURCES** — S-11.2's input-and-program, and S-11.5's suite command that passes on the original and fails on the patch; only one is a `ReproducingInput`, and `CLAUDE.md` allows the abstraction once a second case exists. **AC 2 is enforced twice**: at the verdict (`broken` without a reproduction cannot be constructed) and at the attack result (`BROKE_IT` is *defined* as the outcome carrying one and no other may), the second stopping `broken` being reached through a reproduction attached to a passing attack. **THIS STORY IS FINALLY THE ROUND CAP'S CALLER** — S-11.1 wired `authorize_round`/`record_round` and left the conclusion to the caller *in as many words* because S-11.2–S-11.5 had not defined their verdicts; `clean` and `suspicious` spend no round, and `broken` returns to the Surgeon only while one remains because a third attempt is the same loop with a bigger bill. **Sabotage: 38 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after two survived — BOTH BRANCHES NO FIXTURE COULD REACH.** `from_cheat`'s *vanishes cold* branch: a warm-only gain usually trips the caching check too, so every fixture reached it with a detected cheat already beside it and deleting it changed nothing — it needs the shape S-11.3 needed its own case for. And `route`'s `clean` handling: **`authorize_round` checks the cap and does not record**, so a spurious check is invisible to `used` — what sees it is exhausting the cap and then routing a `clean`, which must still ship. **A counter that only increments on `record` cannot detect a spurious `authorize`; the observable difference is behaviour at the boundary, not the number.**

### S-11.8 — Adversary ablation study
Depends: S-11.7
Why: if the Adversary adds little, it is theatre and should be cut.
AC:
- Runs a set of findings with and without the Adversary
- Counts bad patches reaching a human in each condition
- Reports the delta
- Repeats at two model tiers to test whether the mid tier misses attack classes

**DONE (2026-08-19)** — `src/coldfix/eval/ablation.py`, ADR 119, 33 tests in `tests/eval/test_adversary_ablation.py`. All four AC met. **`00-BRIEF.md` §5 calls Step 11 the contribution of the whole project and in the same breath says *if the delta is small, cut it — it would be theatre*, so this is the study that decides that, written by the same process that built the thing being judged.** `CUT` is a first-class return of the same function as `KEEP` — no separate negative path anybody could forget to call — and the negative outcomes have more tests than the positive one. **BLOCKING EVERYTHING IS A PERFECT CATCH RATE AND IS WORTHLESS, WHICH IS THE MEASUREMENT THIS STUDY EXISTS TO GET RIGHT**: AC 2 asks for *bad patches reaching a human* and an Adversary that objects to every patch scores perfectly on it while being a wall rather than an audit, so the sound patches are counted too and an arm whose over-blocking rate is at least its catch rate is `CUT` however many bad patches it stopped. **AN EDGE THE CORPUS CANNOT ESTABLISH IS NOT AN EDGE** — the *lower bound* of the catch rate must clear the over-blocking rate, not the point estimate: 12 of 20 against 10 of 20 leads by ten points with an interval from 39% to 78%, and a corpus that could as easily have produced the opposite ordering has shown nothing; the same rates over 200 per label do establish it. **The counterfactual is structural, not measured** — without the Adversary every patch that satisfied the Surgeon's gate reaches a human unflagged because nothing else is in the way, so the *without* arm is the bad-case count by construction and re-running the pipeline would buy a known number at the cost of the corpus; **`suspicious` is not counted as unflagged**, because it reaches a human *with the concern stated* and counting it otherwise would score the escalation as a failure to catch anything. **Ground truth comes from outside** — a corpus labelled by the Adversary would be it marking its own work — and a bad case must name the class that should catch it, because **AC 4's answer is coverage rather than counts**: two arms can stop the same number while one is blind to a whole class. Wilson intervals in stdlib arithmetic (ADR 015), because the normal approximation puts bounds outside [0,1] at exactly the rates this study lives at and claims certainty from twenty clean runs. **TWO BRANCHES OF `recommendation` WERE UNREACHABLE AND THE SABOTAGE PASS FOUND IT**: *the interval reaches zero* cannot happen (a Wilson lower bound is zero only when nothing was caught, which the over-blocking test already returns `CUT` for) and *a rate came back `None`* was already covered by `underpowered` and existed only to narrow `Optional`. The first was **replaced rather than deleted**, because the intention behind it was right and the expression was not — **writing a dead branch to express a real intention is its own failure: the rule *a catch rate indistinguishable from zero* is a good rule, and tested against the wrong quantity it became decoration.** **Sabotage: 45 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after four survived — two of them overdetermined fixtures for the fifth time this epic**: the small-corpus test was thin on *both* labels so removing either clause of `underpowered` left the other firing, and the interval tests asserted only shape properties the normal approximation also satisfies; fixed with a corpus thin on one side only and by pinning `wilson(8, 10)` to the published (0.4902, 0.9433). **`tests/eval/test_ablation.py` collided with `tests/primitives/test_ablation.py`** under mypy — same basename, no `__init__.py` — the exact class of failure `pyproject.toml` already carries a note about for the spike checkouts.

---

**`read_file` CLOSED (2026-08-20)** — `CandidateSession.sources` and `CandidateSession.original_of`, ADR 121, 6 tests in `tests/sandbox/test_modes.py` plus `Subject.of` in the composition. The gap the Epic 11 composition check named: §6.2 lists `read_file(path)` among the Adversary's tools, nothing implemented it, and both `Candidate` and `ScopeAudit` needed source they had no way to obtain — which blocked S-12.1's *runs end to end*. **THE INTERESTING PART IS WHERE THE METHOD COULD NOT GO.** The obvious home is the base `Session`, where both types inherit it, and that is wrong for S-2.3's own reason: **a `DiagnosticSession` may run any command, so it may write any file — give it a reader and a diagnostic run can emit a diff to disk and hand it out, defeating ADR 004 through a reader rather than a writer.** The existing surface test, which asserts `DiagnosticSession`'s public names *by list* so that adding one has to be argued for, is what forced the question to be asked; it now names `sources` and `original_of` among what must not be there. **Both revisions come from the candidate worktree**: `sources()` reads the working tree because that is where the patch is, and `original_of` reads `git show HEAD:path` in the *same* worktree because no applied patch has touched the commit — the alternative, a second session at the base revision, would be a diagnostic one. Read on the **host** like `diff`, because a file read through the container is a file the container could have arranged. Best-effort per S-3.9: non-UTF-8, oversize and `.git` paths are skipped rather than raising, and a path absent at `HEAD` is a patch that added a file rather than a failed read. **SECOND TIME A CAPABILITY LISTED IN §6.2 TURNED OUT NOT TO EXIST, both found by composition rather than by reading the document — the tools table is a design sketch, not an inventory, and Epic 12's nodes are specified the same way.**

**EPIC 11 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-19)** — `tests/audit/test_patch_audit_composed.py`, `src/coldfix/audit/patchcompose.py`, ADR 120, 20 tests. Eight stories, every one with its own sabotage pass — and the epic **could not perform its own sentence**, *defeat the patch, not review it*. **FIFTH CONSECUTIVE EPIC TO END THIS WAY**, same shape every time: a value one story produces and another consumes, where nothing in either story's tests holds both ends. **FOUR DEFECTS.** (1) **THE ROUND IS AUTHORIZED TWICE AND THE SECOND CHECK ANSWERS A DIFFERENT QUESTION.** S-11.1 wired `authorize_round` for *may this round start* and S-11.7's `route` calls it again for *may another round start*, with `record_round` between them as the only thing that moves the counter — so on the last permitted round the pre-check passes, the attacks run, and `route` is asked before the round that just happened was recorded. It says yes, and **the patch goes back to a Surgeon whose reply nothing is left to audit.** The order is authorize, attack, **record**, then route. (2) **THE SUITE COMMAND WAS PASSED TWICE AND NOTHING HELD BOTH ENDS**: S-11.5 runs the suite with one command and S-11.7 builds the reproduction from another argument of the same name, so **the reproduction can name a command that was never run** — and it is the one thing a human is told to paste. `Subject` carries it once and `audit_patch` has no parameter for it. (3) **A STRENGTHENED TEST COULD NEVER BECOME A REGRESSION TEST.** `RegressionTest` refuses a proof of failure about a different test — S-10.3's *re-gated, not trusted*, at the artifact that ships — and the only `Falsified` in existence here is the one for the Surgeon's **original** test, so on the branch where the audit strengthened something `keep` could not be called at all: **S-11.6's AC 3 was unreachable.** The replacement now goes back through S-10.2's gate against the diagnostic worktree and S-10.6's `verify` against the candidate, and returns `None` where it passes on unpatched code — `PASSED_UNPATCHED` reaching the permanent artifact, where shipping it would install a test that can never fail. (4) **NOTHING IN THIS PROJECT CAN READ A FILE OUT OF A WORKTREE**: `03-agents.md` §6.2 lists `read_file(path)` among the Adversary's tools, no session has it, and both `Candidate` and `ScopeAudit` need source. Named rather than closed — `MISSING_READ_FILE` is in every report — and it is **a real dependency of Epic 12 on a capability nobody has built**. Also: two sessions because two prompts (Epic 10's Surgeon defect, not repeated), and `unattempted` so *nothing ran* cannot be spelled as the empty list `verdict_for` refuses. **Sabotage: 12 properties, all caught, zero skipped** — each defect restored one at a time, each failing the check. **THREE FIXTURE LIMITS MASQUERADED AS DEFECTS FIRST, and telling them apart was most of the work**: the fake worktree recognised a suite by `argv[0] == "pytest"` so `python -m pytest` ran `--maxfail=1` as a program and both revisions failed; the cheat metrics named two of the five things S-11.3 needs, so four classes came back `UNTESTED` and every round was correctly `suspicious`; and both worktrees held identical source, so no falsification test could fail on one and pass on the other — they now differ by an **added symbol** rather than a changed answer, keeping the equivalence attack quiet and the clean path reachable. **Source-normalising tooling rewrote `\n` escapes into real newlines** and broke the string literals repeatedly; the scripts are built with `chr(10)`, the same remedy S-11.2's unicode fixtures needed.

---

**EPIC 11 COMPLETE (2026-08-19)** — eight stories, ADRs 112–119. The epic's own sentence is *defeat the patch, not review it*, and it can now perform it: S-11.1 hands over a `Candidate` with nowhere to put the Surgeon's reasoning, S-11.2 to S-11.6 mount the five attacks of §6.3, S-11.7 combines them into `clean`/`broken`/`suspicious` and routes on the two-round cap, and S-11.8 is allowed to conclude the whole thing was theatre. **ONE PATTERN RAN THROUGH EVERY STORY**: five separate constructions all built to say *an attack that could not run is not an attack that passed* — `Equivalence.survived` requires something compared, `CheatAudit.clean` requires every class checked, `TradeAudit.clean` requires every envelope resource read, `ScopeAudit.clean` requires every touched file readable, and `PatchVerdict` makes an unanswered attack a concern. **AND ONE FAILURE RAN THROUGH EVERY SABOTAGE PASS**: seven of the eight stories ended with at least one survivor that was a fixture unable to discriminate — sources echoing the diff's own lines, guards whose `at_most` equalled their `baseline`, callers reached only through attributes, corpora thin on both labels at once. The composition check this epic still owes is the one the previous four epics each discovered late: **nothing yet runs S-11.1's `Candidate` through all five attacks into S-11.7's verdict on one patch**, and every epic that has ended without that has found a defect at exactly that join.

# EPIC 12 — LangGraph orchestration

**Goal:** durable execution across hours, crashes, and multi-day human gates.

### S-12.1 — Graph assembly
Depends: E7–E11
AC:
- Seven nodes wired: ground, screen, investigate, audit_finding, repair, audit_patch, ship
- Four routing functions implemented per `08-audit.md`
- Graph compiles and runs end to end on the target repo

**DONE (2026-08-20) — AC 1 and AC 2 fully; AC 3 for the graph, NOT for the target repo.** `src/coldfix/orchestrator/graph.py`, ADR 122, 20 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_graph.py`. **TWO OF THE FOUR ROUTING FUNCTIONS ALREADY EXISTED**, which is what checking before building is for: S-9.8's `verdict.route` decides where a finding audit sends an investigation and S-11.7's `patchverdict.route` decides where a patch audit sends a patch, and writing either again would be a second answer that disagrees the first time a cap moves. **THE DECISION IS MADE IN THE NODE AND READ AT THE EDGE**: both routers take a `Budget` whose caps cannot be reconstructed from the state's `budget` projection, and a conditional edge sees only state — so a node calls the router where the budget is and writes the answer to a new `route` channel on `CheckpointedState`, with `decided()` the one place a route becomes a string because *which spelling goes in the channel* is what two nodes come to disagree about, and the disagreement shows up as a run that ends early rather than as an error. The two routers this story **does** own — after screening and after shipping — are exactly the two that need nothing but state. **A route nobody wrote ends the run** rather than resolving to a default: a run that stops is one somebody looks at. **The graph owns the shape and the epics own the work** — `Wiring` is the seam, because the seven compose entry points want eleven kinds of argument between them, and it is what lets the whole graph be compiled and **executed** in a unit test so a routing mistake is a wrong path rather than an expensive one. **Nothing found is an answer**: the edge after screening goes to `END`, since treating an empty screen as an error would turn `00-BRIEF.md` §9 into a crash. **Only three edges do not decide, and one is F2** — `investigate` goes to `audit_finding` and never straight to `repair`, which makes *nobody audits the diagnosis, only the patch* unavoidable rather than conventional. **F14's re-screen reads what the ship node invalidated, and is only expressible because of Epic 6's composition check**, which changed `screening` from a sequence to a mapping keyed by workload id — a flat sequence cannot be filtered per workload, so the policy had a correct answer with nowhere to go. **S-6.3 PREDICTED THIS STORY'S TYPE ERROR IN WRITING BEFORE IT EXISTED**: its `node` docstring says LangGraph's protocol declares `__call__(self, state: ...)` with a *named* parameter so a plain `Callable` fails at `add_node`, `Step` was written as exactly that alias, and mypy failed at exactly that line — it is a `Protocol` now; the same check then found that with a Pydantic schema LangGraph hands a node the **schema instance**, not a mapping. **Sabotage: 22 properties, all caught, zero skipped, after one survived — the declared reachable set**, where `_destinations` returning `[]` changed no assertion because nothing looked at the *drawn* graph, which is the only place an unreachable node is visible: a conditional edge whose destinations LangGraph must infer draws no edges at all, so a node nothing can reach looks identical to a correctly wired one. **AC 3'S REMAINDER IS REAL AND OPEN**: *runs end to end on the target repo* needs the seven adapters binding `Wiring` to the epics, a container and a database, none of which this story builds. That binding is the next piece of work and should be its own story before S-12.2.

### S-12.2 — Checkpointing
Depends: S-12.1, S-6.3
AC:
- SQLite checkpointer in development, Postgres supported for concurrent campaigns
- State persisted after every node
- Checkpoint size bounded per S-6.3

**DONE (2026-08-20)** — `src/coldfix/orchestrator/checkpointing.py`, ADR 124, 16 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_checkpointing.py`. All three AC met. **TWO PACKAGING DEFECTS CAME FIRST, both found by checking before building** (third story running). **`langgraph` was a dev dependency and `src/` imports it** — S-12.1 introduced that, so a wheel would fail at import, and it sat in the dev group *beside a comment citing it as an example of something `src/` does not import*, which stopped being true the day the graph was written. And **neither checkpointer backend was installed**: `langgraph` ships only the in-memory saver, which persists nothing across a process, while `langgraph-checkpoint-sqlite` and `-postgres` are separate packages and AC 1 names both. **AC 2 NEEDS NO MECHANISM, ONLY A DEMONSTRATION** — LangGraph writes a checkpoint after every node when compiled with a checkpointer, so what this story owes is a test that walks a real graph against a real SQLite file and reads them back; a criterion satisfied by a library's default is still a criterion, and taking it on trust would have missed both findings below. **The backends are named for the question, not the technology**: SQLite serialises writers so a file is a lock, and the choice is about how many runs share the store — `for_campaigns` returns a **DSN** rather than a saver, because opening a connection would make importing this module a thing that talks to a database. **The bound is S-6.3's imported, and checked in the encoding that is written**: that story measured JSON as a proxy (ADR 003 says the checkpointer stores JSON) and recorded msgpack as ~15% smaller — the safe direction — and `measure` now reads the real serialiser with a test pinning the proxy-to-actual relationship rather than assuming it survives a serialiser change. **`with_config(checkpointer=...)` IS ACCEPTED, CHANGES NOTHING, AND WRITES NO CHECKPOINTS** — the first attempt, five tests failing with an empty list of saved states. The checkpointer is a **compile-time** argument, so `assemble` grew a parameter; a graph compiled without one keeps no history, which is right for a unit test of the shape and wrong for anything that must resume. **That is precisely the failure AC 2's demonstration exists to catch: a run that looks persisted and is not.** **A CHECKPOINT HOLDS ONLY THE CHANNELS WRITTEN SO FAR, NOT THE WHOLE SCHEMA** — the earliest have no `project` key at all rather than an empty one, which is **S-12.3's problem arriving early**: a resume reads these, and code assuming every channel is present fails on the first checkpoint of every run. **Sabotage: 15 properties, 14 caught, one survivor that is genuinely equivalent** — deleting a connective clause from the refusal leaves both the cost (*after every node*) and the remedy (*references rather than results*) stated and asserted, and pinning the prose word-for-word would test the wording rather than the content; left uncovered deliberately and recorded.

### S-12.3 — Crash resume
Depends: S-12.2
AC:
- A run killed mid-investigation resumes from the last checkpoint with full state
- Tested by killing the process at three different nodes
- Resumed run produces the same final result as an uninterrupted run

**DONE (2026-08-20)** — `src/coldfix/orchestrator/resume.py`, ADR 125, 20 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_resume.py` plus the `crashing_run.py` subprocess. All three AC met. **THE PROCESSES ARE REALLY KILLED**: `os._exit` inside the node, because an exception would unwind, run every `finally`, flush every buffer and close the SQLite connection — *a graceful shutdown wearing the word crash*, proving nothing about what a reboot leaves. **AND WHAT IT HELD, AT FIRST, WAS NOTHING — S-12.3 SHIPPED NOT MEETING AC 1, AND ITS TESTS COULD NOT HAVE SAID SO** (corrected same day, ADR 125). LangGraph submits each checkpoint write to a **background executor** and does not wait, so `os._exit` took the process before those threads ran and a kill at any of the first four nodes left exactly one checkpoint holding nothing but `__start__`, however far the run had got. `SqliteSaver` was not at fault — its cursor commits in a `finally` — and the claim that it *commits per write* was read off S-12.2's test, which closes the connection **cleanly**, which is not a kill. **Every test still passed because with nothing durable a resume restarts from the beginning and reaches the same final answer**, so AC 1 and AC 3 both held vacuously; the only test that could see it was flaky rather than failing, giving `[1,1,2]` when the last write raced through and `[1,1,1]` when it did not. The fix is **`durability="sync"`** in `start` and `resume` as a module constant, since a run started durable and resumed asynchronously would be half-protected in a way nothing reports — measured, kills at five successive nodes now leave 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 checkpoints against 9 for a completed run. **The tests were strengthened to be able to fail**: `checkpoints >= 1` is satisfied by the empty `__start__` checkpoint, so the assertion is now that a channel the run actually wrote survived, and the three depths must be *distinct* because equal counts are what an asynchronous write produces. The three nodes are three different *kinds* of point (after the first write, mid-investigation, inside the repair cycle) and a test asserts the kills land progressively further along, so the parametrised cases are three cases rather than one run three times. **`invoke(None, ...)` RESUMES AND `invoke(state, ...)` RESTARTS, AND THE MISTAKE IS INVISIBLE**: LangGraph reads `None` as *continue this thread* and anything else as *begin with this*, so handing the initial state back after a crash is a second run that repeats every node and bills every phase again — and **both calls return a final state, so the wrong one is detectable only by the bill**. `start` and `resume` are two names for that one argument, and `resume` takes the checkpointer so it can refuse a thread nothing was written for, since `invoke(None, ...)` against an unknown thread starts a new run rather than failing. **A missing channel is not an empty one** (S-12.2's finding carried through): `progress_of` fills nothing, because a default would hand a resumed run a value it never had. **AT-LEAST-ONCE IS REPORTED, NOT FIXED** — a checkpoint is written *after* a node so a crash inside one re-runs it, and `experiments` gaining the same entry twice is S-8.4's append-only guarantee broken by a crash rather than a rewrite; `duplicated` reports it and does not deduplicate, because deduplicating would hide that a node was not idempotent. **What makes this safe today is *where* the appends happen** — in the node's return value, after the work — so an interrupted node contributes nothing rather than half, and a node that grew a side effect before its return would break it. **THREE STORIES IN THIS EPIC HAVE NOW BEEN ABOUT A LIBRARY'S DEFAULTS RATHER THAN OUR CODE**: LangGraph hands a node the schema instance (S-12.1), `with_config(checkpointer=...)` silently does nothing (S-12.2), and `invoke(None, ...)` is the difference between resuming and paying twice — all three invisible to a passing suite, and all three found by writing the demonstration the criterion asked for instead of assuming the library did what its name suggested. **The suite gained ~95 seconds of subprocess launches**, which is the cost of AC 2 being about processes; the fast subset now runs well past ten minutes and that needs a decision rather than more drift.

### S-12.7 — Node adapters
Depends: S-12.1, S-7.13 (done 2026-08-21), S-8.11 (done 2026-08-21)
Why: S-12.1's AC 3 remainder. `Wiring` takes seven `Step`s and nothing builds them, so *runs end to end on the target repo* has never been performed.
AC:
- Seven adapters translating `CheckpointedState` to each epic's entry point and back
- A test drives the compiled graph through all seven nodes on `tests/fixtures/` with a replaying client
- The state channels hold what each phase produced

**DONE (2026-08-21) — AC 1 and AC 3 fully; AC 2 for the translation, NOT for the drive.** `src/coldfix/orchestrator/adapters.py`, the `repaired` channel on `CheckpointedState`, ADR 129, 17 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_adapters.py`. **THIRD ATTEMPT, AND THE FIRST TWO ARE THE STORY.** The 2026-08-20 attempt was reverted uncommitted after finding two of seven nodes had nothing in `src/` to call; S-7.13 and S-8.11 built those producers, and this is the binding on top of them. **A NODE IS A REHYDRATE, CALL, SERIALIZE SANDWICH AND IT OWNS ONLY THE BREAD** — the call in the middle belongs to a composition that already has its own check, so every test here is about a slice: what survives a round trip, and what a node refuses to invent when a channel is empty. **TWO TYPES ARE NAMED `Session` AND CONFLATING THEM WAS THE FIRST DRAFT'S MISTAKE, IN FIVE PLACES**: every compose entry point takes `cost.session.Session` *positionally* and a `sandbox.modes` session by *keyword*, and they share a name and nothing else. `Sessions` is keyed by the step's system prompt because that is what `refuse_shared_session` compares, and every prompt asked for is one `agents/roles.py` independently asserts belongs to the role spending. **`repaired` IS A CHANNEL §1.1 NEVER HAD** — it replaces rather than appends, like `chain` and `target`, and **not `attempts`**, which is append-only history and would conflate *what happened* with *what is being audited now* the moment S-11.7 sends a patch back. It carries the falsification proof as well as the patch, since `Falsified` refuses to describe a failure as a success and re-deriving it past a checkpoint would build that proof from something other than the run. **THE EXPERIMENT LOG IS STORED WITHOUT `detail`, AND S-6.3'S DESIGNED PATH IS UNREACHABLE**: building an `ExperimentRef` needs a `Recall` that only the replay cache produces and `run_investigation` never sees it, so what is stored is the artifact minus its raw output — the arithmetic is the same (`MAX_REFERENCE_BYTES` is 1 KiB, a record without `detail` is under it, forty still fit) and `detail` is precisely the megabytes-per-node write F13 exists to prevent. The log is rebuilt by replaying `append`, because the log assigns indices and `read_experiment(7)` has to mean the seventh. **`ship` DOES F14 AND DOES NOT PRETEND TO DO S-16.2**; its touched files come from `touched_paths(diff)` rather than a field, because `Patch` deliberately has none. **S-6.3'S NAMED-PARAMETER PREDICTION LANDED A THIRD TIME** and a test now compiles the real graph over the real closures. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught** — keeping `detail` fails two tests, defaulting a missing channel fails three. **AC 2'S REMAINDER IS REAL AND OPEN**: the seven-node drive needs a container, a database and a recording per model call, which is S-17.1's, and rounding it up here would repeat exactly what S-12.1's own note warned about.

---

**The blocker this story was filed under, kept because it is why the shape changed:** The note on S-12.1 says this needs *the seven adapters binding `Wiring` to the epics*, which counts seven entry points where there are **five** — and an attempt to write the module found a third gap first. **THE COMPOSITION CHECKS PRODUCED TESTS, NOT ENTRY POINTS.** Every epic ended with a composition check performing its own sentence once, and for Epics 9, 10 and 11 that landed in `src/` as `audit/compose.py`, `repair/compose.py` and `audit/patchcompose.py`. For Epics 7 and 8 it landed in a **test file and nowhere else**: `GroundingRun` is constructed in `tests/explorer/test_run.py` and in no module under `src/`, and `chain_from` — the function that assembles the `EvidenceChain` every downstream node reads — is called only in `tests/diagnosis/test_diagnosis_composed.py`. So `ground` has nothing to call, and `investigate` can run S-8.9's loop but cannot produce `state.chain`, because `run_investigation` deliberately does not build one (*the loop does not build the chain*) and the interpreted half `chain_from` needs — mechanism, site, context, and a share of cost per confirming experiment — has no producer outside that test. **A partial binding does not help**: `investigate` is what fills `chain`, and `audit_finding`, `repair` and `audit_patch` all read it, so four adapters with that one missing is a graph that cannot reach its second node with anything in hand. **THE THIRD GAP IS IN THE STATE ITSELF.** `03-agents.md` §1.1 gives eleven channels and none of them carries a patch: `repair` returns a `Repaired` — the patch, its slack classification, every attempt — and `audit_patch` needs the patch, the falsification test, the proof it failed unpatched, the subject and the before-and-after measurements. **Nothing joins the two nodes**, which is the same shape as all four defects Epic 11's composition check found — a value one story produces and another consumes, with neither story's tests holding both ends. §1.1 was written when `repair` and `audit_patch` were adjacent boxes in a diagram rather than two nodes with a checkpoint between them. The fix is a `repaired` channel replacing rather than appending, like `chain` and `target`; **not `attempts`**, which is the append-only history of what was tried and would conflate *what happened* with *what is being audited now* the moment S-11.7 sends a patch back for a second round. **Two smaller corrections found the same way**: `Subject.of` wants an `equivalence.Probe` and not the loop's `Executor`, and `Conditions` is read off the workload by `emit.conditions_for` rather than held as a run-wide constant — *two statements of one fact drift, and here the one that drifts decides whether a correct exclusion is ever reopened*. **AND S-6.3'S PREDICTION LANDED A THIRD TIME**: a step built as a closure and annotated `Callable[[CheckpointedState], Mapping[str, object]]` fails `Wiring` because `Step.__call__` declares a *named* parameter and a `Callable`'s are positional-only — the same error S-12.1 hit and S-6.3 wrote down before either existed. **Nothing was committed**, because a module that cannot type-check is not a smaller version of this story.

### S-7.13 — Grounding composition, in `src/`
Depends: E7
Why: S-12.7 has nothing to call, and a composition that lives in a test cannot be a node.
AC:
- A function under `src/` stands one repository up and returns the project facts and the workloads
- `tests/explorer/test_explorer_composed.py` calls it instead of rebuilding the sequence
- The nine-module order Epic 7's composition check established is preserved, and its six join defects stay fixed

**DONE (2026-08-21)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/compose.py`, ADR 127, 6 new tests in `tests/explorer/test_compose.py` plus the 9 existing ones now driving `ground_workload`. All three AC met. **WRITING THE SEQUENCE IN THE TEST FIRST WAS RIGHT AND STOPPING THERE WAS NOT.** The composition check is how Epic 7's six join defects were found, and a design that had gone straight to `src/` would have found them later and more expensively — but *a working sequence with one caller is a working sequence with no callers*, and `tests/` is not importable from `src/`. **A SEVENTH JOIN DEFECT, INVISIBLE TO THE CHECK THAT OWNED IT.** The check calls `resolve_auth`, asserts `resolution.resolved`, and then calls `verify_work` with **no `headers` and no `cookies`** — `attach` exists precisely to turn a `Credential` into the two mappings a subsequent request carries, and nothing was calling it. The credential was minted, asserted to exist, and dropped. On a subject whose every route is open that changed no assertion, which is exactly why it survived a check whose entire purpose is this class; a route that actually required auth would have been driven unauthenticated and **measured whatever a 401 costs**, in a system whose first non-negotiable is *no finding without a measurement*. `carried()` is **public rather than private, because it is the join and a join with no test of its own is how this one survived** — and its tests live in a new fast file rather than beside the check, which is `slow` and therefore not where a join gets exercised. **Sabotage: restoring the defect fails three tests.** **The sequence refuses rather than guesses**: `Plan` carries what the Explorer decides — `entity` sharpest among them, since it is defect six, where two equally-good factories tie and the alphabetical tie-break seeded a hundred authors and drove `/books/` for one query and thirteen bytes. Three stages stop the run rather than continuing with a value the previous stage did not produce, each naming the stage, because **stopping is an answer** (§9) and S-7.11's acceptance is that the Explorer reports failure rather than claiming success on empty data. **`None` stays *not recorded* and never *resolved against today*** — S-7.12's distinction, and a rerun that silently resolved a different Django voids S-0.4's byte-identical guard counters. **THE GENERALISABLE FINDING IS ABOUT COMPOSITION CHECKS RATHER THAN ABOUT GROUNDING**: a check proves the parts fit and does not put the assembled thing anywhere the system can reach. Two of five epics ended with the sequence stranded in a test and **neither `DONE` note records it**, because from inside the epic there was nothing left to do — the gap is only visible from the caller, which arrives an epic or two later. Worth adding to the habit: **when the check passes, ask where the sequence now lives.**

### S-8.11 — Chain assembly, in `src/`
Depends: E8, S-8.6
Why: `investigate` must produce an `EvidenceChain` and only a test can today.
AC:
- A function under `src/` takes a confirmed `Investigation` and returns its `EvidenceChain`
- The interpreted half — mechanism, site, context, and a share of cost per confirming experiment — has a named producer rather than a test fixture
- `chain_from`'s refusal stands: an investigation that confirmed nothing owes a partial chain, and neither this nor the caller may invent a share of cost

**DONE (2026-08-21)** — `src/coldfix/diagnosis/explain.py` and `compose.py`, `share_metric`/`AblationResult.reported` in `ablation.py`, ADR 128, 14 tests in `tests/diagnosis/test_explain.py` plus the composition check rewired. All three AC met. **NOT THE SAME SHAPE AS S-7.13.** Epic 7's sequence existed and needed a home; Epic 8's **did not exist** — the loop stops short of a chain deliberately (*the loop does not build the chain, and that is a refusal rather than an omission*) and `chain_from` takes the rest from its caller, so what was missing was two producers nobody had written. **STATING THE CONCLUSION IS A ROUTED STEP AND §3 ALREADY HAD A ROW FOR IT**: `StepType.EVIDENCE_CHAIN` carried the mechanical check *schema requires a measurement* before anything used it, and that check is what makes a cascade safe here — `CLAUDE.md` forbids cascading on hypothesis generation and attack design because no deterministic validator exists for either, and here one does and it is the schema rather than a prompt. **THE REPLY CANNOT CARRY A NUMBER**: `Explanation` has a mechanism, a site and a context list, `extra="forbid"`, and no field a figure could arrive in — tested against three spellings, because a schema refusing one word refuses a vocabulary rather than a behaviour. **An investigation that confirmed nothing is never asked**, and the refusal is *before* the call: asking a model to explain a cause nothing established is the one place a finding could be written with no measurement under it, and it would cost a frontier call to do it. **THE SHARE OF COST WAS NOT UNREACHABLE — IT WAS UNNAMED, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS STORY RECORDED THE WRONG FINDING.** It said `AblationResult.share` computes the fraction, `Executor` returns `Mapping[str, float]`, and therefore nothing could carry it across; **Epic 8's own fixture disproved that**, having emitted `round(result.share("seconds"), 2)` under `"seconds.share_removed"` since the epic was written. A fraction is a float and fits through the boundary; what was missing was a name both ends agree on, which `share_metric` now owns in `ablation.py` beside the arithmetic. **This is a different failure from Epic 9's**, which stands: `kinds` and a `Fit` are not floats, cannot cross at all, and leave three attacks `UNTESTED` — the boundary is **narrow, not closed**, and the two want different fixes. **The order is shares then the model**, so a log with no share fails without spending a call. `assemble_with` takes an explanation and `chain_of` buys one, which lets the composition check supply a *reply* — the only thing a test can honestly supply — parsed through the same `parse` a live one would take, instead of supplying the conclusion. **Sabotage: both new guarantees restored one at a time, each failing its tests** (`extra="ignore"` fails three, a defaulted share fails two). **THE CORRECTION IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING: a claim that something is structurally impossible is exactly the claim to check against what the system already does, because the code disproving it is often a fixture nobody reads — *unreachable* and *unnamed* look identical from the call site and want completely different work.**

### S-0.9 — The gate the project checks itself against is flaky
Depends: none
Why: `CLAUDE.md` requires the fast subset green before a story is called done. It is not reliably green, and the failures are load-dependent — which weakens that claim in exactly the direction that hides a regression.
AC:
- The fast subset passes repeatedly on a loaded machine, or the load-sensitive tests are excluded from it and say why
- No timing test asserts the **absence** of an effect against a fixed threshold without a measured noise floor
- A test that would flake is failed by a deliberate load rather than discovered by chance

**DONE (2026-08-25)** — `tests/fixtures/contention.py`, `tests/primitives/test_load_sensitivity.py`, a fourth pytest marker, twenty-two tests quarantined and one repaired, ADR 142. All three AC met, and **AC 1 is measured rather than argued**: the full fast subset passed **twice in a row on a machine with one spinner per core**, 2899 and 2900 tests, at about seventeen minutes a run.

**AC 3 WAS BUILT FIRST AND THAT IS WHAT MADE THE REST EVIDENCE.** The note called the fix *a decision, not an obvious fix* and listed three candidates; the first question turned out not to be which fix but **which tests**, and that is measurable. **THE FIRST LOAD GENERATOR WAS WRONG AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID SO**: spinning Python threads all contend for one GIL, so thirty-two occupy about one core — against the four known-fragile tests that produced **one failure in sixty runs**. Separate *processes* occupy cores, and at twice the core count the same four failed 0/5, 2/5, 4/5 and 5/5. **A FULL HEAP WAS THE WRONG HYPOTHESIS AND WORTH RULING OUT**: the flakes appear in the long single-process run and not standalone, so accumulated objects and a generational collection landing inside a timed measurement was the obvious suspect — `bench.timing` deliberately does no GC control — but six thousand live blocks produced one failure in twenty where oversubscribed cores produced eleven.

**ONE MECHANISM EXPLAINS ALL OF IT: wall time inflates under contention and a count does not.** `off_cpu` calls a half-compute block blocked because elapsed grew and CPU did not; `instructions.hidden_work` decides a pure-Python workload ran in C for the same reason; `test_epic_composed` asserts a wall-clock gap stays under S-0.4's 20ms floor and measures 21.4ms. Eight symptoms, one finding — established by capturing the actual assertion text rather than inferring it, because **marking a test on a guess about its mechanism would hide a defect** and two of the candidates looked like string assertions.

**THE MARKED SET IS A FUNCTION OF THE LOAD, AND THE LOAD IS A KNOB — the part of this story that changed while running it.** Seven sweeps at 2×: 13 found in the first, then 1, then 2, then a clean five-run pass, then 3 in `tests/bench`, then 1, then 1 in `tests/explorer`. **At 2× the tail does not terminate**, because twice the core count is harsh enough that anything with a subprocess timeout eventually fails — the last one found was a `docker ps` call, not a timing assertion. So 2× is the setting for *hunting* and the acceptance bar is **one spinner per core**: a fully busy machine, which is what a parallel build produces and what all five real sightings happened under. **The durable deliverable is the harness, not the list.**

**QUARANTINE IS THE LAST RESORT AND `test_off_cpu.py` ALREADY HELD THE ANSWER**, two tests below the flakiest one: *constructed rather than provoked … a test that sometimes lands between two classifications **tests the machine's scheduler rather than the classification***. `test_a_run_that_does_both_is_reported_as_mixed` provoked its condition with a real half-busy, half-sleeping block and failed **5/5** under load; its subject is the boundary arithmetic, so it now constructs an `OffCpuProfile` and **stays in the gate**, with the empirical claim kept beside it as a `timing` test rather than deleted. The rest are genuinely claims that a primitive *measures* what it says, and a constructed version would assert that arithmetic works on numbers the test wrote itself.

**A FOURTH MARKER, BECAUSE THE REASON IS A FOURTH REASON.** `pyproject.toml` already keeps `slow`, `docker`, `postgres` and `index` apart with a comment each; these are **not slow**, a daemon would not help, and they pass alone — what they need is a **quiet machine**, as much an environmental dependency as a Postgres server. The gate is now `-m "not slow and not timing"` and `-m "timing"` is the deliberate pass. **AC 2 IS MET BY THE PARTITION**: every absence-of-effect assertion against a fixed threshold — the two `assert not result.detectable`, the envelope control, the 20ms floor — is outside the gate and runs where its threshold means what it was set against. `certify` would give each a measured floor at 25 baseline samples plus 200 bootstrap trials; that is the right instrument for an investigation which certifies once and runs many experiments, and here it buys a much slower suite and the same answer. Recorded as the road not taken, because it is the fix this note proposed first.

**ONE SWEEP CAME BACK CLEAN AND WAS WRONG.** Launched from stdin, where Windows cannot re-import the main module to spawn a child: every spinner died instantly with `OSError: Errno 22` and three runs measured a quiet machine. Re-run from a file it found three failures immediately. `under_load` now yields **how many spinners are alive** rather than how many were asked for — a load generator that silently generates no load turns every sweep into a false negative, which is the same failure as an agent reporting a measurement it did not take.

**`tests/explorer/test_standup.py::test_ps_lists_what_is_running` is recorded and deliberately not marked** — it failed once at 2×, the mechanism was never characterised, and it is a `docker ps` subprocess whose failure is more likely a timeout. Marking it would be marking on a guess.

**Measured 2026-08-22**, during S-12.6, on `tests/primitives/test_isolation.py::test_a_component_sharing_nothing_shows_no_interference`:

| Scope | Result |
|---|---|
| the single test, five times | 5/5 pass |
| `tests/primitives/` alone (488 tests) | pass |
| the whole fast subset | **failed 2 of 3 runs** |

`isolation.py` imports `statistics`, `threading`, `time`, `measurement`, `registry` and `scope` — nothing the change in hand touched, which is how it was attributed. **Third sighting**, all in `tests/primitives/`: S-4.3's sabotage results were noise-dependent and a guard's removal *passed on a lucky afternoon*; 2026-08-17 saw two different off-CPU tests fail on one run and a third on the next.

**Fifth sighting, 2026-08-24**, gating S-13.5: `test_off_cpu.py::test_a_run_that_does_both_is_reported_as_mixed` **and** `test_perturbation.py::test_in_serial_code_the_sensitivity_is_just_the_share_of_runtime` failed together, and both passed standalone (40 passed). **A third file joins the family**, and neither module imports anything that change touched — `perturbation.py` imports no `bench` module at all. **Two sightings in two days, on two consecutive stories**, which is the rate this now costs: every landing needs a standalone re-run and a full re-run to tell a flake from a regression, and that is three subset runs at ~7 minutes each.

**Fourth sighting, 2026-08-23**, verifying `main` after S-13.7: `test_the_neighbour_actually_contended_with_is_named` failed in the full subset and passed **21/21 in its own file and 5/5 standalone**, with a diff entirely in `explorer/`, `orchestrator/` and `state/`. **The same test as 2026-08-17**, which says the family recurs rather than drifting — and it is the *other* shape in the same file: not asserting an effect is absent, but asserting **which** of three neighbours contended, which machine load reorders just as easily. **The cost is now visible per story rather than per epic**: every landing needs a standalone re-run to tell a flake from a regression, which is the check that keeps `CLAUDE.md`'s gate meaningful and the reason to spend the story.

Notes: **the shape is what makes it fragile** — `measure_interference(..., repetitions=5)` asserting `not result.detectable`, which is a real-timing measurement asserting an effect is *absent*. Machine load adds exactly the signal it is looking for. This is the same class the project flags in other people's code: a threshold that holds on a quiet afternoon. The options are to mark them `slow`, raise repetitions, or compare against a measured noise floor rather than a fixed ordering — **a decision, not an obvious fix**, which is why this is a story rather than a patch.

### S-8.12 — Widen the loop boundary
Depends: E8, E9
Why: `Executor` returns `Mapping[str, float]`, so everything a primitive knew *about* its numbers is discarded where the loop records them. Epic 9's composition check recorded this and it has never had a story; three of its six attacks answer `UNTESTED` because of it.
AC:
- What a primitive returns reaches the experiment log without being flattened to floats — `kinds` at minimum, and a `Fit` where the primitive produced one
- S-9.6's cheat detection, S-9.2's and S-9.4's sweep attacks run against a real log instead of reporting `UNTESTED` for want of an input
- `Experiment` stays JSON-representable, because a checkpoint that cannot serialize is a run that cannot resume (S-6.1)
- The loop still measures nothing: what widens is what it *carries*, not what it computes

**DONE (2026-08-25)** — `Measured` in `diagnosis/loop.py`, `kinds` and `fit` on `Experiment`, `fits_from`/`kinds_from` in `audit/compose.py`, the checkpoint round-trip in `adapters.py`, ADR 144, 5 new tests in `tests/audit/test_audit_composed.py` plus two inverted. All four AC met. **ONE RESULT TYPE AT THE BOUNDARY RATHER THAN A WIDER MAPPING.** A union of `Mapping[str, float] | Measured` would have avoided seventeen call sites and left two ways to say one thing — and worse, made *a primitive that fitted nothing* and *a caller that did not bother* the same value. With one type **absence is a statement**: an ablation sets `fit=None` because it drew no curve, and S-9.2 already refuses to judge a curve nobody drew. `kinds` is checked against the measurement, because a kind describing a number the experiment did not take is a claim about a measurement that does not exist. **THE LOOP CARRIES AND STILL MEASURES NOTHING** — AC 4 asserted as an absence, by inspection: `diagnosis/loop.py` contains no call to `fit_growth` and none to `metric_kind`, and a loop that could produce a fit would be the one place `CLAUDE.md`'s measurement rule is unenforceable. **DERIVING KINDS FROM THE NAME STAYS FORBIDDEN AND THE FIXTURE IS WHERE THAT BECOMES VISIBLE**: the thesis ablation reports `seconds.share_removed`, a *share of a duration* that `metric_kind` reads as a count, and the fixture now labels it from `result.kinds["seconds"]` — the primitive that produced it. **THE LOG IS THE SOURCE, SO `fits` AND `kinds` STOPPED BEING PARAMETERS.** Optional overrides would have left two answers to *what kind is this metric*, and they would disagree the first time one moved. `rerun` stays a parameter and the asymmetry is the point: re-running is the harness's, not the record's. **A CHECKPOINT CARRIES THEM OR A RESUMED RUN AUDITS WEAKER** — `_stored` keeps them and `_log_of` replays them; a `Fit` is a stdlib dataclass in a pydantic model and round-trips through JSON unchanged, so AC 3 needed no change to Epic 1, and a stored experiment stays under S-6.3's ~1 KiB. **THE EVIDENCE CHAIN'S GOLDEN FILE MOVED AND WAS REGENERATED DELIBERATELY**, as its own test asks — four lines, `"fit": null` and `"kinds": {}` per embedded experiment — and it is worth recording because the chain is the Surgeon's input, the Adversary's input and the PR body. **THE HEADLINE RESULT IS IN THE PROJECT'S OWN SHOWCASE**: the thesis diagnosis gained a **third objector** without changing its verdict, because the scale attack had been answering `NOT_RUN` all along and the thesis sweep is deliberately narrow. And an audit can now reach `sound` on what the log alone carries. **Two tests that asserted the gap were inverted, and their old docstrings were this story's specification** — the third time a test written to record a gap turned out to be the thing that closed it. `INCONCLUSIVE` stays reachable, and its test now withholds the one input the log genuinely cannot carry. **Sabotage: 3 properties, all caught.**

Notes: **do not confuse this with the share of cost, which is a different failure.** S-8.11 found that a share crosses the boundary perfectly well — a fraction is a float — and what it lacked was a name; `share_metric` fixed that. `kinds` and a `Fit` are not floats and cannot cross at all. The boundary is **narrow, not closed**, and only the second half needs this story. Deriving `kinds` from the metric name is worse than not running the attack: `metric_kind` defaults to `COUNT`, and a duration read as a count diverges on every re-run.

### S-12.4 — Human interrupt at ship
Depends: S-12.2
AC:
- `interrupt_before=["ship"]` at trust level 0
- The human sees the evidence chain, the patch, before/after measurements, and the Adversary verdict
- Resume after an arbitrary delay works

**DONE (2026-08-21)** — `src/coldfix/orchestrator/gate.py`, `gated` on `assemble`, ADR 130, 15 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_gate.py`. All three AC met. **THE STORY NAMES A CONDITION NOTHING CAN EVALUATE, AND S-13.4 ANSWERS IT**: the trust ledger is an epic away and `state/checkpoint.py` leaves it out deliberately (F5 — a rewind would restore the trust level preceding the failure that caused the rewind), but S-13.4's third criterion is *new projects start at level 0 regardless of cross-project history*, so **level 0 is the only value any project can be at and a gate conditioned on it is always on**. **THERE IS NO TRUST-LEVEL PARAMETER AND THE ABSENCE IS THE ENFORCEMENT** — `repair/slack.py`'s construction for the same reason. One would have exactly one reachable value, and the danger is not that nobody could flip it but that somebody **could**, turning the gate off with no ledger to justify it. What `assemble` gained is `gated: bool = True`, named for what it is for — a test needing a graph that runs to completion — rather than for a policy it does not implement. **THE DEFAULT FLIPPED AND NINETEEN CALL SITES MOVED**, which is the right direction for a safety property: the dangerous configuration is the one that has to be asked for. Three of S-12.3's resume tests were genuinely changed — those runs reach `ship` and now park — and compile ungated with a note, because they test a crash and what survives it. **A gated graph with no checkpointer is refused**: `interrupt_before` parks the run *in the checkpoint*, and with nowhere to park the approval a human gives on Thursday has nothing to return to. **PARKING IS VISIBLE IN THE PENDING TASK, NOT IN THE CHANNELS** — `progress_of` reads what a run *wrote* and parking writes nothing, so a state-only view cannot tell a parked run from one that ran `ship` and changed nothing; `waiting_at` asks the graph for its next task. **AN INCOMPLETE APPROVAL IS REFUSED, NOT RENDERED WITH BLANKS**: a person shown an empty evidence section reads it as *no evidence* rather than as *the report is broken*, and the first is a reason to reject a good patch. `NotAtTheGateError` is separate from `MissingInputError` because *has not reached ship* and *reached ship with nothing to show* are a run working and a defect. **The verdict shown is the last, not the first** — S-11.7 sends a broken patch back and round two appends another flag. **Two rendering rules stop a flattering report**: a metric measured on one side only says so rather than showing a fall to zero (*a metric that vanished is a measurement nobody took*), and a slack-reducing patch says so **first**, because §4 says prominently and a label under four screens of diff is not. **The measurements are taken once and read twice** — measuring again for the gate would put two disagreeing sets of figures under one patch and pay for the sweep twice. **AC 3 is tested by making the delay irrelevant rather than simulating one**: the run parks, every object is dropped, and a second `for_development` opens the same file — an hour and a fortnight are the same test. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught** (never interrupting fails three, rendering blanks fails one).

### S-12.5 — Early human checkpoint
Depends: S-12.4, S-9.8
Why: otherwise the human arrives after all cost is spent.
AC:
- Optional interrupt after the finding audit, before repair
- The human sees what was found and why, before any fix is attempted
- Enabled at trust level 0, skipped at higher levels

**DONE (2026-08-21)** — `Finding` and `found` in `orchestrator/gate.py`, `early_review` on `assemble`, ADR 131, 8 new tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_gate.py` (23 in the file). All three AC met. **THE WORD *OPTIONAL* IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** S-12.4's AC does not have it and this one does, and the asymmetry is deliberate rather than an oversight: the ship gate guards an **irreversible outward act** and has no off switch (ADR 130); this one guards a **budget**, so an operator running unattended may decline it and the worst case is euros. `early_review` exists and no `trust` parameter does, and the two facts are consistent rather than in tension. **`gated` IS THE MASTER SWITCH AND `early_review` NARROWS IT, WHICH IS NOT HOW THE FIRST DRAFT READ IT** — two independent booleans meant *no interrupts at all* took two arguments, and **fifteen existing tests said `gated=False` and parked at `repair` anyway**, which is the signature of a switch that does not do what its one obvious use implies. `gated=False` now removes every gate; `early_review=False` keeps the ship gate and drops the early one. **TWO REPORTS, AND NEITHER CARRIES THE OTHER'S EVIDENCE**: `Finding` has no patch field and nowhere to put one, because there is not a patch yet and an empty patch section invites the reader to answer *is this patch right* with the material for *is this worth trying to fix*. What it carries is `Routing.describe()` — the verdict, where it sends the run, and **why that rather than the obvious**, since S-9.8 recorded that `because` is not decoration: two of five routes are reached from more than one verdict. `spends_repair` rides along as the premise — a finding going back for more experiments spends no repair budget, so nothing is owed a decision and the report says so. **ONE RULE ABOUT WHICH FLAG TO READ, SHARED**: both gates take the **latest** of their kind, never the first, because S-11.7 and S-9.8 both send things back and a second round appends; `_latest` returns `None` rather than raising because *no flag of this kind* is a defect at one gate and *not there yet* at the other. **Sabotaging that rule fails a test at both gates, which is what confirms it is one rule and not two.** **The checkpointing suite is now ungated throughout** and says why: a parked run visits four nodes rather than seven, which would let *a checkpoint after every node* pass while proving it for under half of them. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught.**

### S-12.6 — Time travel
Depends: S-12.2, S-6.2
AC:
- Rewind to an earlier checkpoint restores checkpointed state
- **Persistent store is unaffected** — failure memory from the later state survives
- A test rewinds after a failed repair and asserts the agent does not repeat the same approach

**DONE (2026-08-22)** — `src/coldfix/orchestrator/rewind.py`, `remembered` on `repair`, ADR 132, 10 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_rewind.py` plus 4 in `tests/repair/test_repair_composed.py`. All three AC met. **F5 WAS MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS FIXED AND IT REPRODUCED**: a probe drove a run to completion, and at the checkpoint whose next step is `repair` the `attempts` channel is `[]` — resuming from there produced the approach the completed run had already recorded as failed. The test asserts that repetition rather than describing it. **THE FIRST FRAMING OF THE MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG AND THE CORRECTION IS THE FINDING**: the helper counted approaches appearing more than once *within* the attempts list and reported nothing, because **a rewind forks the history rather than appending to it** — the new branch starts from the checkpoint's values, so `attempts` holds one entry afterwards, not two, and nothing is repeated inside either branch. The repetition is *between* them, **which is exactly why F5 says nobody notices**. `already_failed(discarded, retried)` takes both sides. **SPLITTING THE STATE IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT**: S-6.1 and S-6.2 stop the knowledge being *destroyed* and do not put it back in front of the Surgeon, and `repair` began every call with no prior attempts — so a rewound run got the earlier code state and none of the later knowledge, which is the inversion F5 names. `remembered` feeds **what the Surgeon is shown** and **the repeat check**, and deliberately not the cap, the temperature ramp or the escalation report: those are facts about *this* repair, and charging a rewound one for attempts it is only being told about would let two rewinds exhaust a budget without the Surgeon writing a line. The repeat check compares **diffs, not the `approach` label** — F12's finding, that the label is self-judged and the agent can rename the same idea. **AC 2 IS PROVEN ON BOTH ARMS AND ONLY ONE IS INTERESTING**: a SQLite checkpoint file is trivially not the store and `refuse_shared_store` returns early on a `Path`; the arm worth testing is **Postgres**, which S-12.2 supports for concurrent campaigns and where the two could actually collide. **Rewind points are addressed by node rather than by checkpoint id** — somebody rewinds *to before the repair*, an id is a fact about one run and the node is what two runs have in common — and by the **first** such point, since a cycle visits `repair` more than once and rewinding to the second keeps the attempt the rewind is about. `history` reverses LangGraph's newest-first ordering once. **Sabotage: 3 properties, all caught.** **S-13.3 IS NOW LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN MERELY NEXT**: `remembered` is a parameter nothing fills — the adapters do not read `Collection.FAILURE_MEMORY` and nothing writes it — so until that story lands **a real rewound run still repeats itself**. The mechanism is in place and the wiring is not; `test_without_the_memory_the_same_run_ships_what_already_failed` is the control that keeps the gap visible.

---

**EPIC 12 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-22)** — `tests/orchestrator/test_orchestrator_composed.py`, ADR 133, 6 tests. Seven stories, and the epic's sentence is *durable execution across hours, crashes, and multi-day human gates*. **SIXTH CONSECUTIVE EPIC TO FIND A DEFECT AT A JOIN, AND THE FIRST WHOSE SHAPE IS DIFFERENT.** **`interrupt_before` parks a run *at* a node** — the checkpoint it stops on is the one whose next step is that node — and `invoke(None, config)` from that checkpoint is exactly what a human approving the gate does. S-12.6's `before` returned **that same checkpoint**, so the two calls were indistinguishable and **a rewind to a gate silently counted as approving it**: measured, a fresh gated run parks at `repair` while a rewind to the identical point ran `repair` *and* `audit_patch` and stopped at `ship`. Somebody rewinding to reconsider the direction got the repair re-run instead, unasked — the inverse of what a gate is for. **NEITHER STORY COULD HAVE FOUND IT ALONE**: S-12.4 and S-12.5 test gates on runs that were never rewound, and S-12.6 tests rewinds on graphs compiled `gated=False`, because a run that parks never reaches the nodes whose writes those tests check. The defect lives only where both are true. **The fix is in `before`**: for a gated node, target the checkpoint one earlier so the run *re-enters* the node and the interrupt fires as it does on any other entry — done only where a gate would otherwise be skipped, since re-entering re-runs the preceding phase and that is a model call, and `interrupt_before_nodes` is **asked of the compiled graph** rather than passed in. **`crashing_run.py` gained a fourth argument** so a *gated* run can be killed: S-12.3's tests need an ungated one and the composition needs the opposite, because a campaign that dies between an approval and the next gate is the ordinary case rather than an exotic one. **The three readers of a run are now compared** — `progress_of` counts writes, `waiting_at` asks the graph, `history` lists checkpoints, and nothing had checked they agree. **Sabotage: restoring the pre-fix `before` fails three of the six.** **THE PATTERN IS NEW AND WORTH NAMING**: the previous five checks all found *a value one story produces and another consumes with neither story's tests holding both ends*; here **no value crosses at all**. What the two stories share is a **call** — `invoke(None, config)` — that means two different things depending on which checkpoint the config names, and neither fixture could contain the other's configuration. S-12.3 recorded two members of that family already (`invoke(None)` resumes, `invoke(state)` restarts, both returning a final state); this is the third, and the tell is identical: **both calls succeed and only the outcome differs.**

---

# EPIC 13 — Memory and trust

### S-13.1 — Playbook store
Depends: S-6.2, S-7.1
AC:
- Entries keyed by framework fingerprint
- Structured: situation, action, outcome
- Retrieved into Explorer context at grounding

**DONE (2026-08-22)** — `src/coldfix/explorer/playbook.py`, the `playbook` argument on `ground_workload`, ADR 135, 10 tests in `tests/explorer/test_playbook.py`. All three AC met. **CHECKING FIRST FOUND MOST OF IT ALREADY BUILT**, which is the seventh story running that the habit has changed: AC 1 was done (`Fingerprint.playbook_key()` is framework-and-major-version, `playbook_from_store` reads by it, `Collection.PLAYBOOKS` has been a member since S-6.2), AC 3's seam existed and **nothing filled it**, and only the entry schema was genuinely missing. **THREE MODULES HAD DEFERRED THAT SCHEMA AND NONE OF THEM GUESSED**: `persistent.py` stores `(collection, key, entry)` and leaves the columns to Epic 13, `auth.PlaybookLookup` returns `Mapping`s and reads inside none of them, and `Resolution` carries what it was given **unread**. That restraint is why this story is small — the alternative, each module inventing the shape it needed, is the failure found at six consecutive epic joins. **SITUATION, ACTION, OUTCOME AND NO FOURTH**: the tempting one is `worked: bool` and it is exactly S-13.2's, with the counters that justify it; `extra="forbid"` refuses it rather than storing it to be believed later. **Whitespace is not content and `min_length` does not know that** — a single space satisfied `min_length=1`, found by a test that expected a refusal and did not get one, and closed the way `Implicated.reason` and `FalsificationTest` close it. **THERE IS NO PRODUCTION WRITER AND THAT IS THE STORY BOUNDARY**: `record` is the way in that S-13.2 will gate, nothing in `src/` calls it, and a writer before that gate would be *a wrong entry propagates silently to all future runs and compounds* with nothing to stop it. **The safety boundary was already drawn in code** — entries are consulted before probing and carried *unread*, so nothing believes one and S-13.1 can land without opening the window S-13.2 closes. **THE JOIN IS TESTED FIRST-CLASS BECAUSE S-13.3 LEARNED IT THE HARD WAY ONE DAY EARLIER**: `ground_workload` called `resolve_auth` with no key, so the consult never happened and not even `no_playbook` was reached; the test drives the composition and asserts the arguments `resolve_auth` was passed, stopping at that stage rather than building a Django project to reach it. `no_playbook` is the default rather than `None`, because a first run against a fresh store has learned nothing and S-13.5's learning curve depends on *consulted and empty* being distinguishable from *not consulted*. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught.**

### S-13.2 — Provisional writes and promotion — **SAFETY**
Depends: S-13.1
Why: a wrong entry propagates silently to all future runs and compounds.
AC:
- New entries are provisional and carry success/failure counters
- Promotion to trusted requires N successes **across different projects**
- Two failures demote and quarantine
- Entries are fingerprint-scoped, never global
- A test proves a wrong provisional entry does not affect a different project

**DONE (2026-08-22)** — `Status`, `Standing`, `note_use`, `standings` and `trusted` in `src/coldfix/explorer/playbook.py`, ADR 136, 7 tests in `tests/explorer/test_playbook.py` and 5 `postgres` ones in `tests/state/test_persistent_store.py`. All five AC met, **AC 5 against the real journal**. **F4'S FIRST CLAUSE IS IMPOSSIBLE AS WRITTEN**: it says entries *carry a use counter with success and failure tallies*, and `persistent.py` installs a **database trigger** refusing `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` — a counter on the entry could never be incremented. So a use is its own appended row and the tally is a fold over them, which is **a better answer than the one the audit imagined**: the evidence for a promotion is itself append-only, so nothing can quietly raise a count. A stored counter is one number anybody with a connection can set; a fold over immutable rows is a claim with its receipts attached. **QUARANTINE IS CHECKED BEFORE TRUST AND THE ORDER IS THE PROPERTY** — an entry that failed twice is quarantined however many successes it has, because F4's remedy for a poisoned entry is that it stops being offered and a rule where successes outweighed failures would let a widely-repeated mistake earn its way back, which is the compounding F4 names. **N IS THREE AND F4 DOES NOT FIX IT**: two is the weakest reading that satisfies *different projects*; three is the smallest that survives one coincidence, since two projects sharing a wrong belief is ordinary when both came from the same tutorial; and it **must exceed the demotion threshold**, or an entry with two successes and two failures is promotable and quarantined at once and the winner is decided by the order of two `if`s rather than by a reason — a test asserts the inequality rather than the numbers. **Promotion counts projects, not uses**, and `note_use` refuses an empty project: fifty successes on one project is one project's opinion, which is F15's trust-ledger finding reached from the playbook side. **The row and the thing are two schemas** — `PlaybookEntry` stays the three fields S-13.1 refused to add a fourth to, while the *row* carries a `kind` discriminator, explicit rather than inferred from shape so that a `use` is skipped and an unaccountable row still raises. **AC 5 uses F4's own poison**: project A learns *"DRF always uses TokenAuthentication"* and it works there once; project B shares the fingerprint, **sees** it through `recall` and gets nothing from `trusted` — readable as context, not actionable, which is the distinction `resolve_auth` was already built around. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught in the unit tests and in the store tests.** **Still no production writer** — the gate now exists, so wiring the Explorer to record what it learned is safe and is its own piece of work.

### S-13.3 — Failure memory
Depends: S-6.2
AC:
- Rejected approaches recorded per finding
- Carried into retry context
- Survives rewind

**DONE (2026-08-22) — all three AC met.** `src/coldfix/repair/memory.py`, `Resources.failures` and the wiring in `orchestrator/adapters.py`, ADR 134, 6 tests in `tests/repair/test_memory.py` plus 2 in `tests/orchestrator/test_adapters.py` and 2 `postgres` ones in `tests/state/test_persistent_store.py`. **TAKEN OUT OF BACKLOG ORDER because it closed a gap in shipped code**: S-12.6 added `remembered` to `repair` and left it empty, so a rewound run repeated the approach that had already failed — F5's defect, held open on purpose by a control test. `Collection.FAILURE_MEMORY` had existed since S-6.2 and **nothing had ever written to it**. **THE WHOLE ATTEMPT IS STORED, NOT THE APPROACH**: S-10.4 first showed retries only the previous `approach` strings, which F12 records as the self-judged field an agent can rename, and S-10.5's repeat check compares **diffs** — so a memory keeping only labels would feed that check the one field it cannot trust while looking correct in any test that merely asserted something was remembered. **The successful attempt is recorded too**, because S-11.7 can send a patch back after the Adversary breaks it and an approach that passed its own test then failed the audit is exactly what must not be re-proposed. **`Resources.failures` is required rather than optional** — a run without it repeats itself after a rewind, and an optional store is a switch that turns that guarantee off with nothing to justify it (S-12.4's argument about the ship gate). **THE THIRD SABOTAGE SURVIVED, AND IT WAS THE WIRING THIS STORY EXISTS TO ADD.** Removing `remembered=recall(...)` from the repair adapter changed no test outcome: `test_adapters.py` covered the translation helpers and `test_repair_composed.py` called `repair()` directly with `remembered=` supplied by hand, so **neither held both ends** — a story whose whole content is a join had no test of the join.
