Closed with two tests that drive `adapters.repair` with the epic calls recorded rather than run, which is the `_Generations` technique Epic 10 added for the identical reason one layer down (*sabotages that stopped showing prior attempts changed no outcome*). **Seventh consecutive time a passing sabotage has meant a missing test rather than a redundant guard.** **AC 3 WAS UNVERIFIED FOR AN HOUR AND THEN WAS NOT**: the journal is Postgres and Docker Desktop was not running, so the two tests that round-trip an `Attempt` through the real store were reported **skipped rather than passed**. Docker was then started and they pass — along with **93 `postgres`/`docker` tests** that had been skipping all session. Worth recording as a process note rather than a result: a suite that skips a whole marker is green in a way that means less than it looks, and the honest report says which. **`tests/fixtures/chains.py` was added** — the third copy of the same evidence-chain builder is what made it a fixture rather than a coincidence.

### S-13.4 — Trust ledger
Depends: S-6.2
AC:
- Levels 0–2 per fix category
- **Keyed by project shape characteristics, not category alone**
- New projects start at level 0 regardless of cross-project history
- Cross-project history shown as advisory context
- Any revert or rejection demotes one level

**DONE (2026-08-22)** — `src/coldfix/state/trust.py`, ADR 137, 13 tests in `tests/state/test_trust.py` and 5 `postgres` ones in `tests/state/test_persistent_store.py`. All five AC met, **AC 3 and AC 4 against the real journal**. **TWO OF F15'S OWN TERMS WERE UNDEFINED ANYWHERE** — *fix category* and *project shape characteristics* — and deciding them is most of this story. **THE SHAPE IS MEASURED AND THE UNIT IS AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE**: F15's worry is payload per row, `RESPONSE_BYTES` is a metric the harness already takes at every scale point, so *wide parent table* is a number this system has rather than a label somebody applies. `payload_magnitude` is `floor(log10(bytes per unit of scale))` — **a decade rather than a threshold, so nobody has to decide where *wide* begins**: a narrow/wide cut needs a number defended in a docstring and wrong for some project, while a decade makes the question *is this the same kind of project*, which is what F15 is asking. It reads the **widest** scale point, because a wide parent table at ten rows looks like a narrow one and the trade only becomes enormous at volume. A shape derived from nothing measured is refused — *a shape guessed here would be the label it replaces*. **FIX CATEGORY IS A SUPPLIED STRING, NOT AN INVENTED ENUM**: nothing enumerates categories, and a closed set would be a taxonomy with no source, wrong the first time a fix did not fit. **LEVEL IS DERIVED AND THE TWO HISTORIES ARE NEVER ADDED** — `Standing` carries this project's `accepted`/`demotions` and `elsewhere` as a separate field `level` does not read, which is the whole of F15's second sentence. **Every level test carries a non-zero `elsewhere`**, because a fixture where nobody else has history cannot tell *advisory* from *unused* — the weak-test shape this project has now recorded eight times. The store test uses F15's own example: fifty approvals across ten projects, and the newcomer is still `GATED`. **Three accepted per level, and F15 fixes only the demotion** — three for S-13.2's reason (smallest that survives one coincidence), which prices level 2 at six clean fixes in one category on one shape. `Outcome.demotes` names `REJECTED` and `REVERTED` together rather than deriving from `not ACCEPTED`, so a fourth outcome has to state which it is. **`state` does not reach up**: it imports only `cost`, `replay` and `sandbox`, so `Shape` takes plain values and whichever caller holds a `Fingerprint` and a `Workload` assembles them. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught at unit level and against the store.** **NOTHING CONSULTS THE LEDGER YET** — S-12.4 and S-12.5 hardcode their gates to *always* on ADR 130's correct reasoning that level 0 was the only reachable value while no ledger existed. That is no longer true, and wiring the gates to `standing(...).level` is a change to a safety property that belongs in its own story. **A ledger that exists and is not read is exactly as safe as no ledger, and exactly as useless.**

Notes: a `select_related` fix approved 50 times on narrow-table projects may be wrong on a wide-table project. Trust learned elsewhere is context, not authority.

### S-13.5 — Learning curve measurement
Depends: S-13.1
AC:
- Explorer steps to first runnable workload recorded per project
- Plotted against number of projects with that fingerprint
- Should decline; if it does not, memory is not working
- **A controlled ablation of the playbook itself**: ground the same unseen repository with the playbook retrieved and with it withheld, and report the difference in stage completion and steps-to-ground

**BLOCKED (2026-08-23) — nothing was built, and the reason is two measurements that cannot be taken.** **THERE IS NO SUCH THING AS AN EXPLORER STEP YET.** AC 1 records *Explorer steps to first runnable workload per project*, and `GroundingRun` — the driver that accumulates `attempts` — is constructed in `tests/explorer/test_run.py` and **in no module under `src/`**. `agents/roles.py` has been recording this since the role index was written: `Agent.EXPLORER` carries `attributed=False` and the note *no call site in `src/` names it … either the loop that drives it is not built, or its calls are billed to nobody.* S-7.13's `ground_workload` runs the nine stages mechanically, once each, with no retries and no model calls — so *steps to ground* is a constant, and a constant plotted against project count is not a curve. AC 1 and AC 2 have nothing to record. **AND THE ABLATION WOULD MEASURE ZERO BY CONSTRUCTION.** AC 4 compares grounding *with the playbook retrieved and with it withheld* — and **nothing reads a playbook entry**. `resolve_auth` attaches them to its `Resolution` and mints the credential from `recipe`; `playbook_entries` appears three times in `src/` and every one is a store, a count in a `describe` string, or the assignment itself. That is deliberate — S-13.1 gives entries meaning and S-13.2 decides when one may be believed, and `Resolution`'s own docstring says they are *carried unread* — but it means the withheld and retrieved conditions are identical runs. **Reporting *the playbook adds nothing* from that would be true and actively misleading**: it would be a finding about the wiring wearing the costume of a finding about the playbook, which is exactly the confound the ablation exists to remove. **A harness that can only ever measure zero was not built.** This project has already recorded two things that are *designed and unreachable* — `ExperimentRef` (ADR 129) and `gates_for` (ADR 138) — and a third would be a pattern rather than an accident. **S-7.14 and S-13.7 are the unblocking stories.** **The AC themselves are sound**; what is missing is underneath them.

**DONE (2026-08-24)** — `src/coldfix/eval/learning.py`, `schedule` extracted into `bench/interleaving.py`, ADR 141, 31 tests in `tests/eval/test_learning_curve.py`. All four AC met. **CHECKING FIRST CHANGED THE INSTRUMENT, AND THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN DOCSTRING IS WHAT CHANGED IT.** The obvious comparison for two groups of numbers is `rank_test`, and it says: *on heavily tied data — a metric taking three distinct values — it runs about 30% the other way, **which is the unsafe direction**. Counts are the tied case, and counts do not need this test.* **Steps-to-ground is a count.** Reaching for the nearest available comparison would have produced p-values biased towards *inventing* a difference, in the one study whose whole job is to be able to report there is none. What replaces it is the shape the interleaving already provides: rounds are **paired** — same repository, both ways, adjacent in time — so the statistic is a **sign test** over rounds and the interval is Wilson's over that proportion, reusing `eval/ablation.py`'s rather than adding a second one. The lower bound must **clear a half**, not merely lead: six wins in ten runs from 26% to 88%, which is the Adversary study's third test in the form a paired study takes. **THE CURVE REPORTS AND THE ABLATION CONCLUDES**, which is the note's own instruction — the series declines if the playbook works *and also* if later projects were easier, so `Curve` has no verdict claiming causation and `describe()` **says in its output that it is not evidence the playbook caused it**, naming the ablation instead. **THE GUARD IS CHECKED BEFORE THE STEP COUNT AND RETURNS BEFORE IT.** `CLAUDE.md`'s *queries down while rows explode is not an improvement* reads here as: a run that took fewer steps because it gave up three stages earlier has not learned anything. Reporting a fallen guard *alongside* a step delta would put a number next to the reason it means nothing, so `GUARD_FELL` returns first — and **both tests for that shape are written so the step count improves** (the curve halves its steps while grounding four stages instead of nine; the ablation wins every round). A study reading AC 1's figure alone would call each of them its best result. **BOTH ARMS ARE CALLABLES AND ARE RUN HERE** — S-1.6's construction borrowed wholesale, and sharper: a recorded step count would predate the very entries under test. `rounds` is floored at `MINIMUM_GROUP_SIZE` and checked **before anything runs**, because each round is two full groundings. **`schedule` MOVED INTO `bench/interleaving.py`**, where that module's docstring already says the order is what it owns; a copied loop would be a second answer to *what a fair schedule is*. **Process state is a column and nothing filters on it** — the note forbids S-0.4's fixed warm-up discard, and Barrett et al. is why. **Four refusals in the constructor**, the load-bearing one being **a withheld arm that was offered entries**: that is the confound the ablation exists to remove, and a study carrying it would compare the playbook against itself. **THIS HARNESS HAS NO PRODUCTION CALLER AND THAT IS THE CATEGORY RATHER THAN A GAP** — `eval/ablation.py` established it an epic earlier: an evaluation study is driven by a person running it, and `study()` there is imported only by its tests. Different from `ExperimentRef` and `gates_for`, which are machinery the *pipeline* would use. **Sabotage: 5 properties, all 5 caught.**

**UNBLOCKED (2026-08-23) by S-7.14 and S-13.7, both landed the same day.** *Steps to ground* is a variable now — the loop takes one step per command it proposes, `Exploration.steps` reports it and the `ground` node writes it as `grounding_steps` — so AC 1 and AC 2 have something to record. And **the ablation can measure something other than zero**: a trusted entry changes what `resolve_auth` does with a `401` that names no scheme, so the retrieved and withheld conditions are different runs. Both blockers are gone; what remains is S-13.5's own harness, which is the story itself. **Its AC were sound throughout** — the note below was about what sat underneath them.

Notes: the curve is longitudinal and confounded — it declines if the playbook works, and also if later projects happen to be easier. The ablation is the causal measurement, and it is the project's own core primitive applied to the project. It also serves as a regression test: if a playbook edit stops helping, the delta shrinks and that is visible. S-0.4 supplies the method — interleave conditions and require a guard counter (here, stage completion) to move alongside the timing. **Do not copy S-0.4's fixed warm-up discard**: that spike hard-coded one, which S-1.2 forbids for good reason (Barrett et al. found at most 43.5% of VM/benchmark pairs reach steady state at all, so 'discard the first N' is an assumption that is wrong more often than not). Record whether each sample ran in a fresh or reused process and let the analysis decide.

---

### S-13.6 — Read the ledger and write the playbook — **SAFETY**
Depends: S-13.1, S-13.2, S-13.4
Why: three stores now exist that nothing reads or writes. A ledger that is not consulted is exactly as safe as no ledger and exactly as useless, and a playbook nothing records never bends S-13.5's curve.
AC:
- The ship gate and the early checkpoint condition on `trust.standing(...).level` rather than on *always*
- Level 2 does not clear a `slack-reducing` patch — `00-BRIEF.md` §4 holds at any level
- The Explorer records what it learned as a **provisional** playbook entry, through S-13.2's gate
- A patch that ships and a patch a human declines both reach `record_outcome`
- A test proves a project at level 0 is still gated when another project is at level 2

**DONE (2026-08-22)** — `gates_for` in `orchestrator/gate.py`, the ship node's refusal and outcome record in `adapters.py`, `writer`/`learned_from_auth` in `explorer/playbook.py` wired into `ground_workload`, ADR 138, 4 tests in `test_gate.py`, 2 in `test_adapters.py` and 3 in `test_playbook.py`. All five AC met. **ADR 130'S REFUSAL WAS RIGHT AND IS NOW SPENT**: it declined a `trust` parameter because one would have had a single reachable value that a caller could flip with nothing to justify it — and a level is now a thing a project *earned*, recorded append-only, obtainable only through `standing`. **THE EARLY GATE OPENS ONE LEVEL BEFORE THE SHIP GATE**, which is ADR 131's asymmetry spent rather than restated: the early one guards a budget and the ship gate guards an irreversible outward act, so the cheaper protection drops first. **§4 IS ENFORCED IN THE NODE, BECAUSE THE GATE IS COMPILED BEFORE THE PATCH EXISTS.** `interrupt_before` is compile-time (S-12.2: there is no runtime equivalent), a level is available then and **a patch is not** — so a graph compiled for a `TRUSTED` project has no ship gate at all, and `00-BRIEF.md` §4 requires review for a slack-reducing patch *at any trust level*. The ship node refuses one outright and escalates, whatever `gates_for` returned; nothing is invalidated and no outcome is recorded, because nothing shipped. **LangGraph's dynamic `interrupt()` was measured and deliberately not used** — it would express a runtime gate directly, but resuming one needs `Command(resume=...)` rather than `invoke(None, config)`, which would change `resume`, `waiting_at` and the `before` fix ADR 133 built on `interrupt_before` semantics. A node that declines to ship gets the same guarantee without touching three modules and a safety property, and it is where the rule belongs: the patch is refused by the thing that would have shipped it. `REVIEWED_AT_EVERY_LEVEL` lives beside `LABEL` in `slack.py` so the node enforcing it and the report explaining it read **one sentence**. **A SHIPPED PATCH MOVES THE LEDGER, OR THE LEDGER NEVER MOVES** — without a writer `gates_for` could only ever read `GATED`. **`Resources.failures` became `Resources.store`**, because by now it is one journal answering three questions and a field named for one of them is one the next caller looks past. **The playbook writer records only what auth established**, after that stage rather than at the end: F4's poison is a claim about what a project of a kind *requires*, and a run that fails later has still learned whether this kind needs a credential. Everything written is **provisional structurally** — `writer` appends and nothing else, and there is no argument through which something could be written already believed. **Sabotage: 3 properties, all caught.** **S-6.3'S NAMED-PARAMETER TRAP APPEARED A FOURTH TIME**: `written.append` does not satisfy `PlaybookWriter`, because the protocol declares a named parameter and `list.append`'s is positional-only. **Nothing in `src/` calls `gates_for` yet** — the campaign entry point that would is S-17.1's, and this story stops at making the wiring possible and provable rather than inventing a caller for it.

Notes: **the two halves are one story because each is unsafe alone.** Wiring the gates without a writer means levels never rise and the gates never open, which is harmless but pointless; wiring a writer without the gates means entries accumulate that nothing ever consults. What makes this SAFETY rather than plumbing is the first two criteria: they are the only place in the system where a human review can be *skipped*, and ADR 130 refused to build that switch until a ledger could justify it. The refusal was right and it is now spent — so the check that replaces it has to be the thing tested, not the thing assumed.

### S-7.14 — The Explorer loop, in `src/`
Depends: E7, S-7.13
Why: `Agent.EXPLORER` is declared and unattributed, `GroundingRun` is constructed only in tests, and S-13.5 has no *steps* to count because nothing takes any.
AC:
- A function under `src/` drives `GroundingRun` — proposing, attempting, observing — until a workload is emitted or the run gives up
- Its model calls are attributed to `Agent.EXPLORER`, and `agents/roles.py` records `attributed=True` with a prompt it owns
- Steps to first runnable workload are readable off the result
- The stage budget, the stall check and S-5.4's sixty-step cap are the three bounds `GroundingRun` already refuses to be built without

**DONE (2026-08-23)** — `explorer/proposal.py` and `explorer/loop.py`, the `ground` node rewired in `orchestrator/adapters.py`, ADR 139, 29 tests in `tests/explorer/test_loop.py` plus 4 in `test_adapters.py`, 2 in `test_run.py` and the roles index corrected. All four AC met. **THE LOOP SITS ABOVE `ground_workload` AND THE PARTITION IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** Six of ADR 009's nine predicates are facts about the *environment* that the mechanical sequence never establishes and cannot — a checkout, an importable framework, a configuration the framework accepts, a database that answers, applied migrations, an enumerable route — and each has commands that make it true. The other three are the sequence's: `resolve_auth` mints the credential, `verify_work` seeds both scale points and drives the route. So the loop repairs the six and then runs the sequence once, and `REPAIRABLE | ESTABLISHED_BY_THE_SEQUENCE == set(Stage)` is asserted so a tenth stage cannot fall out of both. **`seed` IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG**: it sits *ahead of* `endpoint` in the stage order, so a migrated and deliberately empty repository — which is exactly Epic 7's composed subject — reports `seed` as `first_incomplete`, and a loop reading that number spends its budget filling a database the sweep fills correctly thirty seconds later and then measures a scale nobody asked for. `blocking()` returns the first incomplete **repairable** stage; sabotaging it back to `first_incomplete` fails a unit test *and* sends the end-to-end run round `auth` eight times. **A REFUSAL FROM THE SEQUENCE ENDS THE RUN RATHER THAN STARTING A REPAIR** — when all six hold and `ground_workload` still says no, what it refuses is about the repository's *content*, and no command at `connect` or `migrate` produces a drivable route. **THE DEFECT THIS STORY FOUND HAD SWITCHED OFF ONE OF THE THREE BOUNDS AC 4 NAMES.** `Session.run` recorded a step for any phase whose cap is counted in `StepUnit.STEP` — grounding and nothing else — and `GroundingRun.attempt` records one too. Two records per turn halve the sixty-step cap, and they do worse to the stall: `record_step` with **no conclusion clears the run of repeats**, so one model call between two attempts reset the counter every turn and fifteen identical stage reports could never accumulate. S-7.10's second bound was decoration in the first system that used it. The fix is not to make the two agree — two records carrying the same digest halve both deliberately-chosen numbers — but to delete the recording, which makes the rule uniform and is what the other five phases already did: **the session bills, the phase's owner counts.** `conclusion` left `Session.run` with it. **Sabotage: restoring that one line fails both bound tests** (the cap stops at one turn of three; the stall never fires and the reason changes to *whole budget*). **THE REPLY IS ARGV AND NOT PROSE**, because `04-cost.md` §3's mechanical check for this step is the command's exit code and a free-text instruction moves that check to whoever interprets it. **There is no `validate` and not for S-8.1's reason** — this step *has* a validator, but the exit code is known only after the command runs and S-5.6 validates the reply; the loop uses the same check where it is available, by feeding a failed command into the next question. **`Hands` IS SUPPLIED, NEVER HELD**: `03-agents.md` §2.5 puts the denylist, the blocked egress and the workspace confinement on the container, and a loop with its own executor is a second place all three must exist — asserted by inspection. **`finish` IS CALLED RATHER THAN TRUSTED AROUND**, which needed `Grounded` to carry the verification and the reset proof: `finish` takes the measurement and the proof, not the document they produced, and the two alternatives were re-running a sweep already paid for or **a second success path** past the one S-7.10 AC 5 built to be the only one. Sabotage: returning `grounded.emitted` instead passes the success test and fails *a workload whose data no longer exists*. **THE PRODUCTION CALLER IS `adapters.ground`**, which now calls `explore` by name — the fourth designed-and-unreachable thing was the risk and it was checked for. `Resources` gained `root`, `python` and `hands`; **the session is the node's**, from `sessions(_EXPLORER_PROMPT)`, because `Sessions` keys on the prompt `refuse_shared_session` compares and a loop making its own would be the one agent whose prefix nobody checked. A repository that will not ground writes a `null_result`. **`grounding_steps` IS WRITTEN AND S-13.5 HAD NOTHING TO READ BEFORE.** Also: `GroundingRun` keeps the report its last attempt was judged against (`measured`), so the loop routes on the reading the bounds were enforced against rather than a second one taken with nothing having happened in between — counted rather than asserted, at four evaluations for three turns against seven. And **closing the Explorer's gap made `describe`'s unattributed line unreachable**, so it took the index as a parameter — `role_of`'s argument, arriving at the second function that needed it.

Notes: **this is the third instance of the pattern S-7.13 and S-8.11 closed** — an epic's mechanism built, tested, and never given a production caller. `run.py` already refuses a budget whose `stall_after` is not grounding's, so the driver has the bounds; what it lacks is the loop and the prompt. `00-BRIEF.md` §5 step 5 calls grounding *the step the project's viability turns on*, and it is currently the one phase with no agent behind it.

### S-13.7 — Act on a trusted playbook entry
Depends: S-13.2, S-13.6
Why: entries are written, promoted and carried — and read by nothing. Until one can change an outcome, S-13.5's ablation compares a run with itself.
AC:
- `resolve_auth` consults `trusted(...)` before probing and may act on what it finds
- A **provisional** entry is never acted on, only carried — S-13.2's boundary, unchanged
- Acting on an entry is recorded through `note_use`, so a wrong one demotes itself
- A test proves the same repository resolves differently with a trusted entry present and absent

**DONE (2026-08-23)** — `TrustedLookup`/`trusted_from_store`/`actionable`/`Established.REMEMBERED` in `auth.py`, `remembered_requirement` and `UseRecorder`/`uses` in `playbook.py`, the use recorded in `compose.py`, the four seams wired in `adapters.ground`, ADR 140, 21 tests in `tests/explorer/test_trusted.py`, 5 in `test_adapters.py` and 2 in `test_persistent_store.py`. All four AC met. **TWO LOOKUPS, AND THE SEPARATION IS AT THE TYPE LEVEL.** `PlaybookLookup` is unchanged and still carried unread — provisional entries, context, what the Explorer is *shown*; `TrustedLookup` returns only what `standings` promoted and is the only list a decision may rest on. A single lookup with a filter applied by its caller would put the safety decision at every call site, and the note's warning is exactly that: **acting on `recall` is the mistake**, because it returns provisional entries too. **THE ONE PLACE A MEMORY DECIDES IS A GAP AND NEVER A DISAGREEMENT**: a `401` with no `WWW-Authenticate` is the route saying *something is enforcing authentication and I will not say what* — a credential is known to be needed, `UNKNOWN` is not mintable, and **that repository does not ground at all today**, which is what makes AC 4 a visible difference rather than a subtle one. Every other verdict is a measurement of *this* route and a prior about projects of its kind does not override one; an inconclusive probe (`404`, `500`) is refused separately, because `Requirement`'s own docstring sends the Explorer to a different path rather than to a user nobody asked for. **`Established` GAINS `REMEMBERED`, THE WEAKEST OF THE THREE** — the enum already refuses to merge a declaration with an observation, and a prior about a *kind* of project is weaker than either. **TWO TRUSTED ENTRIES NAMING DIFFERENT SCHEMES ARE REFUSED RATHER THAN RESOLVED**: each was earned on three different projects, so a disagreement is evidence the fingerprint does not determine the answer, and picking one is S-7.13's alphabetical tie-break. Refusing on *more than one scheme* rather than *more than one entry* is the part that matters — a lesson learned twice still acts. **THE USE IS RECORDED WHERE THE ANSWER IS, WHICH IS NOT WHERE THE DECISION WAS.** `resolve_auth` is the tempting place and the wrong one: a mint that succeeds says a user was created, not that the route accepted the credential, and F4's poison mints perfectly well in a session-authenticated project and then collects a `403` on every request — three successes, never demoted. So `Resolution` reports `acted_on` and the composition records the use once the workload has been driven, **on both outcomes**, and only for failures the credential could have caused: `WorkVerificationError` and `EmissionError` are *the work did not hold up*, while an interpreter that would not start travels without a use, because two failures quarantine and spending one on a fact about the machine demotes a memory that was right. **THE SEAMS TAKE THE KEY, AND NOT TAKING IT IS WHY THEY HAD NEVER BEEN FILLED**: `writer(store, key)` needed `Fingerprint.playbook_key()`, which is derived *inside* the sequence, so a caller could only bind one by fingerprinting the repository itself first — the one place two spellings of a key can disagree. `PlaybookLookup` had it right from the start. **`adapters.ground` NOW WIRES ALL FOUR**, from `Resources.store` and `Resources.project`: the campaign owns the repository, the run owns the journal, and the node reduces the result to the no-argument callable the loop takes. `playbook_from_store` had been unreachable since S-13.1 and `writer` since S-13.6. **Sabotage: 4 properties, 2 caught first time** — and both survivors were **the join rather than the helpers**, which is S-13.3's lesson for the ninth and tenth time: the failure half of the use recording asserted only that the error travelled, and the node test asserted a callable was wired rather than *which list it read*. Both now fail three ways.

Notes: **the safety argument for reading is the one S-13.2 built.** F4's poison propagates because *nothing validates a write*; three different projects agreeing is the validation, and `trusted` is the only list a caller may act on. What this story must not do is act on `recall` — that returns provisional entries too, and it is the list the Explorer sees as *context*. The distinction already exists in code; this is the story that depends on it being right.

# EPIC 14 — Adapters and MCP

### S-14.1 — Adapter interface
Depends: E1–E4
AC:
- Formal interface: `discover_workloads`, `seed`, `run_workload`, `run_tests`, `read_source`, `apply_patch`, `reset_state`, `capabilities`
- Declares hook points, framework-internal frames, protected paths, ORM dialect
- Defined as a Protocol with full typing

**DONE (2026-08-26)** — `adapters/interface.py`, ADR 146, 27 tests in `tests/adapters/test_interface.py`. All three AC met. **THE INTERFACE IS EXTRACTED, NOT INVENTED**: every one of the eight operations already exists as Django-specific code in `src/` and every one of the four declarations is already *consumed* by a core type that receives it by hand — the module docstring carries both tables. **NOTHING IN `src/` BUILDS ANY OF THE FOUR TODAY**: no `ProjectProfile` is constructed outside tests, no `Localizer` is constructed anywhere at all, no query hook is registered in production, and every `PatchPolicy` is the bare default. The codebase had been writing this story's absence down for eleven epics — `counting.py` says counters *attach through hooks a framework adapter declares (S-14.1)*, `normalize`'s `deny` is *the adapter's list*, `PatchPolicy` says *an adapter is the eventual source of the project-specific entries*, and `Framework.supported` names the three Django-specific things as *the adapter, the reset strategies and the query counter*. **THE DECLARATIONS RETURN THE CORE'S OWN TYPES** — `patch_policy()` a `PatchPolicy`, `localizer()` a `Localizer`, `run_workload` the `Drive` screening reads, `seed` exactly what `work.Seeder` returns — because an adapter with its own vocabulary for a thing that already has one puts a translation where a safety rule has to survive. **AN ADAPTER MAY ADD PROTECTED PATHS AND MAY NOT REMOVE ONE**: `patch_policy()` concatenates onto `DEFAULT_PROTECTED_PATTERNS`, there is no argument that narrows it, and the natural wrong implementation — take the declaration as the policy — drops seventeen defaults when an adapter declares two, whose visible symptom is a patch that edits the test suite and applies cleanly. **THE WRITE GOES THROUGH THE SESSION**: `apply_patch` and `read_source` take a `CandidateSession`, so S-2.3 survives the new seam as a *type error*; said plainly in the docstring that this constrains the interface and not the implementer, because an adapter is arbitrary Python and what it cannot be is *handed* an unguarded writer. **`reset_state` IS A PROVIDER, NOT THE ACT, AND THE NAME IS THIS AC'S** — S-2.7 will not trust a reset it has not driven ten times (S-0.5: rollback alone failed 10/10 while passing its story's check) and §1.5 requires a *fallback*, so it returns the mechanisms cheapest-first, which is `choose_reset`'s existing shape. **CAPABILITIES ARE SPLIT AND THE HARNESS HALF IS DERIVED** — four are an adapter's (event counters, fixture seeding, fixture shaping, state reset) and the complement is computed, so a thirteenth `Capability` is a classification rather than an omission; *enforcing* that an adapter claims only its four is S-14.4's, because a Protocol cannot constrain a return. **NOT `@runtime_checkable`** — `isinstance` checks that eight names exist and nothing about their signatures. **HALF THIS STORY IS CHECKED BY MYPY AND THAT IS DELIBERATE**: the conformance assertions are annotated assignments and `type: ignore` comments, and `warn_unused_ignores` is part of strict — widen `apply_patch` to `Session` and the ignore on a diagnostic session goes unused and the gate goes red. **That sabotage passes pytest and fails mypy**, which is the concrete argument for the gate being four commands. `AdapterError` was written and deleted: every failure here already has an owner. **Sabotage: 7 properties, 7 caught.**

### S-14.2 — Django adapter
Depends: S-14.1
AC:
- Implements the full interface for Django + DRF + Postgres
- Query hook via `execute_wrapper`
- Works on the target repo and the holdout repo

**DONE (2026-08-26, ADR 147) — AC 1 and 2 met, AC 3 HALF MET DELIBERATELY.** `adapters/django.py`, plus `declared_test_runner` extracted in `explorer/fingerprint.py`, 28 tests in `tests/adapters/test_django.py` (3 marked `postgres`). **SEVEN OF THE EIGHT OPERATIONS ARE TRANSCRIPTIONS** of code Epic 7 and Epic 2 already tested, so their tests assert *that the delegation happens and with what* rather than re-testing `drive`. **THE EIGHTH IS THE HOOK, AND IT HAD A DECISION IN IT THE AC DOES NOT MENTION.** `db.query`'s amount is *rows returned by that statement*, `execute_wrapper` reads `cursor.rowcount`, and **whether that means anything depends on the backend — measured, both**: Postgres reports `3` for a three-row `SELECT` and a true `0` for an empty one; **SQLite reports `-1` for every `SELECT`**, so `-1` (no row count, which DDL gives everywhere) and a real empty result are indistinguishable there. **A BACKEND THAT CANNOT REPORT ROWS IS REFUSED, NOT RECORDED AS ZERO** — `max(rowcount, 0)` makes `db.rows` read flat while rows grow, which is the guard-counter failure `CLAUDE.md` names in its own words; recording `1.0` instead is quieter and worse, because `db.query.total` would equal the query count and `measure_once` already calls that *a plausible number and the wrong one*. `ROW_COUNTING_VENDORS` is a measured list; an unmeasured vendor is refused rather than guessed either way. A SQLite project loses this hook and keeps `drive`'s count. **`execute_wrapper` AND ADR 008's `force_debug_cursor` ARE NOT COMPETITORS**: `drive` counts inside the subject's interpreter and still does, and the in-process hook is the only one of the two that can produce **per-event stacks** — `connection.queries` is read after the fact and a stack must be walked *inside* the call, so without a callback-shaped hook **there is no S-3.9 localization on Django at all**. That is the real argument for AC 2. **NOTHING UNDER `src/` IMPORTS DJANGO AT MODULE LEVEL** — the import is inside `query_hook` and a test imports the module in a fresh interpreter and asserts `django` did not arrive with it; one mypy override for `django.*` alone, never globally. **`suite_command` ASKS S-7.1 RATHER THAN ANSWERING AGAIN**: the first draft read `fingerprint().test_runner`, which returns `Unsupported` for a repository whose manifests do not name Django — so a project declaring `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` and nothing else fell through to `manage.py test` and ran a different set of tests. `_identify_test_runner` already had all three cases; it now takes a plain `Framework` and is exposed as **`declared_test_runner`** (not `test_runner`: pytest collects on the prefix, which is why `TestRunner.__test__ = False` sits one line above). Only `SUITE_COMMANDS` stays here. **`capabilities()` IS A FUNCTION OF WHAT THE ADAPTER WAS GIVEN**, not a constant — no seeder and no target means no `FIXTURE_SEEDING`, because claiming it gets scaling *offered* and then failed at seeding instead of withheld with a reason; and `FIXTURE_SHAPING` needs a `target` specifically, since only synthesis takes a distribution and a factory's shape is not a *chosen* one. **AC 3: the target repo and the reserve are exercised; THE HOLDOUT IS NOT TOUCHED.** S-0.6 designates it as never used during development, `tests/test_holdout_discipline.py` enforces it, and **S-17.1 is where that half is earned** — spending the project's only measurement of generalization on a green checkbox is the trade this refuses. Container restart is deliberately not among the reset candidates: it needs a container name, image and seed SQL, none of which is a fact about Django. **Sabotage: 10 properties, 10 caught** — and the eighth found a weak assertion first, because dropping the interpreter left `routes_are_complete` false either way and `Resolution.error` is the only place *not attempted* and *asked and refused* differ.

### S-14.3 — Second adapter
Depends: S-14.2
AC:
- SQLAlchemy or Flask adapter implementing the same interface
- **Core code unchanged** — asserted by a test that runs both adapters through the same pipeline

**DONE (2026-08-26, ADR 148)** — `adapters/flask.py` (Flask + SQLAlchemy), `tests/adapters/test_flask.py` (27) and `tests/adapters/test_both.py` (25). Both AC met, with the scope named rather than blurred. **THE PIPELINE IS THE INTERFACE'S OPERATIONS, NOT THE GROUNDING SEQUENCE.** No file outside `adapters/` changed, and the core turns two entirely different mechanisms into the same artifacts — a route table *asked of* the framework versus decorators *read off* files, an `execute_wrapper` versus an `Engine`-class event listener, one `Enumeration`, one `db.query`, one `Drive`, one `PatchPolicy`. **What is NOT claimed: grounding does not run on Flask** — `compose.py` calls `enumerate_entry_points` directly, `stages.PREDICATES` has one entry and `Framework.supported` is Django only. Those are *correct today* and **making `fingerprint` accept Flask without the rest would be worse than leaving it**: the refusal would move from an accurate *not a supported framework* to a `KeyError` on `PREDICATES[Framework.FLASK]` one call later. Filed on S-14.5, including that replacing `supported` with *an adapter exists for it* needs a registry that cannot live in `explorer/` — adapters import it, so the decision moves to the campaign. **THE DIFFERENCES ARE THE EVIDENCE.** *No synthesis*: `explorer/synthesis.py` introspects Django models, so `FlaskAdapter` has no `target`, seeds only from a supplied mechanism and never claims `FIXTURE_SHAPING` — and the shared pipeline shows the consequence, a primitive **withheld with a reason naming the missing capability** instead of offered and failed at seeding, which is the tri-state design paying for itself across two adapters for the first time. *Read, not resolved*: `Enumeration.resolution` records that the framework was never asked, so a conditionally-registered blueprint is missing **and known to be missing**. *A listener, not a wrapper*: `after_cursor_execute` fires on the `Engine` class and so catches engines created later, which is exactly the limitation the Django hook must document. **Their `reset_state` is identical, and that is a finding**: resetting Postgres is a fact about Postgres and a rollback knows nothing about which ORM wrote the rows. **`ROW_COUNTING_VENDORS` moved to `interface.py`** — the second adapter needing the same answer for a different ORM settles that it is a property of the database. **`query_hook` GAINED `aliases`, AND A TEST FORCED IT**: Django settings are process-global and **`override_settings` does not swap a connection** (it warns, and the handler keeps returning the original vendor) — measured, after two fixtures in one module configured different databases and the second silently got the first one's, undetected because `-m postgres` deselected the fixture that ran first. Both backends are now configured at once and each test names its alias; the parameter is ADR 008's *each connection under measurement* made a stated decision, and an alias Django does not have is refused rather than skipped. **TWO MECHANICAL ASSERTIONS CARRY AC 2**, because prose cannot check it and both ways of faking it are invisible in a diff that also adds a real adapter: `run_pipeline`'s own source is read back and must name neither framework, and `src/coldfix/` is walked with **`ast`** (not grep — a docstring naming the package is not an import) asserting no core module imports an adapter. **Flask and SQLAlchemy are dev dependencies** on the terms already recorded for Django/DRF/factory-boy. A leaked container taught a fixture rule: cleanup that can itself fail goes in its own `try`, ahead of the cleanup that must not be skipped. **Sabotage: 7 properties, 7 caught** — and an eighth gap was found before the pass, because the blueprint claim in the docstring had no test behind it.

### S-14.4 — Adapter conformance suite
Depends: S-14.1
AC:
- A test suite any adapter must pass
- Covers all interface methods plus reset reliability and hook overhead
- Documented so a third party can implement an adapter

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 149)** — `adapters/conformance.py`, `docs/09-adapters.md`, 17 tests in `tests/adapters/test_conformance.py`. All three AC met. **IT SHIPS IN `src/`, AS A HARNESS** — a suite living in this repository's `tests/` is one nobody outside it can run, and the AC is that a *third party* can implement an adapter. Nothing under `src/` calls `run_conformance`; a person does, which is `eval/ablation.py`'s category rather than a gap. **`SKIPPED` IS A THIRD OUTCOME AND IS NEVER MERGED INTO `PASSED`**: most checks need an input the caller supplies, so an adapter run with none of them produces a clean-looking report attesting almost nothing — `conforms` means *nothing failed*, **`attested`** means *nothing failed and nothing was skipped*, and `describe()` names the skips in words. `Selection.withheld_notice`'s rule one layer out. **THE MEASUREMENT CHECK IS SELF-CONSISTENCY, BECAUSE THAT IS WHAT IS CHECKABLE**: the adapter is the last place a measurement can be fabricated and nothing above it can tell an invented number from a measured one, so the suite checks the relationships the *caller* knows — as many samples as repeats requested, a `seconds` that **is** the median of those samples, scale and row counts returned unchanged, a warm-up recorded. A driver reporting a median it never computed is the one nothing else in the system would catch, since `Drive.seconds` is what screening fits a growth curve to. **Reset reliability is `choose_reset`'s ten cycles and overhead is per event against a stated denominator** — `OVERHEAD_BUDGET` and `REFERENCE_OPERATION_SECONDS` moved from S-1.3's test into `primitives/counters.py`, beside the `CounterOverhead` enum whose `NEGLIGIBLE` claim they make checkable, because two spellings of one budget drift and **the copy nobody runs is the one that stays generous**. **TWO CHECKS ARE WEAKER THAN THEY LOOK AND NOW SAY SO — the deliberately-broken adapters are what exposed it.** *Protected paths*: `patch_policy` concatenates onto the defaults, so an adapter using `Declarations` **cannot** narrow them and the first fake passed correctly; the check is kept for the one remaining route, a `Declarations` subclass overriding the method. *Internal frames*: the stack is synthesized from the adapter's **own** first fragment, so plausible nonsense passes — it catches an empty declaration and a broken hand-off and nothing more, because a harness that knew what a Flask stack looks like would be a harness with a framework in it. **A conformance suite is exactly the code that is never exercised in its failing direction unless somebody makes it fail on purpose.** **No check raises** — each catches broadly and reports, because a suite that stopped at the first failure tells an implementer about one problem per run. **A fake caught a real Protocol violation before any test ran**: the base fake narrowed `run_tests`'s parameter from `Session` to `CandidateSession` and mypy rejected it on contravariance — S-14.1's *half the story is checked by mypy* paying out on the first outside implementation. `docs/09-adapters.md` is linked from `00-BRIEF.md`'s authority map and from `CLAUDE.md`, and its last section tells an implementer plainly that a conforming adapter will still be refused by the campaign at fingerprinting. **Sabotage: 6 properties, 6 caught.**

### S-14.5 — MCP server extraction
Depends: S-14.3, S-14.4
Why: MCP earns its place only when an adapter exists in a different language.
AC:
- Adapter interface exposed as an MCP server
- Orchestrator connects to it over the protocol
- A reference adapter in a non-Python language demonstrates cross-language operation
- Conformance suite runs against the MCP-hosted adapter

Notes: this is genuinely last. Adding a protocol between two components you wrote yourself is overhead.

**Inherited from S-14.3 (2026-08-26), and it is not MCP work — it is the boundary this epic did not finish.** The *interface* is framework-neutral and two adapters prove it; the **grounding sequence is not**. Three places in core still know Django: `explorer/compose.py` calls `enumerate_entry_points` directly, `stages.PREDICATES` holds one entry, and `Framework.supported` is `self is Framework.DJANGO`. All three are honest today. **Do not fix `supported` alone** — a `fingerprint` that accepts Flask turns an accurate *not a supported framework* into a `KeyError` on `PREDICATES[Framework.FLASK]` one call later, which is a crash where there was a clear refusal. Replacing it properly means *an adapter exists for it*, which needs an adapter registry — and the registry **cannot live in `explorer/`**, because adapters import `explorer.fingerprint`; the decision has to move up to the campaign, the only layer allowed to know both. ADR 148 §1.

# EPIC 15 — Evaluation

### S-15.1 — Diagnostic agreement harness
Depends: S-12.1
Why: the headline reliability number, and nobody publishes it for this domain.
AC:
- Runs diagnosis N times independently on one repo with cache disabled
- Reports agreement on the primary finding as a percentage
- Reports the distribution of alternative findings
- Runs at N=10 minimum

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 153) — the harness, not the run.** `eval/agreement.py`, 15 tests in `tests/eval/test_agreement.py`. **It runs nothing**, the rule `eval/ablation.py` and `eval/learning.py` already follow: producing the ten runs it consumes costs ten investigations, and the harness is buildable now. **THREE WAYS THIS NUMBER IS QUIETLY WRONG, AND EACH IS ARITHMETIC SOMEBODY WOULD WRITE WITHOUT NOTICING.** (1) **A cached run is refused, not down-weighted** — S-5.1's cache returns the recorded answer, so ten cached runs are *one run reported ten times* and the study comes back at 100%. That is a measurement of the cache, not a weaker measurement of agreement, so the artifact cannot be constructed over one; a single cached run among ten is enough, because the study is over the set. AC 1's *with cache disabled* is structural rather than noted. (2) **A null result is an outcome, not an exclusion** — the obvious implementation counts findings, and counting findings drops the nulls, so nine runs finding nothing and one finding a cause report **100% agreement over a single run**. `None` is a key in the distribution like any other, and *primary: nothing found* at 100% renders as the good result it is. §9's rule reaching the evaluation layer, where the temptation to drop nulls is strongest because they are what makes the number look worse. (3) **A tie has no primary finding, and `None` cannot carry that** — `None` already means *nothing found was modal*, so the tie is a third state: `modal_outcomes` is the primitive and **`primary` raises** on a tie, the construction `Selection.get` uses, which makes *read the tie first* structural. The report leads with it and says **that is the result**. **The point estimate carries a Wilson interval** (8/10 is 80% with a 95% CI of roughly 49–94%), reusing `ablation.wilson` — one implementation, three callers. **`flipped` is separate from the rate**: nine-to-one is 90% *and* has flipped, and a catalogue reading only the rate would record the repository as reliable and lose the run that disagreed — which is exactly S-15.4's AC 3, and why this story came first. **The finding key is the caller's and must be measured** (a causal site, not the agent's wording), for `factory_seeder`'s reason. **Sabotage: 5 properties, 5 caught.**

### S-15.2 — Benchmark runner
Depends: S-12.1
AC:
- Runs a defined subset of SWE-Perf instances
- Compares against expert patches
- Reports per-category, not aggregate
- Subset size and selection criteria stated openly

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 156) — the harness, not the run.** `eval/benchmark.py`, 18 tests. The corpus is external (140 instances, arXiv:2507.12415) and running it costs an investigation per instance, so this takes recorded results on the same terms as the other three `eval/` modules. **AN UNCERTIFIED SPEEDUP IS A THIRD OUTCOME.** `05-research.md` §10.4 is a finding about benchmarks of exactly this kind: a 2026 audit of GSO, SWE-Perf and SWE-fficiency found runtime measurements are **not fixed quantities** — the same patch appears faster, slower or unsupported depending on where it is replayed — **despite those benchmarks already using repeated trials, outlier filtering, statistical tests and reference patches**; the implication it draws is that certification is a first-class requirement rather than a refinement. So `Result` carries a `Certification`, `Standing.UNRESOLVED` says *the instrument could not see an effect this size*, and **certification is checked before the numbers are compared at all** — an unresolvable speedup is an unread instrument, not a small speedup. **The refusal is symmetric** (an uncertified *failure* is unresolved too), which is what makes it about the instrument rather than about optimism. **Unresolved instances are excluded from the rate, not counted against it**: counting them as failures claims something about the patch when nothing was measured, and a category where nothing resolved has `matched_rate is None` rather than zero. **THERE IS NO AGGREGATE AND NO PROPERTY THAT PRODUCES ONE** — AC 3, asserted as an *absence* by inspecting the public surface, because a test reading only the rendered report would pass for a version carrying a rate nobody printed. **The categories are the dataset's**, like S-15.1's finding key: inventing a taxonomy would give a per-category report about categories nobody else uses. **`speedup=None` is not 1.0** — *nothing proposed* and *proposed and changed nothing* share a standing and stay separate fields, or a system that never answered gets credited with a null improvement. Refuses an unstated selection (AC 4: a subset whose criteria are unstated cannot be told from one chosen after the numbers were seen), a subset larger than its corpus, an instance scored twice, and an expert patch that sped nothing up. **Sabotage: 3 properties, 3 caught.**

### S-15.3 — Cost report
Depends: S-5.3
AC:
- Euros per confirmed finding, per run, per project
- Broken down by phase and model tier
- Cache hit rate and escalation rate included

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 152)** — `ProjectReport` and `Ledger.by_model` in `cost/accounting.py`, `Session.by_tier`/`unrouted_usd`/`tier_report` in `cost/session.py`, 12 tests. **MOST OF IT WAS S-5.3's AND EPIC 5's COMPOSITION CHECK's** — `RunReport.eur_per_confirmed_finding`, `Ledger.by_phase`, the cache section and the escalation section all existed. **AC 3 needed nothing**: cache hit rate is per model because caches are model-scoped, and `StepStatistics.escalation_rate` returns `None` below a minimum sample rather than calling one escalation out of one attempt a 100% rate. Two gaps, one per remaining criterion. **THE PROJECT RATIO IS TOTAL OVER TOTAL, NOT THE MEAN OF THE RUNS'** — averaging ratios weights a cheap three-finding run equally with an expensive one-finding run, and **a run that confirmed nothing has no ratio to average in**, so the arithmetic that looks like a summary is also the one that deletes the null runs. `04-cost.md` §11 is why the project is the unit at all: grounding is once per repository, so a run that found two things looks cheap because an earlier run — possibly a null one — paid to stand the repository up. `runs_confirming_nothing` is reported because **a null result is an answer and not a free one**. **EUROS ARE CONVERTED ONCE, FROM DOLLARS**: the vendor bills dollars and summing per-run euro figures adds numbers taken at several rates into a total nobody can reproduce; `render` says so when a run used a different rate rather than restating it, since a project measured over months legitimately spans several. **THE TIER CUT LIVES ON `Session` BECAUSE OF THE IMPORT GRAPH** — `routing` imports `accounting`, so `Ledger.by_tier` would be a cycle; the ledger gained `by_model` (what it knows) and the session maps those through the router that chose them. **Spend on a model no tier names is reported, not absorbed**: folding it into the cheapest band makes the table sum to the run while describing a routing that did not happen, and dropping it makes it sum to less — `Ledger.reconciles`' defect one level up — so `unrouted_usd` is its own figure and a test asserts the two sum to the run. **S-5.9's AC 6 now has a report to be published in and stays PARTIAL for the reasons already recorded** (no second vendor account; S-0.8's scenarios produce no findings). Nothing in `src/` calls `Session.report` or `ProjectReport` — the documented report/harness category, like `eval/ablation.py`. **Sabotage: 5 properties, 5 caught.**

### S-15.4 — Failure catalogue
Depends: S-15.1
Why: more credible than a success rate.
AC:
- Records repos where nothing was found
- Records caught cheats with the diff and the attack that caught them
- Records diagnoses that flipped between runs
- Records groundings that failed and why
- Published alongside results

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 154)** — `eval/catalogue.py`, 13 tests. All four sources already existed: `NullResult`, `AttackResult`, `Agreement` (S-15.1, landed immediately before) and `explorer.run.Failure`. **EVERY ENTRY HOLDS AN ARTIFACT, NOT A SENTENCE** — each was produced under its own epic's rules, so an entry is a reference to a measurement rather than a report of one; that is what makes AC 2 readable as written, since *the Adversary caught three cheats* is a claim and *here is the diff and the attack that caught it* is checkable. **PADDING IS A LIE TOO, AND IT IS THE HALF NOBODY GUARDS.** Omission is the obvious failure; the other direction is not — **an entry recording a cheat nobody caught, or a diagnosis that did not flip, is a failure that did not happen**, and a catalogue whose credibility comes from being uncomfortable is destroyed as thoroughly by inventing discomfort as by hiding it. The incentive runs the wrong way, because a *longer* failure catalogue looks more honest. So `CaughtCheat` refuses an `AttackResult` that did not land and `FlippedDiagnosis` refuses an `Agreement` that agreed. **`NOT_RUN` is refused alongside `PASSED`** and is the quieter case: an attack that could not see enough is a gap in the audit, and recording it as a catch turns a missing measurement into a success story about the Adversary. **AN EMPTY CATALOGUE IS THE LEAST CREDIBLE ARTIFACT HERE, NOT THE MOST** — empty means *nothing run* or *runs catalogued and none failed*, opposite readings the entries cannot separate, so it carries **`runs_covered`** (S-4.5's distinction one layer out) and renders as *"Nothing was recorded across N run(s), and that is not a result to be pleased about"*, naming both readings. A catalogue over zero runs is refused. A section with no entries is **absent rather than empty**, because an empty heading reads as *we looked and found none*. **THE HOLDOUT DISCIPLINE TEST CAUGHT THIS STORY** — a fixture repository whose name contained the holdout's failed the gate; the fix was to rename the fixture, **never to widen the allow-list**, and it is worth recording that it fired on a *test fixture*, where naming the holdout feels harmless. **Sabotage: 5 properties, 5 caught.**

# EPIC 16 — Reporting

### S-16.1 — Evidence chain rendering
Depends: S-8.6
AC:
- Human-readable report from an evidence chain
- Includes the growth table, exclusions with reasons, and the causal site
- Readable in under two minutes without re-deriving the reasoning

**DONE (2026-08-26) — found already built.** `EvidenceChain.render` has produced this since S-8.6: symptom, mechanism, localization, **the growth table** as S-1.5's vocabulary rather than raw numbers, **the causal site** as a path and a line range, the implicated files with their reasons, and **every exclusion with its preconditions** — the property that was already tested first-class, because *not the database, queries flat* printed with no mention of the uniform fixtures is F3's false fact with a reviewer's signature under it. **Nothing was written.** Two assertions were added for the criteria nothing had covered: AC 2's growth table and site, and AC 3 in the only form a test can hold it — every section a reader would otherwise have to go and answer for themselves is present, including the confidence's own note that it is *not a probability*. Eighth story running that checking first changed what got built.

### S-16.2 — Pull request generation
Depends: S-16.1, S-11.7
AC:
- PR body contains: before/after on every varied axis, the evidence chain, guard metrics showing what did not regress, test results, Adversary verdict, and round-one reproducing cases if any
- The falsification test is attached as a permanent regression test
- `slack-reducing` patches carry the staging warning prominently

**DONE (2026-08-26)** — `src/coldfix/report/pullrequest.py`, `deltas` and `change` moved out of `gate.py`, ADR 145, 21 tests in `tests/report/test_pull_request.py`. All three AC met. **THE GUARDS ARE READ OFF THE FALSIFICATION TEST AND NEVER PASSED IN.** AC 1 asks for *guard metrics showing what did not regress*, and the only honest source is the test that declared them — `CostClaim.guards`, which S-10.1 required non-empty because *a cost claim with no guard is a test a cheat passes by moving one number*. A body taking its own list could show a reviewer guards the test never checked; the signature has no parameter with `guard` in its name and a test asserts it by inspection. **A GUARD NOBODY MEASURED AFTER THE PATCH IS UNVERIFIED, NOT SATISFIED** — three states rather than two, and the third is the one a report gets wrong. That is the delta table's rule applied to guards: absence renders as absence, because reading a missing measurement as satisfaction puts the most flattering available answer under a reviewer's signature. **SABOTAGE FOUND THAT HALF-TESTED**: making `held` answer `True` for an unmeasured guard changed no outcome, because the tests read `unverified_guards` and the rendered text and neither goes through `held` — but `held` is what a caller branches on, so `all(item.held for ...)` would have come back green on a patch whose guards were never checked. **Ninth time a surviving sabotage was a missing test.** **THE DELTA TABLE HAS ONE OWNER NOW**, and the first draft wrote a second `change()` that rendered `-87%` — correct, and worse: a signed percentage leaves the reader to work out whether down is good, which for the guards it is not. The gate's `87% better` was already tested and already right, so it **moved rather than being rewritten**. **ROUND ONE'S REPRODUCTIONS TRAVEL WITH ROUND TWO'S PATCH** — S-11.7 returns a broken patch with something that can be run, and a reviewer who sees what the earlier attempt was caught by is better placed than one who sees only the version that passed; empty renders as nothing, because a heading with nothing under it reads as a section somebody forgot. **A PULL REQUEST IS WHAT A CLEARED PATCH GETS**: any verdict but `clean` is refused, since a body built from one would ask for a merge the audit declined to recommend with the objection printed at the top. The suite result is read off the verdict rather than supplied, and a suite that did not run says so — an absent section reads as *nothing to report*, which for a test suite is the most dangerous available reading. **AC 2's regression test is proposed as a path with the evidence it failed unpatched**, which is what makes it a regression test rather than a test. **Sabotage: 3 properties, 2 caught first time.**

### S-16.3 — Null result report
Depends: S-4.5
AC:
- Structured report naming workloads screened, thresholds applied, and why nothing was flagged
- Distinguishes "healthy" from "insufficient data to tell"

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 150)** — `Unflagged` and `WithheldReason` in `screening/null.py` and `screening/flagging.py`, 6 new tests in `test_null.py` and 3 in `test_flagging.py`. **AC 2 WAS ALREADY MET BY S-4.5** — `healthy` / `unverified` (with `touched_no_data` called out) / `unclassified`, three sentences and a test asserting the healthy one is absent from the other two. **AC 1 WAS MET IN TWO CLAUSES OF THREE**: the artifact recorded *that* nothing was flagged and *against what thresholds*, and **not what any metric actually did**. A `Flag` carries observed growth, expected growth and magnitude and explains itself; a healthy workload carried its name — so *why was this not flagged* got a claim where the flagged case gets a measurement, in a module whose own docstring calls a null result **the largest exclusion this system produces**. **THE FIRST DRAFT INFERRED THE REASON FROM THE SHAPES AND SHIPPED A LIE.** Rendered against a real screen it said `seconds superlinear, within the linear it may be` — that metric grew 9.6x *beyond* its expectation and raised no flag because it is a duration under S-0.4's noise floor, where one sample per scale point is a fit to noise. The sentence reported an unresolvable measurement as a metric that behaved, **in the direction that reassures**. So `flagging` gained `WithheldReason` (two reasons: one about the code, one about the *instrument*) and `withheld_reason()` — the negative half of `flag`'s decision, consulting the same two predicates rather than restating them — and **a test asserts the two partition every fitted metric**: exactly one of *flagged* and *withheld* is true of each, so a change to `_above_the_noise` cannot move one without the other. **THE SAME DEFECT EXISTED ONE LAYER OUT**: the closing sentence read *none of them grew beyond what its metrics may*, false on any screen where a duration was held back by the floor rather than by behaving; it now says *nothing measured qualified as a finding*, and a test asserts the old wording is gone. **A summary written before the evidence existed will describe the evidence it imagined.** `largest` is carried because *within expectations* is **two** conditions for a flat metric — fits its expectation **and** under the flat-cost threshold — so shape alone explains half of why a flat metric at 7 was left alone and none of why one at 119 was. Built only for `healthy` workloads: a growth basis for a workload nothing showed does real work reads exactly like one for a workload that is fine. **NO `report/` MODULE WAS ADDED and the note that said one belonged there was wrong** — `NullResult.report()` already existed with one owner and `Ranking.report()`/`Plan.report()` sit beside it; a second rendering is what ADR 145 warns against. **Sabotage: 5 properties, 5 caught, plus one genuinely equivalent mutant** — a filter against `ranking.unclassified` that never did anything, because every pair in that list has `growth is None` and `withheld_reason` already refuses those. Removed rather than pinned with prose.

**EPIC 16 COMPOSITION CHECK (2026-08-27)** — `tests/report/test_reporting_composed.py` (7 tests), `tests/fixtures/planted/subject.py`, ADR 151. The epic renders three things and each was tested against artifacts built by hand; **nothing had ever walked from a screen to a document**, which is where the epic's sentence lives. The walk needed a screen, so it drove `adapters.screen` **for the first time** — `test_graph.py` tests the router that *reads* the channel and `test_adapters.py` tested the helper that *wrote* it, and between them no test ran the node and read what it produced. **Three defects, and the second means a real run could not have found anything at all.** (1) *The node held its own opinion about what is worth investigating.* `_SUSPICIOUS = frozenset({Growth.SUPERLINEAR})`, and `test_flagging.py` opens with why that is wrong — **the N+1 is linear**, and the verdict is the fit against each metric's *expectation*, where a round-trip count is expected to be constant. Measured on the planted fixture: `flag()` said `db.query=GROWTH`, the node said nothing. The `FLAT_COST` class was unreachable from a run entirely. **A test had asserted the defect**, with the plausible docstring *"S-1.5's vocabulary decides, not a threshold invented at this boundary"* — the vocabulary does decide, against an expectation the helper never consulted. Judgement now comes from `assess.conclude`. (2) **THE NODE ATTACHED NO COUNTERS.** `screen(bindings)` defaults `counters=()` and the node passed none, so `db.query` was never measured: no query growth could be fitted, and **`work_verified` was False for every workload** because F6's test is defined over the query count, the payload and the duration — so a production run could only ever emit a null result **covering nothing**. The artifact had been saying so in `work_evidence` and nothing was reading it. `Resources.counters` is required now, supplied on S-7.2's convention; Epic 14 makes it the adapter's declared hooks. (3) *The ranking was computed and thrown away* — `_first_flagged` read the set back in **name order**, discarding `rank`'s growth-before-flat-cost ordering; the node records `order` from `Plan.investigate` and the name is the fallback for older checkpoints. **A SABOTAGE SURVIVED AND CHANGED THE STATE'S SHAPE**: the regression test asserted the *boolean*, and restoring the superlinear-only rule passed it, because the N+1's `seconds` sometimes fits superlinear on timing noise — the wrong rule reaching the right answer by accident. The channel carries **`flagged_metrics`** now and the test asserts the flag is `db.query`. **Still open and deliberately not stubbed:** `pull_request` has no caller, because it needs a live `PatchVerdict` and `audit_patch` puts only `verdict.describe()` into `flags`; making the verdict travel structurally is a state channel and a story of its own. **Seven epics for seven**, and this is the worst haul yet — **S-17.1 would have produced a null result for the wrong reason**, not *this repository is healthy* but *the harness measured nothing*, spending the holdout on a run that could not tell those apart. Sabotage: 4 properties, 4 caught.

**THE PATCH VERDICT CHANNEL (2026-08-27, ADR 155)** — the remainder Epic 16's composition check named, closed. `CheckpointedState.audited`, written by `audit_patch` and published by `ship`. **`pull_request` had no caller** because it takes a live `PatchVerdict` while `audit_patch` wrote only `verdict.describe()` into `flags`, and a rendered string cannot be rebuilt into the object the report takes. The channel carries the verdict **and both measurement sets**, because the before/after table needs the same numbers the audit reasoned over — measuring twice puts two sets of figures under one patch. **The ship gate now shows the body rather than `patch.describe()`**: a reviewer approving a patch needs the chain that proved the finding and the numbers showing it moved. **A DEFECT THIS WORK INTRODUCED, FOUND BY ITS OWN TEST**: the first version assembled the pull request *after* `record_outcome`, so a run missing its audit refused with a clean acceptance already in the **append-only** trust ledger — the correction would be a second entry rather than an edit, and the trust level would have moved on a patch that never shipped. The slack-reducing branch had always returned before recording; reading both paths together is what made it visible. **`earlier_rounds` stays empty and the reason is the channel** — `audited` replaces, so a second round overwrites the first; carrying them needs an append-only channel of reproductions, which is a decision about what a checkpoint holds. **TWO SABOTAGES SURVIVED THEIR FIRST ATTEMPT.** Serializing the verdict without its `results` changed no test outcome: one test built the channel by hand and the other tested the rehydrator against a hand-built dict, so **neither held both ends**, and **nothing drove the `audit_patch` node at all** — the same shape the composition check had just found at `screen`, one node along. A node nobody drives is this codebase's recurring hiding place. `PatchVerdict.results` is what a reviewer reads: a body saying *clean* without naming the attacks asks for a merge on the strength of one word. **Nothing in `src/` is designed-and-unreachable any more.** A process slip is recorded too: S-15.4's gate ran *before* its ADR and backlog note were written, both named the holdout while explaining why not to, and the commit that landed on `main` failed the holdout test — **run the gate after the documents**, they are files the gate reads. Sabotage: 4 properties, 4 caught.

# EPIC 17 — Hardening and release

### S-17.1 — End-to-end run on the holdout repo
Depends: E12, **S-17.4**, and see below
AC:
- Full pipeline runs on the repo reserved in S-0.6, never used in development
- Produces either a finding with a merged-quality PR, or an honest null result
- No manual intervention beyond approvals

**STILL BLOCKED (re-verified 2026-08-27), and the reason is not the one recorded below.** Asked to run this, the first step was to assemble a `Resources` — and **nothing in `src/` constructs one**. That is not a wiring detail: six of its twenty-three fields are the layer that connects the pipeline to a live subject, and **every one of them is a protocol with no implementation anywhere outside a test fake**:

| field | what it would do | state |
|---|---|---|
| `bind` (`Binder`) | turn a `Workload` artifact back into a runnable `BoundWorkload` | **nothing in `src/` constructs a `BoundWorkload` at all** |
| `ground` (`Grounder`) | run S-7.13's sequence bound to a repository | `Protocol`, no implementation |
| `measure` (`Measurer`) | measure a patch before and after | `Protocol`, no implementation |
| `executor` (`Executor`) | run one experiment and return its metrics | type alias, no implementation |
| `hands` (`Hands`) | run a command the Explorer proposes | type alias, no implementation |
| `probe` (`Probe`) | drive the subject for output equivalence | dataclass, never built in `src/` |

**S-17.4 is not under-delivered** — its four AC were about `Sessions`, one ledger, the gates and session reuse, and all four are met. The gap is *between* S-17.4 and this story and **no story's AC names it**, which is the shape every composition check in this project has found: each side is complete and the join has no owner.

**So the money was never the last blocker.** A run cannot be started, let alone overspend, and the estimate below (~2–10 cents on the expected null path) is sound arithmetic about a run that has no assembly. **Do not treat "there is a balance" as unblocking this.** What it needs is the subject-facing layer above, which is buildable without spending and is a story — or several — rather than a step.

**Superseded reasons, kept because they were true when written:**

**1. There is no API key and the run costs real money.** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset, and a full pipeline run makes live frontier calls at every phase — `04-cost.md` §12.1 costs the worst case at **~$291 per run** with five findings. This is not a thing to spend on somebody's behalf without being asked, and it is the only AC blocker that no amount of building removes.

**2. AC 2's PR half needs S-16.2, which is open.** `adapters.ship` says so itself: *it does not emit a pull request, and the omission is deliberate rather than pending — S-16.2 owns the PR body … a stub here would be a second, worse answer to a question two epics away.* The stated dependency `E12` is incomplete. **But the holdout makes this survivable**: `targets.toml` records that its list endpoint is *already correctly prefetched and no defect was found*, and it was chosen precisely because **the correct answer on it is "nothing found"**. So the expected path is AC 2's *null result* branch, which needs no PR — and S-16.2 becomes a blocker only if the run finds something.

**3. The wiring did not exist**, which is the part that was buildable and is now **S-17.4**. There has never been a `Sessions` implementation anywhere in `src/`, and `gates_for` has been written, tested and uncalled since S-13.6. A run could not have started.

**The holdout discipline test is the other thing to remember, and it caught this note being written.** `tests/test_holdout_discipline.py` fails if the holdout's name, URL, commit or settings module appears outside a six-entry allow-list — and the first draft of this entry named two of them, exactly as S-0.6 warned (*it is easy to reach for the holdout as a convenient second example, and nothing about doing so feels like a violation at the time*). The fix was to stop naming it rather than to widen the list, which is what its own comment asks: *adding one should be a deliberate act with a reason, not a way to make this test pass.* S-17.1 is the story that earns those entries. Nothing has been added for it yet.

### S-17.4 — The campaign entry point
Depends: E12, S-13.6
Why: `Sessions` is a protocol with no implementation and `gates_for` has no caller, so nothing can start a run — and both gaps are invisible from inside a node.
AC:
- A `Sessions` implementation under `src/` gives each agent step a budget its phase will accept
- Every session bills into one ledger, so the euro ceiling sees the whole run
- The graph compiles with the gates `standing(...)` says the project has earned
- A test proves a session is reused rather than rebuilt per call

**DONE (2026-08-25)** — `src/coldfix/orchestrator/campaign.py`, ADR 143, 19 tests in `tests/orchestrator/test_campaign.py`. All four AC met. **`Sessions` HAS BEEN A PROTOCOL WITH NO IMPLEMENTATION SINCE S-12.7**, and every caller in the tree was `lambda system: object()` in a test — a gap nothing could see from inside a node, because a node asks for a session by prompt and uses it, and any plausible factory satisfies every test in the suite. **THREE PHASES WANT THREE DIFFERENT PROGRESS CHECKS AND TWO OF THEM REFUSE TO RUN AGAINST THE WRONG ONE**: `GroundingRun` will not be constructed unless its budget stalls after **15**, `run_investigation` refuses anything but **8**, and everything else takes S-5.4's default of **3**. One budget cannot satisfy the first two at once, which is *why* the decision is per prompt — and `Session` is the only thing that builds a `Budget`, so the prompt is where it has to be made. **ONE LEDGER UNDERNEATH ALL OF THEM, OR THE CEILING IS PER-PHASE**: `Budget.spent_eur` reads its own ledger's total, so sessions with separate ledgers each see a fraction of the spend and a run could pass six ceilings on the way to breaching one. **THE FACTORY MEMOIZES AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN AN OPTIMISATION** — `adapters.investigate` asks for its session on *every* node execution, so a fresh `Session` per call is a fresh `Budget` per call, and S-5.4's per-phase caps would reset every time a node ran: **enforcement counting to one.** It would also discard the prompt cache `04-cost.md` §4 is built on. **A PROMPT NO ROLE OWNS IS REFUSED, BY ASKING THE INDEX RATHER THAN MIRRORING IT** — `owner_of` had no production caller either, and a second list of prompts here would disagree with `roles.py` the first time a step was added. **`gates_for` FINALLY HAS ITS CALLER**: `gated_graph` reads the level through `standing`, which reads the append-only ledger, and **there is no argument through which a caller could ask for fewer gates** — ADR 130's refusal, still standing now that a level is earned rather than passed. A gated graph with no checkpointer is refused in the campaign's own words, because an interrupt parks the run *in* the checkpoint and an approval given on Thursday needs somewhere to return to. **Sabotage: 2 properties, both caught** — a fresh session per call fails two tests, and one progress check for every phase fails five.


### S-17.5 — SPIKE: where does a screening measurement happen?
Depends: S-14.2
Why: **the six subject-facing implementations S-17.1 needs all inherit this answer**, and `Binder` is where the two measurement worlds meet. Writing it first and discovering the answer halfway through is how the wrong one gets locked in.
AC:
- Measure, for one real out-of-process subject: the endpoint time the subject reports, and the wall time of the whole subprocess that drove it
- Report the ratio, and what it implies for `measure_once`, which times the callable it is given
- Establish whether `extra_counters` can legally carry what the out-of-process path measures, or whether it collides with the metrics `measure_once` reserves
- Write a findings note naming which of the two worlds `Binder` must use, and what that costs the other five implementations

Notes: `explorer.work.drive` counts inside the subject's own interpreter; `bench.counting.count` and `primitives.measurement.measure_once` count in *this* process. S-14.2 recorded both as "not competitors" for counting, which is true and does not tell `Binder` which one to be. No model calls — this is arithmetic and a stopwatch.

**DONE (2026-08-27, ADR 157, `spikes/S-17.5-measurement-boundary/FINDINGS.md`).** All four AC met, and the answer is **the subject measures itself and the harness receives the numbers**. A Flask + SQLAlchemy subject with an N+1, SQLite and no container — so the numbers are a **floor**, since a Docker exec and a Postgres connection both widen the gap. The endpoint costs **9.6 ms** as the subject measures it and **1266 ms** measured from outside: **132x**, of which the endpoint is **0.75%**. **The overhead is not the finding.** Screening *fits growth* on a duration, so the same workload at three scales, fitted with the project's own `fit_growth`: queries 11/41/161 → **LINEAR**, endpoint 5.77/21.64/73.31 ms → **LINEAR**, subprocess 1282.9/1360.4/1447.1 ms → **CONSTANT**. **A workload that is linear in time fits as constant**, with 67 ms of real growth under 1257 ms of fixed startup. Reproduced: different absolute timings, identical classifications. **WORSE THAN A MISSED FINDING** — the query count survives via `extra_counters`, so *this* N+1 is still flagged on `db.query`; what breaks is everything **timing-only**, and the planted fixtures hold two such defects (a quadratic loop, a slow import) that move no query count. `CLAUDE.md` ships *queries flat across 100x scale* as a publishable result, so this screen would publish **"seconds: constant across a sixteenfold increase"** having measured the cost of starting an interpreter. **A false exclusion is an answer rather than a silence.** **THE OBVIOUS REPAIR IS NOT AVAILABLE**: handing the subject's own timing back through `extra_counters` was attempted per metric — `seconds` raises `MetricSetError` while `db.query` and `response_bytes` are accepted, so **the one metric that is wrong is the one that cannot be corrected through the existing seam**. That reservation is right and should not be relaxed (two records of one number disagree); what is needed is a way to say *this measurement came from the subject*, which is S-8.12's `Measured` precedent — widen the boundary rather than smuggle a value through a seam meant for something else. **Running coldfix inside the subject's container was not measured and is not recommended**: it puts the harness in the same process as deliberately broken ablated code, and S-2.1 mounts exactly one directory, which is what makes ADR 004 structural. **The six implementations now split with a decided order**: `Hands` and `Grounder` measure nothing and can be written immediately; `Binder`, `Executor` and `Measurer` sit on the far side of this boundary and **none should be written until the answer above is a type**. Third E0-shaped spike to change a design by measuring the naive implementation — after S-0.4 and S-0.5, and the same shape each time.

## Dependency-ordered build sequence

Condensed to a single path for someone working alone:

```
S-0.1 → S-0.3 → S-0.4 → S-0.5 → S-0.2 → S-0.6 → S-0.7
S-1.1 → S-1.2 → S-1.3 → S-1.4 → S-1.5 → S-1.6 → S-1.7
S-2.1 → S-2.2 → S-2.3 → S-2.4 → S-2.5 → S-2.8 → S-2.6 → S-2.7
S-3.1 → S-3.2 → S-3.4 → S-3.6 → S-3.9 → S-3.8
S-4.1 → S-4.2 → S-4.3 → S-4.4 → S-4.5
S-5.1 → S-5.2 → S-5.3 → S-5.4
      ← M2 reached: a useful tool with zero model calls
S-6.1 → S-6.2 → S-6.3
S-5.5 → S-5.6 → S-5.7 → S-5.8
S-7.1 → ... → S-7.10
S-8.1 → ... → S-8.6 → S-8.7
      ← M3 reached: the thesis demo
S-9.1 → ... → S-9.8
S-10.1 → S-10.2 → S-10.3 → S-10.6 → S-10.4 → S-10.5   (ADR 105: the gate precedes the first patch)
S-11.1 → ... → S-11.8
S-12.1 → ... → S-12.6
      ← M4 reached: the contribution
E13 → E14 → E15 → E16 → E17
```

**Remaining primitives** (S-3.3, S-3.5, S-3.7, S-3.10 through S-3.18) slot in wherever the Diagnostician needs them. The registry in S-3.1 is what makes that possible — each is an afternoon, not a refactor.
